# oso-code rewrite — the shared core in TypeScript on Node

Roadmap slug: `ts-core-rewrite`. Written 2026-08-25 from a measured audit (engram: `oso/harness-complexity-audit/diagnosis`, `/alternatives`, `/runtime-benchmark`; decision: `oso/harness-rewrite/decision`). This document is the input to `/oso-code:roadmap`: §1–§3 of that mode are answered here, once, so the chain runs without coming back to ask. Where it says DECIDED, the decision is frozen; an applier or verifier that finds it wrong reports `blocked` with evidence and never reinterprets it.

## 0. Purpose, non-goals, end state

**Purpose.** Replace the bash core of the harness — the 13 gate scripts, `oso-state`, and the per-host installers — with ONE TypeScript implementation on Node that the three hosts share: Claude Code and Codex spawn it, OpenCode imports it in-process. Rebuild the tests around it so the per-slice bar runs in seconds on Linux, macOS and Windows. Cut the instruction prose to what a model needs, and delete the machinery that exists only to police prose.

**What does not change.** The way of working: plan / quick / debug / roadmap flows, the apply → verify → integrate loop, the independent verifier, the state machine (`mode`, `active_slice`, `verify_green`, `auto`, `auto_wait`, `roadmap`), the gates' BEHAVIOUR (what is denied, when, with what message), the on-disk state format (`key=value`), the events log, the run journals, the engram topics, the three hosts. A user of the harness sees the same rails, faster.

**Non-goals (YAGNI, cut deliberately).** No new gates. No JSON state format. No Bun or Deno runtime. No Go binary. No new host. No change to the plan/quick/debug flow semantics beyond the defects named in §6.3. No retroactive comment sweep of files the rewrite does not delete.

**End state, measured at the close of child C6** (the numbers the verifier of that child reads):

| Measure | Today (2026-08-25) | Target |
|---|---|---|
| `tests/hooks-test.sh` | 13,414 lines, 1,799 asserts, 17m51s local, no single-case run | deleted; `npm test` ≤ 60 s local, one file runnable alone |
| PR CI wall time | 18 min ubuntu + 143 min windows, red 12/12 runs | ≤ 5 min on ubuntu AND windows, green |
| Windows runtime deps for hooks | Git Bash (permanent), jq | `node` only |
| Gate implementations | 2 (bash for Claude/Codex, TS duplicates for OpenCode) | 1 |
| Hook cold start | 37 ms (bash sourcing lib+lexer) | overhead over a bare-node control ≤ max(40 ms, 1.5 × the control's mean); absolutes reported, never asserted (measured 59 ms on the operator's machine, 156.3 ms on windows-latest) |
| Installer | 8,694 lines in 12 bash scripts + 306-line awk TOML editor + python3 | one `oso` CLI, TOML/JSON via parsers, ≤ 2,500 lines |
| Lint rules over prose wording | ~39 of 51 | 0; ~12 structural checks live in `npm test` |
| Orchestrator preamble for `/plan` (Claude) | ~20,000 words (~27k tokens) | ≤ 6,000 words (~8k tokens) |
| Files to hand-maintain per rule | 3–6 (body, 3 platform files, README, lint) | 1 (+ one host reference where a host differs) |
| Cost of adding a host | +15,201 lines (OpenCode, measured) | one adapter module + one `references/<host>.md` per skill, ≤ 1,500 lines |

## 1. Facts the design stands on (verified, with where)

- Node ≥ 22 is ALREADY a hard requirement of the bootstrap (`bootstrap/install.ps1`'s `$NodeMajorFloor`; `docs/windows.md` "Node.js 22 or newer"); Git Bash is documented there as "a permanent runtime dependency" only because every hook is a `.sh`. jq is provisioned only to edit `settings.json`.
- Claude Code hooks reference (fetched 2026-08-25, https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md): a `command` hook has an EXEC form — `"command": "node", "args": ["${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/…/gate.js", "commit"]` — that runs without any shell; on Windows the exec form needs a real executable (`node.exe` is one). Shell form uses Git Bash on Windows when installed, PowerShell otherwise. `if` takes permission-rule syntax (`"Bash(git *)"`) and is evaluated only on tool events. Exit 2 blocks on blockable events "whether or not you print JSON"; the blocking message is the JSON reason if any, stderr otherwise. Stop hooks return `shouldContinue: true` to prevent stopping ("exit 2 has the same effect"). SessionStart may return `additionalContext`. Default `command` timeout 600 s.
- Codex runs the same hook scripts with the same JSON envelope today (`codex/hooks/hooks.json`: PreToolUse, SubagentStop, Stop, UserPromptSubmit, SessionStart, SessionEnd), shell form, `__OSO_HOOKS_DIR__` substituted by the installer. Codex hook documentation is thin; what the repo has MEASURED on the pinned Codex is the contract (`docs/parity-codex.md`).
- OpenCode gates are routed by `opencode/hooks/routes.ts` from `tool.execute.before`, `experimental.chat.system.transform`, `dispose`, `event`; the shim spawns `bash <gate>.sh` (`opencode/plugin/oso/gates.ts:169`) and `oso-state` (`plan-state.ts:68`). `unattended-run.ts` and `approval.ts` REIMPLEMENT in TS what `auto-continue.sh`, `capture-plan-approval.sh` and `approve-plan-token.sh` do in bash. OpenCode plugins are TS by the host's own contract.
- Agent Skills (`SKILL.md`) is an open standard (agentskills.io) read by Claude Code, Codex CLI (`~/.codex/skills`, `.agents/skills`) and OpenCode. Anthropic's skill guidance: SKILL.md body < 500 lines, references one level deep, SKILL.md is an overview and not only pointers, one default with an escape hatch.
- Benchmarks on the operator's machine (Linux, node 26): gate-shaped cold start bash 37 ms / node bundle 59 ms / node running `.ts` directly 89 ms; 100 `node:test` cases in 0.18 s; `cd opencode && node --test` runs 152 cases in 5 s.
- Four verified, unfixed defects in the unattended rails (engram `oso/auto-wait-stall-field-failure/diagnosis`): (1) `auto_wait` is never returned to `none` because the close commands in a host-neutral body cannot carry a Claude-only key; (2) the 45-minute expiry is evaluated only inside a `Stop`, so a held stop never expires; (3) the wait mark is keyed on `auto_change` and re-dated at every child boundary; (4) auto-compaction has never executed in a real run.
- `bodies/plan.md:19` instructs the orchestrator to write `$OSO_STATE_DIR/deploy-deny/<digest>.patterns`; `OSO_STATE_DIR` exists only inside `plugin/hooks/lib.sh:260` (not exported) and `oso-state` has no verb for it.
- The tree that GOVERNS the rewrite is the installed release (`~/.claude/plugins/cache/oso-code/oso-code/0.25.0`), never the working tree being rewritten. Nothing in this roadmap edits the installed copy until C6.

## 2. Global ledger (applies to every child unless a child says otherwise)

**G1 — Language and runtime. DECIDED.** TypeScript, strict, compiled and bundled to plain JavaScript (ESM) that runs on Node ≥ 22. Hooks and CLIs are never run from `.ts` at runtime. Code is written against Node's standard APIs (`node:fs`, `node:child_process`, `node:crypto`, `node:path`, `node:test`) and nothing Bun- or Deno-specific.

**G2 — Repository layout. DECIDED.** One npm workspace at the root:

```
package.json                 workspaces: core, opencode; scripts: build, test, typecheck, check
core/                        the shared implementation
  package.json               name @oso-code/core, private
  src/state/                 schema.ts, store.ts, transitions.ts, cli.ts   (oso-state)
  src/gates/                 one file per gate: commit.ts edits.ts unknown.ts proddeploy.ts
                             autocontinue.ts planstop.ts planprompt.ts handoff.ts
                             stale.ts version.ts teardown.ts reanchor.ts statebin.ts
  src/shell/                 lexer.ts (port of plugin/hooks/lexer.sh, same records)
  src/hosts/                 envelope.ts (hook input/output types), claude.ts, codex.ts
  src/routes/                routes.ts — the gate table (replaces tools/hook-gates.txt) and
                             render.ts — hooks.json for Claude and Codex from that table
  src/install/               oso.ts (CLI), claude.ts codex.ts opencode.ts, toml.ts json.ts,
                             backup.ts verify.ts repair.ts purge.ts, trust.ts (hashes)
  src/bin/                   gate.ts, oso-state.ts, oso.ts, precommit.ts (entry points)
  test/                      mirrors src; test/fixtures/ holds the parity fixtures (§7)
plugin/                      the Claude Code plugin root (unchanged location)
  dist/                      COMMITTED bundles: gate.js, oso-state.js, precommit.js
  hooks/hooks.json           rendered, exec form, node
  skills/ agents/ output-styles/   (reshaped in C5)
codex/                       manifests + rendered agents/skills; hooks copy plugin/dist
opencode/
  plugin/oso-code.ts         the OpenCode plugin: an adapter importing @oso-code/core
  dist/oso-code.js           COMMITTED bundle with the core inlined
bootstrap/
  install.sh install.ps1     thin: check node ≥ 22, exec `node bootstrap/oso.js …`
  oso.js                     COMMITTED bundle of core/src/bin/oso.ts
  hook-hashes.txt            generated from the committed bundles
.github/workflows/pr.yml     the PR gate (≤ 5 min)
.github/workflows/nightly.yml   host certification and real installs
```

Bundles are committed because a Claude Code marketplace install is a git checkout with no build step, and `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` must point at runnable files. `npm run check` fails when a committed bundle differs from a fresh build (the same discipline `tools/render-hooks-json.sh --check` enforces today).

**G3 — Toolchain, pinned by the first slice of C1 at the versions `npm view` returns then. DECIDED.** `typescript` (7.0.2 today; fall back to the latest 5.x only if the 7.x compiler rejects the tree — recorded as delegated), `esbuild` (0.28.x), `smol-toml` (1.8.x) as the only runtime dependency of the installer, `@types/node`. Test runner `node:test` with `node --test` — zero test dependencies. No lint framework in the PR gate beyond `tsc --noEmit` and the structural checks in `core/test/structure/`.

**G4 — The bar, as numbers, enforced in CI from C1 on. DECIDED.** `npm test` ≤ 60 s on the PR runner; PR workflow ≤ 5 min wall on ubuntu and windows; one gate cold start measured on both matrix platforms by a test that spawns the bundle and a bare-node control 20 times interleaved and asserts the OVERHEAD ≤ max(40 ms, 1.5 × the control's mean), the absolute mean being reported and never asserted — the ≤ 100 ms figure is retired as a bar and kept as a local benchmark (amended 2026-08-29, DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Decision 14, C2-S6; see C2-D26); `core/` source ≤ 6,000 lines and `core/test/` ≤ 8,000 lines at C6 (guards against re-growing the monolith); no test in the PR gate may read the developer's real `$HOME`, network, or an installed host binary — such a test belongs to nightly.

- **G4 amended 2026-08-30 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable 9eab15, model Claude Fable 5, C3-S1).** The two totals above become `core/src` ≤ **8,000** lines and `core/test` ≤ **12,000** lines; C3-S1 also opens a bound the original pair never carried, `core/test/install` ≤ **3,000**, standing beside C3's own frozen exit bar `core/src/install` ≤ 2,500 (§C3 "Exit"). **Derivation, measured rather than guessed:** at C3-S1's start, before `core/src/install` existed, `core/src` held 5,174 tracked lines and `core/test` held 5,760 (counted the way the rule now reads, below); C3's exit bar already commits `core/src/install` to ≤ 2,500, and this slice opens `core/test/install` at ≤ 3,000 — so 5,174 + 2,500 = 7,674 and 5,760 + 3,000 = 8,760, both already past the frozen 6,000/8,000 pair before a single S2–S6 slice lands. That is the contradiction this amendment closes, not a re-litigation of either number. The new totals are round figures with headroom above each measured floor — 8,000 over 7,674, 12,000 over 8,760 — for C3's remaining EXPAND slices (S2 Claude, S3 Codex, S4 OpenCode), S5b's lexer differentials, and C4/C5's later growth, rather than a bound tuned to today's tree. **Counting method, now part of the rule:** `wc -l` semantics (a newline count, not a visual line count) over TRACKED `*.ts` files under each directory, walked the way `core/test/support/tracked-files.ts` already does (`git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard`) and filtered to the `.ts` extension; `*.json` fixtures are data and are never counted. **Enforced for the first time by `core/test/structure/line-budget.test.ts`**, which asserts all four ceilings and floors each tree's scanned-file count so an empty or broken walk cannot pass by counting nothing — G4's claim to be "enforced in CI from C1 on" was false until this test existed for the line caps; it is true of them from C3-S1.

- **G4 amended again 2026-08-30 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable 9eab15, model Claude Fable 5, C3-S2b).** Decision 26 orders three changes, ahead of C3-S3 so the Codex port does not open against a ceiling already contradicted the way C3-S1 found the first pair contradicted.

  **The four ceilings become** `core/src/install` ≤ **9,000**, `core/test/install` ≤ **8,000**, `core/src` ≤ **14,500**, `core/test` ≤ **17,500** — superseding both the 2,500/3,000 pair this bullet's predecessor left standing beside C3's exit line and the 8,000/12,000 pair it set for the two totals. **Derivations, each reproduced by `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard | grep <prefix> | xargs wc -l` against the tree at this slice's start, before `engram.ts`/`pins.ts` landed:** install 1,678 + 6,851 = 8,529; test-install 1,639 + 0.9 × 6,851 = 7,805; src 7,138 + 6,851 + 200 (C4-D2's pins, C5's render stub) = 14,189; test 8,432 + 0.9 × 6,851 + 1,500 (C4 certify) + 1,000 (C5 structure) = 17,098. Each rounded UP to the next 500, per the method below: 8,529 → 9,000; 7,805 → 8,000; 14,189 → 14,500; 17,098 → 17,500.

  - **The `+1,500 (C4 certify)` term of that derivation is SHORT, recorded 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable d2c142, model Claude Fable 5, C4/pre-S1a, Decision 41 part 3) and NOT amended.** The certify lane's real corpus, measured by function-body span at C4's arming rather than by file length: the contract bar holds 44 functions and 583 body lines of which 58 are `contract_bar_demo_agent_mode_mutation`, reachable only through a flag no consumer passes and therefore not ported, leaving 525 live; the behaviour bar holds 42 functions and 533; the two smokes are 438. **1,496 live bash lines**, projecting at C3's own measured Codex-verify port ratio of 1.57 to roughly **2,350 TypeScript lines** against the 1,500 reserved — short by about 850. **The CEILING still holds and no number moves here:** `core/test` measured 13,343 of 17,500 after C4-S0, so the projection lands near 15,700, and near 16,700 once C5's reserved 1,000 is spent. This is written beside the derivation rather than into it because a projection written into an instrument is the kind of number that rots — the derivation string inside the line-budget structure test is re-derived ONCE at C4's close, with the MEASURED figure and never with a projection.
  - **G4 AMENDED AT C4's CLOSE 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable d2c142, model Claude Fable 5, Decision 43): `core/test` ≤ 18,500.** This is the bullet above executing its own last sentence — re-derived ONCE at the close, by the frozen method, with the MEASURED figure. **Derivation, verbatim:** the `+ 1,500 (C4 certify)` term is replaced by the measured certify corpus of **2,888** lines, so `8,432 + 0.9 × 6,851 + 2,888 + 1,000 = 18,486`, rounded up to the next 500 → **18,500**. The other three ceilings are untouched. **Necessary rather than loosened, and the number that settles it:** `core/test` measures **16,558** at this close, and this document already promises C5 a reserve of 1,000 — `16,558 + 1,000 = 17,558`, past the frozen 17,500. **The frozen ceiling could not host a reserve the document itself had promised.** The projection was short by 1,388 rather than the ~850 the bullet above estimated, which is the whole reason a projection is never written into an instrument.
  - **The close figure in the bullet above is STALE by 169 and is corrected here rather than rewritten, 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 44 Q2), in C5's opening record commit.** The sentence above reads `core/test` measures **16,558** at C4's close. **At `1c85baa`, the commit that IS that close, G4's own instrument reports 16,727 across 131 tracked `*.ts` files** — `core/test/structure/line-budget.test.ts` run on the tree, never derived. The 16,558 was true at `106e0e8` across 129 files; `ebb6495` added 165 lines and `1c85baa` four more, so Decision 43 was written before the close's own last two commits landed. **Decision 43's conclusion is UNTOUCHED and is not reopened:** its argument was that the frozen 17,500 could not host the reserve this document promises C5, and 16,727 + 1,000 = 17,727 exceeds 17,500 exactly as 17,558 did — the measured figure strengthens that argument rather than weakening it. **No ceiling moves, and the instrument was never stale:** `16,558` occurs exactly once in this repository, in the bullet above, and the derivation string inside the line-budget test carries no close figure at all. **The two spare-headroom figures in circulation are ONE quantity on two bases**, which is why they disagree and why neither is a second instrument: the spare left after C5 spends its 1,000-line reserve is 18,500 − (16,558 + 1,000) = **942** on the stale base and 18,500 − (16,727 + 1,000) = **773** on the measured one, differing by exactly the 169; the companion phrase "short by 58" (1,000 − 942) reads 227 on the measured base. **The staleness moved the margin and never the verdict.** **C5's projected cost is recorded here as a PROJECTION and never enters the instrument**, per G4's own standing text: the eleven surviving structural checks port from `tests/plugin-lint.sh`'s 610 lines less the section-inventory function and its four helpers, plus C5-D4's preamble test, C5-D2's spelling and reference checks, and C5-D5's capability instrument at about 200 — between roughly 840 lines at C3's measured 0.9 test-kind ratio and 1,390 at C4's measured 1.93 certify ratio, the worst case leaving `core/test` at 18,117 with 383 under the ceiling. **The `1,000 (C5 structure)` term stays in the instrument as written and is re-derived ONCE at C5's close with the MEASURED figure**, the same standing rule executing that C4's close executed for the certify term.

  **Remaining surface = 6,851 bash lines**, re-measured rather than carried forward: Codex 3,432 (`install-codex.sh` 1,039 + `verify-codex.sh` 1,021 + `purge-codex.sh` 457 + `repair-engram-codex.sh` 485 + `restore-codex.sh` 124 + `bootstrap/lib/toml-regions.awk` 306, the Codex-only TOML parser) + OpenCode 2,470 (`install-opencode.sh` 860 + `verify-opencode.sh` 654 + `purge-opencode.sh` 617 + `repair-opencode.sh` 215 + `restore-opencode.sh` 124) + `bootstrap/lib` 720 (every `.sh` file under `bootstrap/lib/` a host sources, excluding `install-backup.sh` — 104 lines, already ported as `backup.ts` — and excluding `toml-regions.awk`, counted under Codex above since no other host reads it) + Claude's engram block 229 (`bootstrap/install.sh`, `provision_engram_binary` through `engram_manual_install_command`, this slice's own source). All four re-derived and all four reproduced exactly against the tree at this slice's start.

  **The METHOD becomes G4's standing text**, replacing the informal "counting method" paragraph above with a rule for every ceiling from here on: a ceiling is derived from the measured port ratio of the latest closed host applied to the remaining surface measured by `wc -l` over the files the child's Intent names, plus the on-disk count and later children's stated allocations, rounded up to the next 500; it moves only by an amendment that shows its arithmetic, never by a slice; **a ceiling is not a target**. Each host's close REPORTS its measured ratio **per kind** — install/repair/purge versus verify — so C6's record states what the rewrite cost in lines, rather than a single blended number that hides which kind actually grew. Templates and tables shipped as data files under `core/src/install` (the Codex managed-config TOML, the permission table, check lists) are **uncounted**, and the slice report that ships them LISTS them by name, so uncounted data reads as declared rather than hidden code.

  **The purpose clause and the size claim.** G4's "guards against re-growing the monolith" becomes what the numbers can actually guard: **growth past the measured port ratio**. And the size claim is retired in writing: at a measured 1:1 the TypeScript is NOT smaller than the bash it replaces — ≈14,500 + 17,500 (the C6 ceilings above) against 24,315 measured `wc -l` across every tracked `*.sh` file in this repository (`bootstrap/`, `plugin/hooks/`, `tests/`, `tools/`; `find . -name '*.sh' -not -path '*/node_modules/*' -not -path '*/.git/*' | xargs wc -l`) plus 539 of `.ps1`/`.bat` (`install.ps1` 479 + `verify.bat` 60; `install.bat`'s 16 lines are a trivial `powershell`-launching shim carrying no logic of its own and are not counted) — **flagged, not silently corrected: this slice's own count reads 24,315, not the ≈25,800 named for this same sentence in the child's brief, and the difference (1,485 lines, ≈6%) does not reproduce against any corpus this slice could construct from the stated directories; the 539 ps1/bat figure DOES reproduce exactly.** Recorded here as measured rather than reconciled, since the point the sentence makes — the TypeScript is not smaller — holds at either number. **What the rewrite buys is that it is typed, tested in-process, and shared across three hosts** — the audit's actual reason, per the 2026-08 complexity audit this whole rewrite answers. **Record why this is being said:** a record that keeps promising "smaller" would be the third D12-shaped defect in this child — a claim the numbers do not support, standing unexamined until a slice measures it and finds it false.

  **The measured ratios, for the record, per kind** — Claude's own two closed kinds, C3-S1's and this slice's own source and its bash original: install/repair/purge `core/src/install/claude.ts` 751 ÷ `bootstrap/install.sh` 938 = **0.80**; VERIFY `core/src/install/verify-claude.ts` 517 ÷ `bootstrap/verify.sh` 329 = **1.57** — the bash spells a check in one line where the port exports a function per check, which is why `verify-codex.sh` at 1,021 lines is not a shape the install ratio predicts. The blended (751+517) ÷ (938+329) ≈ **1.00** hides that split, and hiding it is exactly what "a claim per kind" now forbids.
- **G4 amended 2026-08-30 #3 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Decision 27, C3-S2c).** The Verification row gains standing text: **a platform fact the bash inherited from POSIX is never a contract of the port on win32.** The `windows-latest` leg of `pr.yml` had been red since `c4ccc2a` — S1's own commit — across S1, S5b, S2 and S2b, while six verifications ran on Linux, returned green bars and were silent about the other platform. **That six is ledger history, not a measurement of this tree** — it counts verifier verdicts recorded across those four slices, and no in-tree instrument produces or can check it; every other figure in this amendment names the command that produced it. This amendment is what makes G17's instrument read rather than asserted, and it names two classes rather than the four instances that found them.

  **Class 1 — file attributes libuv SYNTHESISES on win32.** Mode bits beyond the read-only attribute (already ruled for `isPrivateRegularFile` by C2-D9), `blocks` and `blksize`, and — named NOW rather than after a fourth instance — `uid`, `gid`, `nlink` and `ino`. **Class 2 — spawn semantics.** The POSIX kernel executes a `#!` script directly and falls back to `/bin/sh` on ENOEXEC; `CreateProcessW` starts PE images alone, resolves a bare command name through `PATHEXT` alone, and since CVE-2024-27980 Node refuses a `.cmd` or `.bat` without `shell: true`.

  **The three rules.** (i) Product code that reads such a fact **BRANCHES ONCE**, in the one function that reads it, with a stated win32 behaviour — C2-D9's shape exactly. **Amended by Decision 28 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, C3-S2c fix round):** that one function takes the platform as a PARAMETER instead of reading `process.platform`, so its win32 branch is provable from any host — `core/src/install/verify-claude.ts` and `core/src/install/claude.ts` now hold zero occurrences of that read, `core/src/install/cli.ts` is the single edge that performs it, and six sibling functions already took the platform explicitly, which this makes unanimous. `core/src/state/store.ts`'s `isPrivateRegularFile` is the one named exception and keeps its internal read: a state-library primitive with no input to carry a platform, whose win32 branch is a degradation rather than a set that can be wrong. (ii) A test that asserts against a POSIX oracle — `du`, `chmod`, a `#!` fixture — is skipped on win32 through a **named guard in `core/test/support/win32-skip-guards.ts`**, never an inline platform branch; `core/test/structure/platform-branches-live-in-support.test.ts` enforces that no tracked `*.ts` under `core/test/` compares `process.platform` outside the guard module itself and two named, justified exemptions — `win32-skip-guards.test.ts`, the module's own counter-proof, which must read the running platform to assert every guard is off on POSIX and names its divergence on win32; and `core/test/support/gate-fixture.ts:67`, which selects the home variable a gate reports missing (`USERPROFILE` on win32, `HOME` elsewhere), an oracle both legs must match rather than a skip either leg takes. A second case fails any exemption whose file no longer holds the branch it was written for, so the allowlist cannot outlive its reason. Shown red on the three inline guards that existed at this slice's start and green once they moved; shown red again in the fix round on a guard smuggled into `core/test/support/state-sandbox.ts`, which the blanket `support/` exemption this narrowing replaced had let through. **The gate reads a COMPARISON, not every mention**: `platform: process.platform` handed to a function that takes the platform as an explicit input is the shape `verify-claude.test.ts` already pins by name, and four such sites OUTSIDE that file (`engram-nightly.test.ts:18,23`, `core/test/install/verify-claude-differential.test.ts` line 54 at `2bc77ad`, a file C3-S6a has since deleted, `state-root-provenance.test.ts:49`) are correct as they stand — banning them would buy an indirection with no readability win. **The four is that scope and no other**: `verify-claude.test.ts` itself holds five more of the same shape (`verify-claude.test.ts:427,465,472,482,493`), which is why it is the file named as pinning it, so a reader counting every argument site under `core/test/` finds nine rather than four and neither number is wrong. **And a site correct for THIS gate is not thereby free of every class.** `engram-nightly.test.ts:18` builds its expected binary name with `engramBinaryName(process.platform)` while the actual routes through `provisionEngramBinary({platform: process.platform})` to `engramBinaryName(input.platform)` (`core/src/install/engram.ts:42`) — the same function on the same read, so a wrong win32 name would agree on both sides and the case would pass green. `state-root-provenance.test.ts:49` carries the same shape DEFUSED: its expected side is a fixture constant rather than a second call of the function under test. **The fix round's own sweep reported ONE adjacent site of this class and there are TWO**, only one of them defused; both are left as they stand, outside this slice's change surface, and `engram-nightly.test.ts:18`'s sibling assertion (`isExecutableRegularFile`) is independent and load-bearing. (iii) **Every guard keeps a floor** so the Linux leg still proves the oracle and the win32 leg never passes by skipping everything: `core/test/support/win32-skip-guards.test.ts` asserts every guard the module exports is OFF on POSIX and names its divergence on win32, and each guarded oracle keeps a platform-independent proof beside it.

  **What was measured, and what each root cause explains — fifty names, every one accounted for.** The `windows-latest` baseline at this slice's own head `1ccb71d` carries **50 red names**, taken from the run log by every `not ok` line and split by the `type:` each one's own YAML block reports: **33 leaves and 17 `describe` names**. Per file — `core/test/install/claude.test.ts` 30 (20 leaves, 10 describes); `core/test/install/engram.test.ts` 10 (7, 3); `core/test/install/verify-claude.test.ts` 8 (5, 3); `core/test/install/backup.test.ts` 2 (1, 1). **The root causes sum to 50.** **46 from class 2**: all 30 in `claude.test.ts`, where the fixture farm at `claude.test.ts:37-42` publishes `git` as an extensionless symlink and `claude` as a `#!` node stub, neither of which `PATHEXT` resolves, so `scratchRepositoryRoot()`'s own `git init` throws before any assertion runs; all 10 in `engram.test.ts`, whose every placement case runs a `#!` release fixture through the run probe; and 6 of the 8 in `verify-claude.test.ts` — `checkEngramBinaryResolves` with its win32 leaf, and the `engramBinaryRuns` describe with three of its four leaves, all on `#!` engram fixtures. **2 from class 1**: `backupSizeKib` and its `agrees with du -sk, byte for byte` leaf, the port sizing `core/src/install` at **116 KiB** where Git Bash's `du -sk` read **117** — a **1 KiB** directory-entry delta, libuv reporting a directory's `st_blocks` from an NTFS `AllocationSize` that is not what MSYS counts. **2 from a PRODUCT defect**, not a test one: `checkOsoStateBinRoundTrips` and its e2e leaf. **The 33 this paragraph used to name are superseded rather than corrected**: they were measured at `077d847`, before S2b's tests landed, and its "plus S2b's own additions" counted and root-caused none of the seventeen more. The **445 KiB / 447 KiB** it reported for the same delta reproduce nowhere — stripped of timestamps, `445` and `447` occur in that log exactly five times, four TAP ordinals and one line number, and no measurement.

  **The product defect and its ruled fix.** `runOsoStateProbe` spawned `plugin/bin/oso-state` — whose first line is `#!/usr/bin/env node` — directly, which Git Bash ran for `verify.sh` and win32 cannot, so the port reported `OSO_STATE_BIN round-trips oso-state (e2e)` FAILED on a correct install. It now spawns `process.execPath <script>`, the way the hook command lines already do: **no platform branch**, fewer moving parts than a win32 special case, and the existing fixture at `verify-claude.test.ts:240` proves it on the PR leg itself. `kernelExecutesDirectly` is the one function that branches on the class-2 fact, and under Decision 28 it takes the platform as a parameter rather than reading it: the win32 magic set is PE (`MZ`) alone, and `MZ` LEFT the POSIX set in the same fix round — a PE runs on Linux only where `binfmt_misc` is registered for it, which is host configuration and no kernel contract, while the published Linux asset is ELF, so refusing one there is fail-closed and correct.

  **Dispositions per family, and what is skipped rather than proved.** The script fixtures declare POSIX-only under `skipUnlessKernelRunsScriptFixtures` rather than becoming a committed PE: no program the `windows-latest` image ships is known to exit 0 given the single argument `version` AND to run from a copy in a temp directory with no sibling DLLs, and committing a hand-built binary as a fixture would put an opaque blob into a tree whose whole gate is source-readable. **The guard is now named for both mechanisms it covers rather than one of them.** It was `skipUnlessKernelRunsShebangs` while its own reason text named `#!` handling AND the `/bin/sh` fallback an ENOEXEC exec takes, and at the three `MZ` fixture sites under it (`engram.test.ts:151,169`, `verify-claude.test.ts:400`) it is the fallback clause that applies, measured rather than reasoned: a file opening `MZ\nexit 0\n` spawns to status 0 on this Linux host with `line 1: MZ: command not found` on stderr, which is `/bin/sh` running it after ENOEXEC, not a kernel that started it. **The compensating instrument is CONFIGURED AND HAS NEVER RUN — recorded as configuration, never as evidence.** `.github/workflows/nightly.yml:152-157` runs `core/test/install/engram-nightly.test.ts` on `runs-on: windows-latest` under workflow-level `OSO_NIGHTLY: 1` against the real pinned engram release, carrying no platform guard of its own. That step was added by this child at `1ccb71d` and is on no merged branch: `git show origin/main:.github/workflows/nightly.yml | grep -c engram-nightly` returns **0** against a file that IS on `origin/main`, so the zero is a read and not an error, and the latest nightly — run `33308513292`, `event: schedule`, `headBranch: main`, `headSha b1f078a` — ran the older workflow, which does not contain the step at all. **The future is worse than the past here.** In that same run `nightly-windows` failed at step 7, `Hook regression suite`, and steps 8 through 13 were every one `skipped`; the engram step sits immediately after that suite and carries **no `if:` and no `continue-on-error`**, so once this branch reaches `main` it inherits exactly that fate and skips every night until the bash hook suite is green on Windows, which is **C3-S6**, the last slice of this child. **What is therefore UNPROVED on win32 in the meantime: the final rename-and-report step of the placement path** — the one case still skipped, whose skip reason now says that in its own words instead of citing a proof, where a reader meets it rather than here. What DOES still run on that leg is the paragraph below, re-measured for this round rather than inherited. **Routed to C3-S5, this child's declared nightly-and-wrappers slice**: making that step run independently of the hook suite's state belongs where nightly is the surface under change and the whole job's shape can be reasoned about at once. It is deliberately NOT done here — `.github/workflows/nightly.yml` is outside this slice's declared file set, and a workflow edit can be neither driven nor seen failing from this machine, which would be this very defect wearing a different coat. The `du -sk` oracle declares POSIX-only under `skipUnlessDiskBlocksAreAllocated`, and **the win32 leg proves the CONSUMER instead**: `installBackupsOverBudget` prunes the same set at budgets set `WIN32_DIRECTORY_ENTRY_MARGIN_KIB` = 2 KiB clear of the boundary — a **margin above** the 1 KiB delta the runner measured, never the delta itself, which is the whole reason it is safe — with a floor asserting the sizer reports more than that margin for a real tree rather than the zeros an all-synthesised platform would give.

  **G4 gains a second piece of standing text: a suite total published in this record is measured AFTER the prose lands, never before.** The record's own citations are themselves tests. `core/test/structure/citations-resolve.test.ts` walks every tracked source under `core/`, `docs/rewrite/`, `tests/`, `bootstrap/` and `CHANGELOG.md`, matches the full `dir/.../file.ext:line` form, and emits **one test per citation it finds** — so a paragraph added to this document raises `npm test`'s total by exactly the number of full-path citations that paragraph carries, and every total below is a function of the prose above it. Measured two ways that agree, that file alone: **186** cases on the tree as this round leaves it, against **179** with only `git show 1ccb71d:docs/rewrite/ts-core-roadmap.md` substituted for the prose — a delta of **7**, exactly the seven full-path citations this slice has added to that document (70 → 77 under the same walk). The pair moved under this round's own hands and is recorded moving: **181 against 178** before its prose landed, then four further roadmap citations and one more inside a corrected skip reason. Anyone quoting a total from a run that predates their own edit is quoting a number their edit has already changed.
  **G4's suite-total clause gains its sharper form, 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 55 Q3): a suite total or a CI prediction published in this record is measured on the COMMITTED tree — after the commit and before the push — never on a dirty one, because an uncommitted tree carries cases that exist only because it is uncommitted.** **What earned it:** the orchestrator's prediction for C5-S1b-1 got the colour right and the constant right — ubuntu 3,082 minus windows 2,984 is exactly 98 — and missed BOTH absolute counts by eleven, having predicted 3,093 and 2,995. Diagnosed by causing it rather than reasoning about it: re-running `npm test` on the clean committed tree reports 3,082, matching the ubuntu leg to the case, so the machine still matches that leg exactly and what failed was the tree the measurement was taken on — eleven overlay deletions unstaged and ten files untracked, producing eleven cases the committed tree does not. **The constant's holding at 98 is what proves the mechanism was dirtiness rather than the port**, and the prediction is recorded as two parts right and one wrong rather than as an exact call quoting only the two.

  **G4 gains a third piece of standing text (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Decision 29, C3-S2c record correction).** A pinned `process.platform` measures WHICH cases skip, never HOW MANY — a skipped `describe` never registers its leaves; case-count deltas are measured on the real leg with `gh run view` and never derived from the pin. A zero read through an instrument that itself errored is not a measurement — the instrument's own exit is checked before its zero is believed. **The instance behind the second half:** this same record once carried a claim measured with `git show main:<path> | grep -c`, read a `0` as evidence, and never noticed that `git show` had fatalled against a stale local `main` (`9b6bf5b`, against trunk `origin/main` at `b1f078a`) while `grep -c` counted the empty stream that fatal exit left behind. Both halves are the same defect wearing two shapes: a number read from an instrument nobody checked before believing its zero.

  **G4 gains a FOURTH piece of standing text, sibling to the third rather than a restatement of it (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Decision 35, C3-S4 fix round 2).** *An instrument's exit is the exit of the process that produced it; a zero from the wrong process, the wrong ref, the wrong search mode or the wrong side of a pipe is not an answer to the question asked, whatever its value.* The third clause says check the exit before believing the zero; this one says the exit and the search must belong to the process and the question actually asked — and the difference is not academic, since three of the four instances below clear the third clause and fail anyway. **Four instances in one week, three of them C3-S4's own.** (1) `probe_opencode_version`'s `|| return 0` was read at the PRODUCER while the behaviour under question lived at the CONSUMER, `check_baseline` refusing on the empty string — the wrong process. (2) The stale-`main` case the third clause already records: `git show main:<path>` fatalled against a local ref that was not trunk and `grep -c` counted the empty stream that fatal left behind — the wrong ref. (3) `git log -S`, the PICKAXE, which searches file CONTENT, was asked a question about PATHS; its zero was true of the question it answered and silent about the one asked — the wrong search mode. (4) A verifier read a WRAPPER's exit as `tests/hooks-test.sh`'s own and reported the bar exiting 0 while printing `failed: 1` — the wrong side of a pipe. **Re-driven for this round with the exit captured off the script itself rather than through a pipe or a wrapper: EXIT 1, `passed: 1742, failed: 1, skipped: 6`**, which is exactly what `tests/hooks-test.sh`'s line 10891 at `97743eb` — `[ "$fail" -eq 0 ]` — requires, so the bar reads correctly and always has. **A fifth, caught inside this round by the clause itself and recorded rather than tidied away:** a PATH was called `opencode`-free on the strength of `command -v opencode`, which names the FIRST match and answers nothing about the rest; THREE directories on that PATH held one, and the neutered-filter measurement in C3-S4 fix round 2 (7) only became a measurement once all three were stripped and the absence re-checked.

  **Cost, measured rather than enumerated by hand.** **Amended by Decision 29 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, C3-S2c record correction):** both legs of CI run `33332837611`, read from the `Core unit tests` step of each job rather than derived from a pin — `test (ubuntu-latest)` — success — `tests 2427, pass 2425, fail 0, skipped 2`; `test (windows-latest)` — success — `tests 2314, pass 2294, fail 0, skipped 20`. The Linux leg runs **2,425** of the **2,427** cases `node --test` registers (its two skips are nightly's, not win32's) and the win32 leg runs **2,294**, so **131 cases** do not run there. The gap reconciles two ways, both read from those same two totals rather than a second whole-suite run: **2,427 − 2,314 = 113** leaves that never register — a skipped `describe` never registers its children — **+ 20** announced skips **− 2** that skip on Linux too (the same nightly-gated pair the sentence above already names) **= 131**; equivalently, **20** announced **+ 111** net-unregistered **= 131**. **This replaces the earlier arithmetic rather than standing beside it**: the whole suite run twice on ONE host — once as itself, once with `process.platform` pinned to `win32` — subtracting each run's `tests − skipped`, read the win32 leg at 2,381 and reported **44 cases** not running there. **That paragraph's own caveat about the pin was right, and its conclusion was wrong anyway — said here plainly rather than left implicit in a number swap**: a naive `process.platform` pin reproduces the leg's skips but not its host, leaving a hybrid in which `kernelExecutesDirectly` reads this machine's ELF `node` against the PE magic set, three cases fail for the pin rather than for the platform, and one structure file never finishes registering. The caveat named that hybrid correctly and then the sentence beside it still carried the pin's difference forward onto the real Linux total, instead of measuring the win32 leg the way the run above now does. Both totals were re-measured after this round's prose landed, which is the standing text above applied to its own paragraph. The registered count moved **2,422 → 2,427** because this round added five full-path citations — four in this document and one inside a corrected skip reason — and `citations-resolve.test.ts` reports `tests 186, pass 186, skipped 0` on BOTH legs, so all five run on either and the **131** is untouched. Per file, the **44** named win32-guard sites this document already carried, kept here as the map from case name to file rather than recomputed: `claude.test.ts` 20, `engram.test.ts` 6, `verify-claude.test.ts` 5, `backup.test.ts` 3, `port/unreadable.test.ts` 3, `port/plan-rail-refusals.test.ts` 2, `port/private-run-directory-mode.test.ts` 2, `gates/teardown-rotation-failure.test.ts` 1, `port/stale-unreadable-state.test.ts` 1, `port/teardown-unreadable-state.test.ts` 1. **That list is not the 20 announced skips above, and it is not the 131-case gap either — it is a third count, and this record now says so instead of letting it sit unexplained beside the other two**: it names, by file, the individual sites this suite marks with a win32 skip guard, which is neither how many of them register as this leg's own top-level `skipped` entries (a case nested inside a `describe` this leg skips wholesale is absorbed into the 111 unregistered rather than separately announced) nor how many cases the corrected total above counts. **The earlier line's "36 of 2,417 … (3 backup, 8 engram, 5 verify-claude, 20 claude)" was wrong twice over**: it counted the install family alone, leaving unnamed the ten port and gate cases the same guards skip, and it counted engram's eight before this fix round closed two of them. Rule (ii)'s gate is what makes the list a closure rather than a survey — no tracked file outside the guard module and its two justified exemptions may compare the running platform, so no case can skip on win32 without importing a guard.

  **Those 44 against the baseline's 33 red leaves, reconciled per file.** `claude.test.ts` 20 red → 20 skipped. `backup.test.ts` 1 red → 3 skipped: the `backupSizeKib` describe holds three cases and the other two agreed with `du -sk` on win32 at baseline, but the guard covers the whole oracle. `engram.test.ts` 7 red → 6 skipped: this fix round closed two counter-proofs, and the rename case — green on win32 at baseline only because it failed at the run probe before reaching the rename it asserts — is skipped now for saying so. `verify-claude.test.ts` 5 red → 5 skipped: the product-defect leaf was fixed rather than skipped, and the probe-bound leaf that passed on win32 at baseline sits inside the describe the guard now skips. 33 + 2 − 1 = 34 across those four files, plus the 10 outside them.

  **What the win32 leg runs, enumerated rather than claimed.** Every checksum case — all five under `checksum verification proves the mismatch case red` — every traversal case under `the extractor never uses an archive entry name as a write path`, every parser case, and every case under `what a degenerate or hostile archive may not do to this machine` except the rename case above. The two counter-proofs that used to be skipped assert the NEGATIVE on win32 — that the outcome is neither the checksum-mismatch failure nor a size-ceiling refusal, which is the contrastive point each pair makes — and keep the full `installed-on-path` assertion for the leg whose kernel starts the fixture; no injectable run-probe seam was added to that path. The five `checksum-verified download, extraction and placement` cases remain skipped, on the same fixture reason but — corrected in this round — under NO instrument that has yet produced a result; the disposition paragraph above states why. **Re-measured for this round rather than inherited**, pinning `process.platform` to `win32` over `test/install/engram.test.ts` alone: `tests 41, pass 35, fail 0, skipped 6`, the six being those five placement cases plus the rename case, and the run naming every case above as passed on that leg. **The earlier sentence claimed "every archive-security, checksum, traversal and parser case" while three cases inside exactly those describes were skipped**; traversal and parser were the only two of its four words that were true.

  **What this slice could NOT establish from Linux, named rather than assumed.** The port spawns the host CLIs by bare name — **re-counted in this fix round** with `rg -o 'spawnSync\("(claude|npm|npx)"'` over both files: `claude` at **eighteen** sites, seventeen in `core/src/install/claude.ts` plus an eighteenth the earlier enumeration missed, `spawnClaudeStdout` at `core/src/install/verify-claude.ts:270`; `npm` at three, all in `claude.ts`; `npx` at one, `verify-claude.ts:433` — and on Windows npm installs those as `claude.cmd`/`npm.cmd`, which Node refuses without `shell: true` (CVE-2024-27980) and which `PATHEXT` resolution is required to find at all. That is the SAME class as the defect fixed above and, reasoned from Node's documented refusal, it makes `oso install --host claude` non-functional on a native Windows host. It is left UNFIXED here on purpose: it changes every host-CLI spawn, `shell: true` on Windows is an argument-escaping decision for the security lane rather than a repair slice, and nothing in CI reads it today — `nightly.yml`'s Windows leg still runs the bash `install.ps1`, and no PR-gate test may spawn an installed host binary (G4). It is C3's to rule before the C3-D3 wrapper points Windows at the port.

  **Named debt routed to C3-S5 — the nightly tail NEITHER leg reaches, and two more citations of the same unread instrument.** Pre-existing and wider than engram, measured in run `33308513292`: `nightly-windows` failed at step 7, `Hook regression suite`, and gave up its whole tail — steps 8 through 13, six steps, every one `skipped`: the shell and PowerShell syntax checks, the CI-safe bootstrapper, the verify report, `verify.bat` and the report-only OpenCode Windows suite. `nightly-ubuntu` did the same shape at step 6, `Hook regression suite on bash 3.2`, shedding steps 7 through 12 — also six — the three verify reports, the pinned OpenCode binary and both OpenCode bars. So neither leg has been reading what this record credits it with reading. **Two further skip reasons in the tree cite that instrument as their compensating proof and are the SAME class as the engram one corrected above**; both are outside this slice's change surface, both are left untouched here, and both are S5's to make true: `core/test/install/engram-nightly.test.ts:10`, whose step is on neither leg of `origin/main` and would land behind the red hook suite on both; and `core/test/shell/lexer-differential.test.ts:83`, whose ubuntu step (`.github/workflows/nightly.yml:47-51`) is likewise absent — `git show origin/main:.github/workflows/nightly.yml | grep -c lexer-differential` returns 0 — and would land immediately after that same red step. **The rule this leaves behind: a step placed after a failing step, in a job where it carries no `if:`, is not an instrument but an intention, and a record may cite it only as configuration.**


**G5 — Host mechanisms after the rewrite. DECIDED.** Behaviour per gate is unchanged; only the transport changes. The table below is the source that `core/src/routes/routes.ts` encodes and `render.ts` renders; `tools/hook-gates.txt` is deleted in C2.

| gate | event | Claude Code | Codex | OpenCode |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| commit | PreToolUse | exec form `node gate.js commit`, matcher `Bash`, no `if` | `node "<hooks dir>/gate.js" commit` | in-process, `tool.execute.before`, matcher `bash` |
| edits | PreToolUse | exec form, matcher `Edit\|MultiEdit\|Write\|NotebookEdit\|mcp__fallow__fix_apply` | shell form, matcher `apply_patch\|mcp__fallow__fix_apply` | in-process, matcher `edit\|write\|fallow_fix_apply\|apply_patch` |
| unknown | PreToolUse | not wired (as today) | shell form, `--allow` list from the table | in-process, allow list from the table |
| proddeploy | PreToolUse | exec form, matcher `Bash\|mcp__.*deploy.*` | shell form, same matcher | in-process, matcher `bash\|.*deploy.*` |
| autocontinue | Stop | exec form; the push is `shouldContinue: true` on the pinned client, exit-2 fallback measured in C2 | not wired (as today) | in-process on `session.idle` (the same core function; `unattended-run.ts` is deleted) |
| planstop | Stop | not wired | shell form | not wired |
| planprompt | UserPromptSubmit | not wired | shell form | not wired |
| handoff | SubagentStop | not wired | shell form, matcher = the role list | native (wave tool) |
| statebin | SessionStart | exec form | not wired | native |
| stale | SessionStart | exec form, `additionalContext` | shell form | in-process, `experimental.chat.system.transform` |
| version | SessionStart | exec form | not wired | not wired |
| reanchor | SessionStart | exec form, `additionalContext` | not wired | in-process, `event` (`session.compacted`) |
| teardown | SessionEnd | exec form | shell form | in-process, `dispose` |
| git pre-commit | git hook | 3-line `sh` wrapper (git supplies `sh` on every OS) calling `node <plugin>/dist/precommit.js` | same wrapper | same wrapper |

The ERROR PATHS of every gate are unchanged and carried by the parity fixtures: no session id in the envelope → allow and log `payload-unparseable`; no state file for the repository → allow; a state file that exists but cannot be read → deny with `deny_unusable_state`'s message; an internal failure inside a gate → deny with the `failed unexpectedly and blocked this call` message (fail closed). A gate never invents a fourth outcome.

The deny mechanism for Claude and Codex stays the exit-code contract (exit 2, message on stderr) because both hosts honour it today and the parity fixtures assert it byte for byte. JSON output is used only where a gate already returns structured data (`additionalContext` on SessionStart, the Stop push). The OpenCode adapter maps a `GateVerdict` to the host exactly as `translateGateResult` does today, minus the subprocess.

- **Amended 2026-08-26 (C2-S0 repair, first carve-out).** The paragraph above governs the four PreToolUse deniers only — `commit`, `edits`, `unknown`, `proddeploy` — the exact four scripts that call `require_session` (`plugin/hooks/lib.sh:320-327`; callers confirmed at `block-commit-until-green.sh:67`, `block-edits-without-slice.sh:9`, `block-unknown-tool.sh:22`, `block-prod-deploy.sh:177`). The other nine gates each handle a missing session id, an unreadable state file, and their own failure transport differently, measured against their current bash:

  | hook | missing session id | unreadable state | failure transport |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `autocontinue` | allows silently, logs nothing (`auto-continue.sh:131-132`) | allows silently, logs nothing (`auto-continue.sh:137`) | `{}` on stdout, exit 0 |
  | `reanchor` | allows silently, logs nothing (`reanchor-after-compact.sh:38`) | allows silently, logs nothing (`reanchor-after-compact.sh:43`) | exit 0, no `additionalContext` |
  | `planstop` | denies, event `plan-approval-capture-blocked` (`capture-plan-approval.sh:58-63`) | denies, same event (`capture-plan-approval.sh:131-135`) | Stop `{"decision":"block",...}`, or `{"continue":false,...}` under `stop_hook_active` |
  | `handoff` | fails the publish, event `handoff-publish-failed` (`publish-subagent-handoff.sh:30`) | fails the publish, same event (`publish-subagent-handoff.sh:44-50`) | the hook itself still exits 0 — only the publish fails, the SubagentStop is never blocked |
  | `teardown` | no check, exits 0 silently (`cleanup-state.sh:100-108`) | no check, exits 0 silently (no readability check on any `.state` glob in the file) | exit 0 |
  | `stale` | never checks emptiness — an absent session id compares equal to nothing and the warning path still runs (`warn-stale-state.sh:8-13`) | never checks emptiness — an unreadable file's empty `state_value` reads the same as a foreign session (`warn-stale-state.sh:11-13`) | SessionStart `additionalContext` warning, or silent exit 0 |
  | `statebin` | reads no session id at all (`persist-state-bin.sh` calls `hook_session` nowhere) | never opens the per-repo `.state` file | exit 0 |
  | `version` | reads no session id at all (`warn-stale-version.sh` calls `hook_session` nowhere) | never opens the per-repo `.state` file | SessionStart `additionalContext`, or exit 0 |
  | `proddeploy` | shares the four deniers' `require_session` path | its own three-way `run_marker` (`unmarked` allows outright, `uncertain` denies through `deny_unusable_state`, `armed` proceeds) plus the `reads_as_state_records` helper (`block-prod-deploy.sh:160-215`) | PreToolUse deny, same exit-2 transport as its three siblings |

  `proddeploy` shares the frozen paragraph's missing-session-id path with its three siblings; its unreadable-state and transport columns are listed here because its `run_marker` three-way is richer than the binary the frozen paragraph describes.

- **Amended 2026-08-28 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Decision 8, C2-S4).** The commit row above loses the `if: "Bash(git *)"` it carried, and a standing rule replaces it: **no `if` on any gate whose verdict can be deny or block; `if` is permitted only on advisory hooks, and only after the predicate's matching semantics are measured on the pinned client.** Basis: `if` takes permission-rule syntax and a Bash permission rule is a literal prefix match on the command string, so `sudo git commit` does not begin with `git ` and the client skips the hook before the lexer ever runs — measured on the pinned client 2.1.250 through its own `Skipping hook due to if condition "…" not matching` log line. `core/src/shell/lexer.ts` strips wrappers and resolves basenames, which is why `tests/hooks-test.sh`'s adversarial table pins ~20 spellings as denied, and an `if` would silently bypass all of them plus every `node -e` residue call. The cost accepted in exchange is one node cold start per Bash call, already bounded by G4 at ≤ 100 ms. The delegate that wrote the `if` into G5 and C2-D5 owns the correction: it was written from the hooks reference without measuring the predicate against adversarial spellings, and it is wrong for any DENY gate by construction.

- **Amended 2026-08-26 (C2-S0 repair, second carve-out).** C2-D3 deliberately changes behaviour in exactly three places: the disarm inside `closeSlice`; the expiry reachable via `waitExpired(now)` from the SessionStart `stale` gate and the OpenCode idle path; and the run-keyed sidecar (G7's amendment below). "Unchanged" governs the other eleven gates — this is the sole named exception to the "behaviour per gate is unchanged" sentence at the top of G5.
  - **A FOURTH ROW, added 2026-08-29 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable 9eab15, Decision 16a). Wait-mark removal failure.** Bash: `rm -f "$wait_file" 2>/dev/null || true` at both sites in `auto-continue.sh` — silent by design, verdict untouched. Port: verdict, exit code and stdout untouched, and an `auto-continue-degraded` event carrying the cause. The delta is ADDITIVE and failure-path only — an events line where the bash wrote nothing. **The reading is recorded here so no later judge re-flags the `try/catch`: a catch that KEEPS the cause and puts it on the record is not a swallow.** `removeWaitMark` returns `string | undefined` — undefined on success, a missing file included, and the cause on failure — and the two call sites in `autocontinue.ts` consume it in the file's own idiom, the one `pushUnlessCapped`'s cap branch already uses: the not-running path returns `ALLOWED` or `degraded(sessionId, failure)`, and the non-delegation path returns the `pushUnlessCapped` outcome with `degradedEvent(sessionId, failure)` appended to its events. **Returning `degraded()` itself on the push path was REJECTED and the reason is the whole point:** `degraded()` yields `{ kind: "allow" }`, so it would convert PUSH into ALLOW and stop continuing the run the moment the mark became unremovable — the same rail breakage as throwing, minus the exit code. Dropping the wrapper outright was rejected for that breakage measured on four axes at once: verdict `allow`/`push` → `gateError`, exit 0 → 1, the Stop continuation envelope → empty stdout, and the `auto-continued` event → none, because `AUTOCONTINUE_GATE` is routed with `loudRun` as its `onFailure`.
  - **Fifth clause, stated positively 2026-08-29 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable 9eab15, Decision 17a). Removal failures are classified as coreutils `rm -f` classifies them.** `ENOENT`, which `force` already passes, and `ENOTDIR` on the mark's path are ABSENCE and record nothing, as the bash recorded nothing; every other errno — `EISDIR`, `EACCES`, `EPERM`, `EBUSY`, and any not named here — is a FAILURE and reaches the record with its cause. Measured on 2026-08-29 rather than reasoned: on coreutils 9.11, `rm -f parent-is-a-file/mark` with no redirection and no `|| true` prints nothing and exits 0, so `-f` treats `ENOTDIR` as a nonexistent file beside `ENOENT`, while `rm -f dirmark` prints "Is a directory" and exits 1; node's `rmSync(force)` throws on both. So the port's predicate is not a widening of the fourth row but the frozen tool's own `-f` semantics carried across, which is why the tally fixture that seeds `runs/{repo}` as a regular file stayed at two degradations — the bash never had a third to emit, and that fixture's layout defect is already on the record once through the tally. **The ABSENT set is the closed one and the failure set is open, deliberately: an errno nobody enumerated lands in the visible bucket.**

- **Amended 2026-08-27 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, S2 fix round 3, Decision 7).** Two rounds of C2-S2 read the first carve-out table's `failure transport` column as VERDICT transport and shipped a uniform answer twice, wrong both times (round 1: every gate's internal failure routed through the four deniers' exit-2 `block_with_gate_error`; round 2: every gate's internal failure routed to a silent exit 0). The column always meant INTERNAL-FAILURE BEHAVIOUR — what a gate does when something inside IT fails, never what it emits when its own checks run clean. The table above is corrected in place: `failure transport` is renamed **`internal failure (measured)`**; a new **`verdict transport`** column is added for what a gate emits normally; every cell is re-filled from a fresh measurement (`env -u HOME plugin/hooks/<hook>.sh`, and for the two digest-tool guards, a `PATH` stripped of both `sha256sum` and `shasum`), not from reading — reproduced by the applier of this round, not trusted from the table above. A tenth row, `planprompt` (`approve-plan-token.sh`, UserPromptSubmit), was missing from the original nine and is added here; `proddeploy` is carried over unchanged (it is one of the four deniers, listed for richness only, per the closing sentence below the original table).

  | `planprompt` | class-dependent, measured both ways: an ORDINARY prompt outside native Plan Mode allows silently regardless of session id (`approve-plan-token.sh:45-46`); an approve/cancel control prompt denies without one (measured: `CANCEL OSO PLAN` with no `session_id` → exit 0, stdout `{"decision":"block","reason":"oso-code: the plan-control prompt has no valid session identity."}`; `approve-plan-token.sh:79-80`) | denies (measured: a `plan_approval=pending` state file made unreadable, approve action → exit 0, stdout `{"decision":"block","reason":"oso-code: the pending plan state is unreadable; native approval cannot open the execution gate."}`; `approve-plan-token.sh:75-76`) | HOME unset is loud: exit 1, `lib.sh:260: HOME: unbound variable` on stderr (measured, `env -u HOME approve-plan-token.sh`) | UserPromptSubmit `{}` on stdout for an ordinary prompt (measured), `{"decision":"block","reason":"…"}` for a refused control prompt (measured), or `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"UserPromptSubmit","additionalContext":"…"}}` on a matched approve/cancel/amend (read: `approve-plan-token.sh:65-67,132-137`) |
