- **Amended 2026-08-28 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, S3 fix round 1, Decision 7).** The `planstop` row above still carried two cells marked "read, not reproduced … outside this round's measurement budget" for `capture-plan-approval.sh:131-135` (unreadable state) and `capture-plan-approval.sh:127-129` (the SECOND, narrower digest-tool guard inside `internal failure`). Both are now measured — twice, identically, by the S3 applier and again by its verifier: `:131-135` → exit 0, stdout `{"decision":"block","reason":"oso-code: the approval document or its plan artifacts could not be recorded; execution remains blocked."}`, stderr empty, event `plan-approval-capture-blocked`; `:127-129` (`PATH` stripped of both `sha256sum` and `shasum`) → exit 0, stdout `{"decision":"block","reason":"oso-code: no SHA-256 implementation is available to bind this approval document."}`, stderr empty, event `plan-approval-capture-blocked` with an empty `command` field. The table above is corrected in place; Decision 7's own instruction was to re-fill every cell from measurement, and a cell left marked unmeasured once its measurement exists is the exact defect that instruction was written to end.

**G6 — State. DECIDED.** `core/src/state/schema.ts` types the runtime state as a discriminated shape and `store.ts` reads/writes the SAME `key=value` file at the SAME path (`$HOME/.local/state/oso-code/<sha256 of the git common dir>.state`) with the same atomic-rename write; illegal combinations are rejected on read with a named error (`StateInvariantError`) instead of being interpreted. `transitions.ts` owns every multi-key write the flows perform (`arm slice`, `close slice`, `arm auto`, `park`, `disarm`), so a rule such as "a slice close returns `auto_wait` to `none`" is CODE the CLI runs, never prose the orchestrator must remember. The CLI keeps every verb and flag `plugin/bin/oso-state` accepts today (`set get show clear event capture-plan approve-plan cancel-plan amend-plan journal handoff publish|wait|consume`) with identical stdout/stderr, plus two new verbs: `close-slice <n>` (writes `active_slice=none verify_green=true auto_wait=none` in one atomic write) and `deny-pattern add <pattern>` (the write `bodies/plan.md:19` currently asks the orchestrator to do by hand).

- **G6's two new verbs DO NOT EXIST, measured 2026-09-01 at `565bc0e`, and the gap is closed by a slice rather than carried (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 47 Q1).** The paragraph above promises the CLI keeps every verb `plugin/bin/oso-state` accepts today **plus** `close-slice <n>` and `deny-pattern add <pattern>`. `core/src/state/cli.ts`'s switch carries eleven verbs — `set get show clear event journal capture-plan approve-plan cancel-plan amend-plan handoff`, with `publish | wait | consume` under handoff — and NEITHER of the two. `close-slice` appears nowhere in `core/src` at all; `deny-pattern` appears only in `core/src/gates/proddeploy.ts`, and only as the READER of the patterns file, which nothing writes. **So G6's own purpose is unmet where it is most quoted:** the sentence that a slice close returning `auto_wait` to `none` is "CODE the CLI runs, never prose the orchestrator must remember" describes a `closeSlice()` that exists in `core/src/state/transitions.ts`, is tested, and is reachable from no verb — §9.1's defect 1 with its fix written and never wired. **The wider count, corrected in the ruling that received it:** `transitions.ts` exports ten functions and EIGHT have no consumer in `core/src`; `armPlan` is consumed by `core/src/state/plan.ts` and `park` by `core/src/gates/autocontinue.ts` and `core/src/gates/reanchor.ts` — so *unconsumed* and *unreachable from a CLI* are two different claims and `park` is the case that separates them. **C5-S0b adds exactly the two verbs G6 names and nothing else.** Whether the CLI grows verbs for the remaining eight, or those functions are deleted as dead code, is a post-rewrite question for the operator and is recorded here as a residual rather than decided in C5. **Why a slice and not a carry-forward:** the alternative ships 0.26.0 with a DECIDED contract unmet and with prose instructing the model to hand-write a gate's deny file through shell, on Windows included; and if the verbs landed after the rewrite, every flow's prose would be edited twice and C5-D5's axis A re-pinned twice. **S0b's conditions.** `close-slice <n>` dispatches to `closeSlice()` rather than re-implementing its patch, writes through the store's one atomic write, logs its event, and REFUSES with a named error and an unchanged state when `active_slice` is not `<n>` — the argument exists so that closing a slice nobody armed is an error rather than a silent write. `deny-pattern add <pattern>` writes one line to the path `proddeploy.ts` computes, through ONE exported resolver shared by writer and reader rather than a second spelling of the path; creates the directory; validates the pattern through the ERE translator AT ADD TIME and refuses an untranslatable one loudly, since a pattern the gate would later log as untranslatable is the guarded-failure class written in advance; is idempotent, reporting an existing pattern as already present; and names the file it wrote. Fixtures in C1-D2's shape, plus ONE consumer proof — add a pattern, drive the gate over a matching command, read the deny. **This run keeps §9.1's hand writes**: it executes under the installed 0.25.0 rails until C6, and the verbs serve 0.26.0's prose.

**G7 — Events and journals. DECIDED.** `events.jsonl`, `runs/<repo>/<change>.log`, the push tally and the wait sidecar keep their paths and line formats. The wait sidecar is keyed on the RUN (`auto_change` at arming plus the session id) so a child boundary never re-dates it (defect 3).

- **Amended 2026-08-26 (C2-S0 repair).** Stated explicitly for whoever ports the sidecar: its LINE FORMAT is preserved byte for byte; only its KEY changes, from label+session (today's `.waiting` sidecar) to the run (`auto_change` at arming plus the session id, as the paragraph above already names).

**G8 — Tests. DECIDED.** Three layers and only the first two run in the PR gate. (1) Unit and gate tests in-process: a gate is a pure function `(envelope, state, clock, fs) → verdict`; the entry point is the only thing that touches stdin/stdout. (2) Installer tests against a fixture `HOME` created per test, no network, assertions read the resulting TOML/JSON through the parsers, never through grep. (3) Host certification in `nightly.yml`: the contract and behaviour bars against the pinned host binaries, a real install into a fixture HOME on ubuntu, macos and windows, `claude plugin validate --strict`. Nightly red opens an issue and never blocks a PR. The parity fixtures (§7) are layer 1 during the migration and stay as the regression net after it.

**G9 — CI. DECIDED.** `pr.yml`: matrix ubuntu-latest + windows-latest; steps: `npm ci`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run check` (bundles and rendered manifests equal a fresh build), `claude plugin validate --strict plugin` and `--strict .` on ubuntu. `nightly.yml`: everything in G8 layer 3, plus the bash-3.2 container until C3 deletes the last bash. The current `ci.yml` is deleted by C0 after both files exist.

- **G8 and G9 amended 2026-08-28 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Decision 8, C2-S4). The installed-copy smoke splits by what it needs.** Parsing `plugin/hooks/hooks.json` and running its exact command line with `node` against a fixture HOME — one PreToolUse envelope, both directions, on ubuntu AND windows — reads no host binary, so it is layer 1 and rides `npm test` on both PR legs without touching G4's prohibition (`core/test/gates/installed-manifest-smoke.test.ts`). Only the `claude --version` and `claude plugin validate --strict plugin` half reads an installed host binary, so it is layer 3: `nightly.yml` gains a Node 22 `setup-node` plus `npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code` on the ubuntu leg, the same global install on the windows leg beside the `setup-node` it already had, and one `Installed-copy smoke against the pinned client` step per leg.
- **G9 amended 2026-08-27 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable 9eab15).** On the PR gate the step scope differs per leg and is stated rather than inherited: steps 10 through 14 are Linux-only, and Windows runs the workspace steps (`npm ci`, typecheck, `npm test`, `npm run check`) plus `bash -n` and the plugin linter. Windows OpenCode is exercised in `nightly.yml`, report-only, until C3 makes it a verified surface — `docs/rewrite/ts-core-roadmap.md:279` records that it leaves C3 as UNVERIFIED. C0-D1 put no Windows steps on the PR gate at all; the matrix C1-D4 added widened three pre-existing steps onto Windows without a decision saying so, and two of those three now pass there.

- **Named residual, routed to C3 — Windows OpenCode.** CI run 33052916830 measured `tests 152, pass 136, fail 16` on windows-latest. The classes: `not ok 7` is the strict path comparison at `core/src/state/plan.ts:203` reappearing because `opencode/`'s own test harness builds paths differently from `core/test/support/state-sandbox.ts` — **this one travels to C2-S5**, whose payload takes it first and whose Verify records the Windows nightly count before and after; `not ok 31, 47, 50` are the `spawnSync("bash", …)` gate path in `gates.ts`, which C2-D6 already has S5 DELETE in favour of the in-process core, so bringing it up on Windows now would be work S5 removes; `not ok 54, 56, 59` are identity and worktree tests where `identity.ts` walks the git common dir by hand, and `not ok 132–143` are twelve `oso_wave` worktree tests — **both of those classes stay routed to C3**. Steps 6 through 9 pass on Windows as of `c6b4eb7`, so C3's Windows-OpenCode work starts from that floor.

**G10 — Prose. DECIDED.** One `SKILL.md` per skill per host wrapper, ≤ 300 lines, carrying the flow itself; host bindings in `references/<host>.md` one level deep, present only where the host differs; agents authored once per role in `core/src/agents/*.md` and rendered to `plugin/agents/*.md`, `codex/agents/*.toml`, `opencode/agents/*.md` by `npm run build`; no rule stated in more than one file; no ADR ids, version history or "before this version" sentences in runtime prose; absolutes only where a gate enforces them. Structural checks that survive (as tests in `core/test/structure/`): every host wraps every skill; every path a prose file references resolves; rendered manifests and bundles equal a fresh build; no shipped file carries a home path; every dot-directory is owned or ignored; no verification script invokes the host binary directly; shipped executables carry no decision citations; the verdict grammar has one implementation; the Impeccable pin is never a placeholder; parity docs name the pinned harness version; the OpenCode platform overlay covers every section or declares the divergence; shell sources carry no comment below the header (until the last shell source is gone).

- **G10 amended 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 44 Q1): the surviving structural checks are ELEVEN, not twelve.** The list above names "the OpenCode platform overlay covers every section or declares the divergence" among the checks that survive the rewrite. **C5-D1 extinguishes that check's SUBJECT rather than relocating it:** C5's exit line requires zero platform files under `_shared/platform/`, and a comparator over platform overlays has nothing left to compare once every overlay is gone. Host divergence afterwards lives in `references/<host>.md`, present only where a host differs — a different concern, with no counterpart roster and no comparison to make. **Measured on a scratch copy of `1c85baa` and reproduced independently by the delegate before the ruling:** deleting `plugin/skills/_shared/platform/` alone takes `bash tests/plugin-lint.sh` to exit 1 with six violations, every one of them from `check_platform_section_inventory_is_matched_or_declared` and every one from that function's declared-divergence loop, while its overlay loop passes at zero iterations; removing the six flagged rows of `docs/parity-opencode.md`'s "Section inventory divergences" table returns the bar to `lint: clean — twelve rules`, exit 0. **A check that passes by walking an empty directory is the defect class this roadmap has spent four children catching, so it is deleted immediately before its subject dies — not with it — because NO PARTIAL STATE of the deletion can pass it, measured on scratch copies of `47183ca`: removing only `platform/claude/` leaves five violations and removing only `platform/opencode/` leaves twenty, so under the by-host pre-split the check and `docs/parity-opencode.md`'s section must both be gone before the first overlay is.** C5-S1 executes it: the check and its four helpers leave `tests/plugin-lint.sh`, its count string reads eleven, and the WHOLE "Section inventory divergences" section of `docs/parity-opencode.md` goes — heading, both paragraphs and all eight rows, the two `no counterpart file` rows included — because after S1 every sentence in that section names a file that no longer exists. **S5 ports ELEVEN checks into `core/test/structure/` and deletes `tests/plugin-lint.sh` under G14 as planned.** G14 does not bind this removal: it governs bash FILES, and this takes one function out of a file G14 already governs at S5, whose Verify carries the completeness grep for all five names.
- **G10 amended again 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 50 Q1): the render sources live under `core/src/prose/`, and "authored ONCE per role" is an END STATE this document now spells out rather than a property any one slice delivers.** **The path.** G10 above names `core/src/agents/*.md`. A flat per-role glob cannot hold what a role actually needs: the three hosts' bodies genuinely differ — Claude's carries `model` and `tools`, Codex's a sandbox mode and a reasoning effort, OpenCode's a permission deny list — so a role needs one body PER HOST, and the skill bodies need the same kind of home. The sources therefore live at `core/src/prose/agents/<role>/` and `core/src/prose/skills/<id>/`, with `routes.ts` carrying the per-host data table and `render.ts` the pure renderers, rendered by `npm run build` and diffed by `npm run check`. Measured before the amendment rather than after: `core/src/agents` occurs in NO tracked `*.ts`, `*.mjs`, `*.json` or `*.md` outside `docs/` — only three times in this document — so the path was a record, never a dependency, and nothing in the tree breaks either way. **The "once", which C5-S1a does NOT deliver and which this amendment refuses to paper over.** After S1a the tree holds THREE FULL BODIES per universal role — `oso-applier` at 49 + 39 + 41 = 129 lines, `oso-verifier` 142, `oso-integrator` 140, the four judge roles 16 each, 475 in total — and the difference between two of them is not a host delta on a shared body: it is the SAME contract paraphrased, `Read the whole rubric before writing (it is short)` against `Read the whole handed rubric before writing`, `Follow the ledger. It is frozen: you never re-decide` against `Follow the frozen ledger. Never re-decide`, with host-specific paragraphs interleaved. That is G10's own "no rule stated in more than one file" broken three times per role, carried into the new namespace. **S1a was RIGHT to land it so and that is why the amendment exists instead of a finding:** an EXPAND slice whose entire proof is a byte-identical oracle cannot also rewrite the bodies it renders, and the oracle is what makes the pipeline trustworthy for prose that will have none. **The END STATE, stated so nobody reads S1a's layout as finished:** `agents/<role>/body.md` carries the role's contract ONCE, `agents/<role>/<host>.md` carries only what that host spells differently — C5-D1's shape for skills, applied to agents — and the renderer concatenates body plus host delta under the host's front matter. The three full bodies per role are S1a's oracle state, not G10's end state. C5-S1c performs the collapse. **What proves it, since there is no byte oracle for a slice that deliberately changes rendered output:** the capability inventory's axes B, C and D over the RENDERED files, which is where two or three hosts read each role, plus the verifier's read of each host delta against the standard "only what this host spells differently".

- **G10's ADR-id ban gains the ASSIGNMENT it never had, 2026-09-02 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 57).** G10 forbids ADR ids in runtime prose and no slice in this document closed them, so C5 was on course to close with a DECIDED rule unmet — the mirror of the defect this roadmap keeps catching, a rule nobody executes rather than a check nobody can satisfy. **It is not pedantry: G11 freezes `docs/decisions/` as history no check reads, so an id in runtime prose points a model at frozen history the runtime must not need.** **THE SET, ruled rather than assumed, because the count moves with it:** runtime prose is every `*.md` and `*.toml` under `plugin/`, `codex/` and `opencode/` — what a host ships and a model reads — and under `core/src/prose/`, their sources. **Excluded and named:** `docs/**` and `CHANGELOG.md`, RECORD, where an id is correct; `core/test/**`, whose anchored fixtures MUST carry the anchor's ids; this document; and `README.md`, which is not runtime prose and whose own six lines die under S5's map criterion instead. **MEASURED on that set at `590f552`, and the pair matters because the two numbers differ:** **134 occurrences on 103 lines across 31 files.** **A figure this orchestrator published as "109 occurrences" was the LINE count of a wider set mislabelled**, caught by the delegate and re-derived here rather than quietly replaced: that wider set — everything but `docs/`, `CHANGELOG.md` and `core/test/` — carries 143 occurrences on 109 lines, and it includes `README.md`'s six, which the ruled set excludes. `rg -c` counts lines and `rg -o | wc -l` counts occurrences, which is the whole distance between the two claims. **No id sits in CODE:** `core/src`, `core/scripts`, `opencode/plugin` and `bootstrap` carry zero across every `*.ts`, `*.mjs` and `*.js`, so the existing comment gate is already doing its half and this is a prose-only class. **THE REPAIR IS DISTRIBUTED TO THE OWNERS**, on Decision 56 Q3's principle that a surface is repaired by the slice that rewrites it: S1c-3 takes `oso-integrator`'s three, S2 takes plan and roadmap where `bodies/plan.md` alone carries the bulk, S3 quick and debug, S4 the judges and the shared files, and every rendered copy follows its source through `npm run build`. **THE GATE IS A WIDENING, NOT A NEW TEST** — `core/test/structure/comments-cite-no-decisions.test.ts:9` already scans four prefixes and its pattern at `core/test/structure/comments-cite-no-decisions.test.ts:15` already matches `ADR-[0-9]{4}`, so widening it to whole text over the prose set is a prefix list and one branch, which is G12's fewer-moving-parts rule choosing between two designs. Ported at S5, **shown RED on the tree of the day it lands, with the line and occurrence counts it finds stated**, then green as the last straggler goes; ≈ 20 lines of `core/test`, inside the noise of S5's own 480–1,030 projection. **STRAGGLERS ARE NAMED RATHER THAN HOPED AWAY:** C5-S1c-1's one id and C5-S1c-2's two are already committed or in flight and are stripped at S5 under the gate — which is exactly what makes a class closed by five slices countable at all. **AND THE LINE BETWEEN A STRIP AND A RETIREMENT, so the chapter's capability figure keeps meaning what it means:** removing an id while the sentence keeps its RULE needs NO row, because a pointer is not a rule; a sentence that was only history — "ADR-0093 fixed the cap at …", "before this version …" — is a RETIREMENT under Decision 54 and takes a `prose` row with home `none` and the reason "version history, G10".

**G11 — Decisions record. DECIDED.** `docs/decisions/` is frozen as history: nothing is deleted, nothing new is added, and no check reads it. This document and the engram ledgers are the record of the rewrite. `docs/blueprint.md` is rewritten in C5 as a ≤ 150-line map of the new tree. `CHANGELOG.md` keeps one entry per release with no rule counting.

**G12 — Execution rules for every applier and verifier (from the operator's standing rules).** Sweep a defect CLASS, never an instance; the verifier's remit is bounded in its launch prompt and everything outside it goes to a `for_the_sweep:` list; every behavioural sentence written in prose or a commit message names the file that supports it; fix rounds are capped at two per slice, after which the finding goes to the sweep with its evidence; zero inline comments in anything written (the rubric's debt class, no repo exception); the green window is two Bash calls — the state write, then the commit — never one; when two options tie, the one with fewer moving parts wins.

**G13 — Models. DECIDED.** The operator runs the orchestrator on Opus. `oso-applier`, `oso-verifier` and the judges launch with `model: sonnet` by default, named in the announcing sentence. `model: opus`, with the reason named, on exactly these slices: C1-S2 (extracting the parity fixtures out of `tests/hooks-test.sh`), C2-S1 (the shell lexer port and the four PreToolUse deniers — the option-arity defect class lives there), C2-S3 (the Stop contract measured against the pinned client), C4-S1 (the bars against real binaries).

**G14 — Deletion discipline. DECIDED.** Every bash file is removed only by a CONTRACT slice whose Verify carries the completeness grep proving zero remaining references (`rg -n '<file>' --glob '!docs/decisions/**'` returns nothing) and whose diff deletes the tests that exercised it. Expand and Migrate slices never delete.

**G14 amended 2026-08-29 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Decision 12, C2-S6).** The completeness check is ZERO LIVE REFERENCES, not zero occurrences of the string. G14's intent was that no CODE PATH depends on a deleted file; a string that names the file as an identifier or as a record is not a dependency, and a check that can never be satisfied is not a bar. LIVE means anything that loads, sources, spawns, copies, hashes, manifests or requires the path. The remaining occurrences are classified per file in the slice report under exactly three classes — IDENTIFIER (the `script` column of `routes.ts`, which IS the event log's `gate` value, pinned by fixtures, and which a behaviour-preserving port keeps), RECORD (`CHANGELOG.md`, this document, `docs/decisions/**`), and CITATION (line-free provenance per Decision 9) — and any hit outside those three is LIVE and blocks the delete. The check is mechanical rather than read: a structure test greps the tree excluding `docs/`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `routes.ts`'s `script:` column and the `"gate":` values of fixtures, and must return nothing. Renaming the event field to a bare gate id was REJECTED: that is a change to the RECORD FORMAT, not a port. Whether `gate` should ever carry a bare id is a post-rewrite decision for the operator, recorded as a residual beside the two lexer holes.

**G16 — Landing. DECIDED.** The PLAN mode's own rule applies to every child: an AUTO child cuts `oso-run/<child>` from wherever the checkout stands, commits on it per slice, and its close pushes that branch and opens its PR against `main`. Children are strictly sequential, so the branches STACK: each child's branch carries every earlier child's commits. Nothing merges mid-chain (a PR merge is never-solo). At the presence phase the operator merges the LAST closed child's PR — it carries everything — and closes the earlier PRs as superseded by it. The production boundary for this repository stays "writing to `main`" (the `oso/preferences` record: no separate staging, PR base `main`).

**G15 — Inherited from `opencode-plan-execution` (engram `oso/opencode-plan-execution/ledger`, `/plan`). DECIDED.** That run was PARKED at slice 3 of 11 on 2026-08-25. Its frozen decisions D2–D9 and D13 stay decided and are NOT re-planned: §11 assigns each to the child that lands it and the test that proves it, and that child's ledger records them as INHERITED naming this entry. Its slices 4–11 are never executed as written; their intent lands through §11. Its named pendings travel to this roadmap's presence phase.

**G17 — Closing bar for a child. DECIDED (2026-08-26, C2-S0 repair; amends G16).** A child is CLOSED only once `pr.yml` is green on BOTH ubuntu AND windows for its last commit, observed by the orchestrator with `gh run view`; the local bar (`npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run check`, `bash tests/hooks-test.sh` and the rest) is necessary but never sufficient on its own. The child opens its PR as a DRAFT at its FIRST slice, not at the close, and it is marked ready for review only at the close once both CI legs are observed green. This amends G16, which put the PR opening at the close alone; G16's stacking, never-solo-merge and presence-phase rules stand as written.

## 3. The queue

Seven children, in order. Every child is a PLAN-mode change arriving with its intent, its own decisions and a proposed cut; its own planning phases confirm the cut against the tree and never reopen a decision written here. Sequential execution for C0, C1 and C6; parallel allowed from C2 on (the bar is `npm test`, which tolerates concurrent runs — each test owns its temp dirs).

### C0 — `rewrite-freeze`: a green 10-minute gate, prose lints gone, nothing else changed

**Intent.** Make the repository cheap to iterate on BEFORE any port: the PR gate becomes fast and green on the existing bash, the checks that police prose wording are deleted, and the record stops demanding per-decision bookkeeping. No behaviour of the harness changes.

**Decisions.**
- C0-D1: `.github/workflows/pr.yml` runs, on ubuntu only for now: `bash -n` syntax, `bash tests/plugin-lint.sh`, `cd opencode && npm ci && npm test`, `bash tests/hooks-test.sh`, `claude plugin validate --strict` (both manifests). Windows and the bash-3.2 container move to `nightly.yml` together with the three `verify-*.sh` reports, the pinned-OpenCode download and both bars.
- C0-D2: every assertion in `tests/hooks-test.sh` that reads the developer machine (installed binary versions, wall-clock bounds, network) is either given a fixture or moved under `OSO_NIGHTLY=1`. The two local failures of 2026-08-25 name the class: `the opencode installer pin matches the installed binary` and `the bounded MCP drift check ends well inside a generous multiple of its own bound`; the Windows and bash-3.2 failures (`Engram repair …`, `every workaround spelling in this table …`) are the same class.
- C0-D3: `tests/plugin-lint.sh` keeps exactly the twelve structural rules G10 lists and deletes the rest, including the four decision-bookkeeping rules (`check_every_decision_records_where_it_landed`, `check_blueprint_index_names_every_decision`, `check_cited_decisions_resolve_to_a_file`, `check_living_records_cite_lines_that_still_carry_something`) and the two count rules. README's `tests/` row becomes one sentence naming the two scripts. CHANGELOG's next entry states the rule count once and no rule reads it.
- C0-D4: `docs/decisions/` frozen per G11. A note at the top of `docs/blueprint.md` says so; the index of decisions stays as it is.

**Proposed slices.**
- S1 — `pr.yml` + `nightly.yml` exist and `ci.yml` is deleted. Files: `.github/workflows/*`. Verify: `gh workflow list` shows both; a PR run of `pr.yml` completes green under 20 min on the existing suite (observed by the orchestrator with `gh run view`); `Verify-exception: workflow files carry no unit test — the run itself is the check`. Depends-on: none.
- S2 — machine-dependent asserts fixtured or gated (C0-D2). Files: `tests/hooks-test.sh`, `bootstrap/lib/verification-fixtures.sh`. Verify: `bash tests/hooks-test.sh` exits 0 on this machine with the OpenCode binary at 1.18.23 and no network; failing-check: a new case asserting the pin check reads `OSO_PINNED_OPENCODE_VERSION` from the fixture rather than the machine. Depends-on: none.
- S3 — prose lints deleted (C0-D3). Files: `tests/plugin-lint.sh`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/blueprint.md`. Verify: `bash tests/plugin-lint.sh` prints `lint: clean — twelve rules`; `rg -c '^check_' tests/plugin-lint.sh` = 12; failing-check: the linter's own self-test asserts each of the twelve rules flags its fixture. Depends-on: none.
- Exit for the child: `pr.yml` green on the last commit; `bash tests/hooks-test.sh` green locally; no file under `plugin/skills` changed.

### C1 — `rewrite-state`: the workspace and `oso-state` in TypeScript, bash and TS proven equal

**Intent.** Stand up the TypeScript workspace (G2, G3, G9) and port the state kernel — `plugin/bin/oso-state`, 725 lines, called at 259 sites of the suite and by every gate — as a library plus a CLI whose observable behaviour is proven identical to the bash by parity fixtures, then switch every caller to it and delete the bash.

**Decisions.**
- C1-D1: `core/src/state/store.ts` reads and writes the existing file format and path (G6); `schema.ts` validates; `transitions.ts` carries `armSlice`, `closeSlice`, `armAuto`, `park`, `disarm`, `armWait`, `clearWait`. `cli.ts` maps the verbs one-to-one to today's; stdout and stderr texts are copied from the bash verbatim (the parity fixtures assert them).
- C1-D2: parity fixtures live in `core/test/fixtures/state/*.json`: `{ name, env, state_before, argv, stdin, expect: { exit, stdout, stderr, state_after, events_appended } }`. `core/test/parity.test.ts` runs every fixture against BOTH `plugin/bin/oso-state` and `node plugin/dist/oso-state.js` while both exist. Fixtures are extracted from the `oso-state` cases of `tests/hooks-test.sh` by reading them, never by running the bash and recording its output (a recorded expectation is tautological).
  - **Amended 2026-08-26 (operator reconciliation).** The superseded text above demanded a count of ≥ 120 fixtures "for every verb", extracted "by reading" the suite. Measured against the tree: 257 lines mention `oso-state`, ~39 are not invocations, ~170 are scaffolding for gate and installer tests, and assertions whose own subject is the CLI total 19 clusters / ~51 checks, collapsing to **40–60 fixtures**. `clear` has ZERO assertions anywhere; `usage()`, the malformed-argv paths and the lock-acquisition timeout at `plugin/bin/oso-state:68-71` have no source at all. The amended decision: fixtures are still read, never recorded; their count is **what reading yields, 40–60, recorded rather than pinned to a floor**; and the surface the suite never asserted is covered by **PORT TESTS** written from the implementation's own preconditions and literal strings, labelled as such in the file and never counted as parity.
- C1-D3: `plugin/hooks/lib.sh` resolves `oso-state` through `OSO_STATE_BIN` already; the MIGRATE slice points it, `persist-state-bin.sh`, the git pre-commit hook, `opencode/plugin/oso/plan-state.ts` and the installers at `node <dist>/oso-state.js`. The bash `plugin/bin/oso-state` is deleted by the CONTRACT slice with G14's grep.
  - **Correction 2026-08-26 (factual error).** `lib.sh` resolves no binary at all — it reads the `.state` file directly via `state_file_for`/`state_value`/`state_says`. The resolution expression `${OSO_STATE_BIN:-$HOOK_DIR/../bin/oso-state}` is instead duplicated verbatim at SIX hook call sites (`approve-plan-token.sh` ×2, `auto-continue.sh`, `capture-plan-approval.sh`, `warn-stale-state.sh`, `publish-subagent-handoff.sh`). `plugin/git-hooks/pre-commit` never calls the CLI at all — it sources `lib.sh` and greps the state file with `state_says` at `:18-27` — so it does not belong among the MIGRATE targets; the MIGRATE slice points the six hook call sites, `persist-state-bin.sh`, `opencode/plugin/oso/plan-state.ts` and the installers at `node <dist>/oso-state.js`. Only 5 of 11 hooks spawn the binary.
- C1-D4: `npm run build` produces `plugin/dist/oso-state.js` (esbuild, `--platform=node --format=esm --bundle`), and `npm run check` diffs it. `pr.yml` gains `npm ci`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run check` on ubuntu AND windows from this child on.

**Proposed slices.**
- S1 EXPAND — workspace, tsconfig strict, esbuild, `node --test`, `pr.yml` steps, an empty `core` that builds; `oso-state` CLI skeleton printing the same usage text. Verify: `npm run typecheck && npm test && npm run check` green on ubuntu and windows; failing-check: `core/test/bin/usage.test.ts` asserts the usage text equals the bash's. Depends-on: none.
- S2 EXPAND (opus) — the parity fixtures for every verb, extracted from the suite. Verify: `node --test core/test/parity.test.ts` runs ≥ 120 fixtures against the bash and all pass (the bash is the oracle at this point); failing-check: the fixture runner fails on a fixture whose `state_after` is unreachable. Depends-on: S1.
  - **Amended 2026-08-26.** The `≥ 120 fixtures` Verify is withdrawn as unreachable from the named source (C1-D2's amendment measures 40–60); it becomes: every fixture reading yields, count recorded.
- S3 EXPAND — `store.ts`, `schema.ts`, `transitions.ts`, `cli.ts`; parity green against the TS bundle. Verify: parity passes against both binaries; `StateInvariantError` on `mode=none active_slice=3`; failing-check: a transitions test that fails when `closeSlice` omits `auto_wait=none`. Depends-on: S2.
- S4 MIGRATE — every caller on the bundle (C1-D3). Verify: `bash tests/hooks-test.sh` green with `OSO_STATE_BIN` pointing at the bundle; `cd opencode && npm test` green; failing-check: a hooks-test case that runs the suite's `persist-state-bin` path and asserts the published path ends in `oso-state.js`. Depends-on: S3.
  - **Amended 2026-08-26.** S4 now carries the change's PARITY PROOF, not only its migration: `bash tests/hooks-test.sh` green with `OSO_STATE_BIN` pointed at the bundle is what establishes that the TypeScript and the bash are the same program. This is the stronger oracle — those assertions were written before the port existed and exercise the CLI through ~170 scaffolding invocations plus the ~51 direct ones (C1-D2's amendment), so they are independent of the ported artifact in a way fixtures derived from it could never be.
- S5 CONTRACT — delete `plugin/bin/oso-state` and its bash-only tests; `rg -n 'bin/oso-state' --glob '!docs/decisions/**' --glob '!docs/rewrite/**'` returns nothing. Depends-on: S4.
- Exit: `npm test` ≤ 30 s; `plugin/dist/oso-state.js` committed and `npm run check` green; hooks-test green; the two new verbs exist.

### C2 — `rewrite-gates`: the thirteen gates in TypeScript, three hosts on one implementation

**Intent.** Port every gate to `core/src/gates/*` as pure functions behind one entry point, switch Claude and Codex to spawning the bundle and OpenCode to importing the core, fix the three unattended-rail defects in code, and delete every gate script, `lib.sh`, `lexer.sh`, the route table and their tests.

**Decisions.**
- C2-D1: `core/src/hosts/envelope.ts` types the hook input (`session_id`, `cwd`, `tool_name`, `tool_input`, `hook_event_name`, …) and the output (`Deny {message}`, `Allow`, `Context {additionalContext}`, `Push {reason}`); `claude.ts` and `codex.ts` serialise them to what each host accepts; the OpenCode adapter consumes the verdict object directly.
- C2-D2: `core/src/shell/lexer.ts` ports `plugin/hooks/lexer.sh` record for record (`>` command word, `.` argument, `<` stdin text); its tests are the lexer cases of the suite as fixtures plus every shape named in the option-arity defect class (git's value-taking options, wrapper prefixes, read-only markers, interpreter payloads, `bash -cO extglob`). No third-party shell parser: the gate must answer the same question the bash answers, and a library's grammar is a second source of truth.
- C2-D3: `autocontinue.ts` fixes defects 1–3: (1) the disarm is `transitions.closeSlice` (C1) so no close can omit it; (2) the expiry is reachable without a future Stop — the SessionStart `stale` gate and the OpenCode `session.idle` path evaluate the same `waitExpired(now)` function and report it, and the Stop net treats an expired mark as no mark; (3) the sidecar is keyed on the run (G7). Defect 4 (compaction) is out of scope: it is an experiment, not a port, and needs 1–3 first.
- C2-D4: parity fixtures for gates extend the C1 shape with `hook_event`, `stdin` (the envelope), `expect.exit`, `expect.stdout`, `expect.stderr`; every `run_hook` / `assert_*` case of the suite that exercises a gate becomes one fixture (~5,000 lines of bash → fixtures + `core/test/gates/*.test.ts`). The bash gate is the oracle until the MIGRATE slice.
- C2-D5: `routes.ts` is the table; `render.ts` renders `plugin/hooks/hooks.json` (exec form, `if` on the commit gate) and `codex/hooks/hooks.json` (shell form); `opencode/hooks/routes.ts` is no longer generated — the OpenCode adapter imports `routes.ts`. `tools/hook-gates.txt` and `tools/render-hooks-json.sh` are deleted by the CONTRACT slice.
- C2-D6: the OpenCode plugin (`opencode/plugin/oso-code.ts` and `oso/*.ts`) keeps its host-specific parts — `oso_plan_approve`, `oso_plan_cancel`, `oso_wave` tools, the workspace adapter, identity, trace, lifecycle wiring — and DELETES `unattended-run.ts`, `approval.ts`'s duplicated approval logic, `gates.ts`'s spawn path and `plan-state.ts`'s spawn, calling `@oso-code/core` instead. Its bundle `opencode/dist/oso-code.js` inlines the core.
  - **C2-D6 amended 2026-08-28 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Decision 10, C2-S5).** `unattended-run.ts` is **host-parameterised, not deleted as duplicate**, and the line falls here: the DECISION is one module in core — push count, cap, milestone-progress reset, expiry through one `waitExpired(now)`, the sidecar — while the delegation MODEL and the continuation ORDER TEXT are host facts the adapter supplies. `core/src/gates/autocontinue.ts` takes a host profile `{ order, delegationsReturnInTurn, sidecarPath }`: `NOTIFICATION_RESUMED_HOST` carries Claude's exact `do NOT relaunch it` order, pinned byte-identical by `tests/hooks-test.sh`'s auto-continue assertions and the `core/test/fixtures/gates/auto-continue-*` fixtures; `DELEGATIONS_RETURN_IN_TURN_HOST` carries OpenCode's, whose order names the report the launch itself returned, and whose `delegationsReturnInTurn: true` makes the in-flight hold branch unreachable there by construction — a delegation on that host returns inside the turn that launched it, so an ended turn has none in flight. `session.idle` then evaluates the same predicate as the Stop net and `stale.ts` without ever posting Claude's order into an OpenCode session, which is the stall diagnosed in engram `oso/auto-wait-stall-field-failure/diagnosis`. The sidecar is keyed on the RUN on both hosts (G7 as amended); OpenCode's old `label=` mark at `<journalStem>.waiting` is ABANDONED rather than migrated — a run in flight across this upgrade is out of scope by DECISION 3 item 6(c), and on a host whose delegations return in-turn a lost mark age costs at most one push and never a stall — and `teardown` removes a stale old-path file when it meets one. The `auto_change` marker is written by the FLOW through `oso-state` on every host and never by the plugin, so it is set on OpenCode; an OpenCode run without it is a flow defect for the sweep, never a reason to key the mark differently. The rest of C2-D6 stands: `approval.ts`'s duplicated approval logic, `gates.ts`'s spawn path and `plan-state.ts`'s spawn are deleted, `opencode/plugin/oso/plan.ts` with them, and the adapter calls `@oso-code/core`.
- C2-D7: the Stop push on Claude is measured on the pinned client in S3: the adapter emits `shouldContinue: true` (documented) and the fixture asserts the client continued; if the pinned client only honours the older `decision: block`, the adapter emits both fields and the ledger records it as delegated with the measurement.

**Added 2026-08-27 (C2's own decision rounds; the entries marked DELEGATED were answered by the operator's delegate, Fable 9eab15).**
- **C2-D8.** S0 is a REPAIR slice carrying four repairs plus these in-branch document amendments. Its Verify is `pr.yml` green on BOTH legs for its own commit, observed by the orchestrator with `gh run view`; no other slice starts before it. The four: the `tsc` route-table check runs against a copy of the file outside any tree holding a `tsconfig.json`, never `--ignoreConfig` (a TypeScript 7-only flag that would break a 5.x compiler), and carries its own failing-check; the parity runner normalizes path separators before comparing, leaving `core/src/state/store.ts` untouched because `path.join` emitting a backslash on win32 is correct for a native program; the chmod-unreadable port test declares its platform assumption in the file and does not run on win32; and C2-D13's sandbox preload.
- **C2-D9.** `isPrivateRegularFile` (`core/src/state/store.ts:153-156`) degrades to `isReadableRegularFile` when `process.platform === "win32"`, in that one function; `core/src/state/plan.ts`'s eight call sites are untouched. Basis: on win32 libuv synthesises `0o666` for any writable file and `fs.chmod` honours only the write bit, so the guard never protected anything there — privacy on Windows is the ACL inherited from the profile. The explicit restrictive ACL is a presence-phase item, never a slice.
- **C2-D10.** The decision-citation rule is extended to `core/src` with `//` syntax. It is a PROHIBITION — no executable's comment may cite a decision id — consistent with the zero-inline-comment rule, and never an instruction to write citations. Its positive fixture is a TEST FIXTURE the rule must flag, never shipped source.
  - **Amended 2026-08-29 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable 9eab15, Decision 15d). The prohibition is ENFORCED IN THIS CHILD, as a `core/test/structure/` test, rather than travelling to C5.** Until it exists nothing holds the rule: `tests/plugin-lint.sh`'s `typescript_comment_citations()` greps `opencode/plugin` alone, and the four TypeScript trees' present compliance — zero comments carrying a decision id — is the "I found no problems" that has not looked. The test scans `core/src`, `core/scripts`, `core/test` and `opencode/plugin` for comment lines of the two shapes the linter already recognises (`//`-led and `*`-led block lines) carrying a decision id, floors the file count with the shared measurability guard, and is shown RED against a positive fixture the scanner is run over — D10's letter, a temp file, never shipped source. **Scope is D10's letter and no wider: a CITATION scanner, never a general no-comment scanner**, which would need a tokenizer to avoid the regex-literal false positives already in the tree; the zero-inline-comment rule stays the operator's global rule, held in review. Recorded beside C5-S5: it RETIRES `typescript_comment_citations()` in favour of this test, so one enforcement remains rather than two.
- **C2-D11.** `core/`'s entry points are declared before any fallow verdict is read; until fallow's attribution counters return a non-zero number, "0 introduced" is not accepted as evidence in any slice.
  - **Amended 2026-08-29 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable 9eab15, Decision 15, seventh item). The declaration LANDS IN C2'S CLOSE, in the tree, because this sentence is false until the config exists.** The close sweep found no fallow config anywhere in the repository, and the judge had to declare the entry points OUT of tree to read a verdict at all. `.fallowrc.json` now declares `core/src/bin/{gate,precommit,oso-state}.ts`, `core/src/index.ts` and `opencode/plugin/oso-code.ts`; the format was read from fallow 3.14.0's own shipped `README.md` and `schema.json` rather than guessed. **The config's proof is the entry-point count: `fallow list --entry-points` goes 5 to 10, the five declared appearing as `manual entry`** — and the re-judge then read a non-zero `dead_code_introduced`, which is what this decision was waiting for. A dead-code issue count is deliberately NOT recorded here: the counts measured before the close's two fix rounds describe a tree that no longer exists, and a number kept without its tree is the stale-record class this child keeps correcting. Its refusal to accept fallow's all-zero attribution as evidence was this decision applied correctly, and that refusal is what surfaced the missing config.
- **C2-D12. Keeping the spawn alive because D22's sentence named it would leave the OpenCode plugin with two event-write paths, which is the duplication C2-D6 exists to remove; the only spawn left in that plugin is `git` (`lifecycle.ts`). `statebin.ts` still appends `export OSO_STATE_BIN=…`, so D22's other half stands untouched. **Named residual:** `OSO_STATE_BIN` no longer steers OpenCode traces — the root resolves from the OpenCode process env (`OSO_STATE_DIR` / `HOME` / `USERPROFILE`), the same env `shell.env` publishes to the binary's subprocesses, and no workflow steering traces to another binary exists.
- **C2-D23.** Cold start is measured on BOTH matrix platforms, and no explicit `timeout` is declared in the rendered manifests. A `timeout` on a DENY gate would convert a hang into a non-2 failure, which the host treats as not blocking — fail-open on the production boundary. The 600-second default hang is a named residual.
- **C2-D26. DELEGATED 2026-08-29 (operator's delegate, Fable 9eab15, Decision 14, C2-S6). The cold-start bar asserts the OVERHEAD the bundle adds over a bare-node control, never the absolute.** `pr.yml` run 33241462916 on `5321a92` was green on ubuntu-latest and red on windows-latest with `not ok 1 - the mean of 20 spawns, 156.3 ms here, is at most 100 ms`, while the same file's own guard read `ok 11 - 20 of 20 cold start(s) of gate.js ran the gate to its unarmed verdict`. The gate RUNS on Windows twenty times out of twenty; only the number was red. Four options were weighed and three lost by name:
  - **Ruled — assert `bundleMean − baselineMean` against `max(40 ms, 1.5 × the measured baseline)`, the baseline being an empty ESM module beside a copy of `plugin/dist/package.json`, spawned interleaved one-for-one with the bundle in the same loop.** Process creation is node's own cost and every hook pays it whatever ships, so an absolute bar on the TOTAL asserts what the port does not control, and on windows-latest it is unsatisfiable by ANY node-based gate rather than by this one. The measured ratio held at 0.57–0.82 across a 2.3× swing in absolute cost, and that invariance is what the derived shape stands on. This is G14's amendment shape (Decision 12) read a second time: a bar the roadmap's own work moved the world out from under.
  - **Rejected — a flat ~250 ms.** One constant and nothing to explain, but it blesses every regression under the ceiling and the ceiling comes from one observation on one runner.
  - **Rejected — platform constants, 100 POSIX / 250 win32.** Declares the platform assumption, which this roadmap's own rule asks for, but carries the blessing problem twice and guesses two numbers from one observation each.
  - **Rejected — skip on win32.** Refused outright: it contradicts C2-D23, which requires cold start measured on BOTH matrix platforms, and a skip is the check saying "I did not look" — the defect class this roadmap has met thirteen times, once already as a win32 skip whose stated reason its own slice's test refuted.

  **The flat bar was also flaky on Linux, not merely unsatisfiable on Windows:** the same commit measured 79.0 ms pre-slice and 115.4 ms in-suite on the operator's machine, a 46 % swing either side of the 100 ms line. So this amendment removes a defect on both legs. **FRACTION 1.5 and FLOOR 40 ms ship as measured on Linux and windows-latest measures the ratio nobody here can** — a red there returns that runner's baseline, bundle mean, overhead and derived budget in one line, which is a measurement rather than a gamble; tightening toward 1.0 was rejected as sitting inside the measured noise band, trading a loose bar for a flaky one on the PR gate of an unattended run. The tightening is a named residual for C4 (C4-D3). **Operator-visible, and a property of the platform rather than a debt of the port:** a bare node spawn plus the gate is ~156 ms on windows-latest, so a 100 ms hook budget is not reachable there by a node-based gate at all. Decision 8's sentence "already bounded by G4 at ≤ 100 ms" is a record of what was true when written and stands.
- **C2-D27. DELEGATED 2026-08-29 (operator's delegate, Fable 9eab15, Decisions 19 and 20). THREE FAIL-OPENS ON THE PRODUCTION DENY RAIL, FOUND BY THE CLOSE'S SECURITY REVIEW AND FIXED.** Each was a measured divergence from G5's "behaviour unchanged" against the bash the operator actually runs, so fixing them RESTORES the port's contract under G5 and C2-D2 rather than adding scope. No security residual was accepted.
  - **F1, `core/src/shell/ere.ts`. An untranslatable pattern must never be the reason a production deploy is allowed.** The range reader accepted a bracket range only when both endpoints sat in the same ASCII block, and every other range fell to the constant that means "grep rejects this pattern" — correct only for patterns grep exits 2 on, and applied here to patterns grep ACCEPTS AND MATCHES. The deny rail reads a non-match as not-denied, so an operator's deny pattern silently stopped biting: `[!-~]+ --prod`, `[A-z]+ --prod` and `ship[ -/]*it` all measured bash=DENY, port=allow. **Two-part fix, and the second matters more.** (a) A range is accepted whenever both endpoints are ASCII and `low ≤ high` by code point — under `LC_ALL=C`, which `plugin/hooks/lib.sh` pins at `:49,:410,:420,:436`, a code-point range IS grep's semantics, and the same-block table was a precaution for a locale the port does not run in. (b) The reader's single failure return is SPLIT: "grep itself rejects this" stays match-nothing, while "grep would accept this but the reader cannot translate it" becomes UNTRANSLATABLE, which the deny rail treats as a MATCH with a visible reason naming the pattern and a `deploy-deny-pattern-untranslatable` event — silence in either direction would be its own defect. Each of the three untranslatable sites was assigned by direct grep probe rather than by guess.
  - **F2, same file. The fix is WIDER than the quantifier bullets and the ledger says so rather than recording only the bullets.** `withDemotedQuantifiers` is DELETED and word-edge atoms route through `withQuantifiers`, the path that already handled `^`, `$`, `` \` `` and `\'` correctly. The wider shape was forced by the ruled table correction: the interval branch re-read the pattern from `cursor + 1`, so `\b{2}$` compiled as `\b2}$` and the pinned table recorded **the exact inverse of grep** for three atoms. GNU applies the interval to the assertion (`\b{2}$ ≡ \b$`). One quantifier path instead of two; `AtomWidth` collapses to two variants. The table is rebuilt at 104 rows over 7 commands with **every `denies` list emitted by `/usr/bin/grep -E`, never by the reader** — a table that pins the port's own answer proves nothing.
  - **F3, `core/src/hosts/envelope.ts`. A differential the port INTRODUCED.** The bash reader preferred `jq` whenever the platform had it and fell back to a regex only when it was absent; the port kept only the fallback, so a field was read by BYTE POSITION and a decoy key nested ahead of the real one shadowed it — the gate lexing `echo benign` while the host executes `vercel deploy --prod`. Fixed by parsing structurally and replicating jq's parent-before-child search, with the regex kept only for a payload that does not parse. **All 509 gate fixtures were checked for a golden encoding the byte-first answer: zero.**
  - **Measured, with the corpus stated, because that is now this roadmap's bar for any clean verdict:** under-denies **52,909 → 12** over 343,980 grep-differential pairs (7,644 patterns × 45 subjects, cross-block ranges the bulk of the range family by construction); the quantified-zero-width family **2,712 → 12**; F3 **254 → 0** over 6,336 comparisons against real `jq` running the bash reader's own program.
  - **A STANDING RULE FOR EVERY JUDGE AND SCANNER OF THIS ROADMAP, banked here because it outlives this child: a clean or no-findings verdict is NOT EVIDENCE unless it reports its CORPUS** — what it generated, how many, and which construct classes it included or excluded. A scanner returned NO FINDINGS on this same file over ~45,000 pairs while F1 was live, because its generator never emitted cross-block ranges; the reviewer's own numbers show why, 456 under-denying patterns dropping to 59 when those atoms are removed from a generator. This is the `provedSomething` floor applied to the security lane.
  - **Residuals, disposed so none is silently swept.** (1) The non-ASCII word-character divergence — the 12 remaining under-denies plus 24 over — is a deny-boundary fail-open entangled with locale semantics, so it is **QUEUED FOR THE OPERATOR, JOINED TO THE `LC_ALL` ITEM**, and carried in PR #5's body. It is safe to leave open through a mid-chain close because a stacked DRAFT PR ships nothing until the operator merges the chain; **it must be fixed or explicitly accepted before that merge.** (2) A `new RegExp` compile failure is classified as grep-rejects, with zero live instances today — named residual to C3 **with its completion shape**, so C3 does not rediscover it: a compile failure on a pattern the reader did not classify as a grep-reject construct is a reader bug and must be UNTRANSLATABLE, completing F1(b) over the compiler level where it today covers only the reader level. (3) `core/src/state/cli.ts:187-190` takes the `capture-plan` digest from argv and the document from stdin, unbound — routed to C3 beside the digest-contract residual as its second named caller. There is no live bypass: an approval binds to the exact presented content through the snapshot equality under `withLock` plus the byte-for-byte `current.md` compare, whatever the digest hashes, and planting a snapshot through that path is inert without an operator-gated approval for that digest. The three callers do not even agree on what the digest hashes, so binding it now would decide an unmeasured contract by implication and make `planstop`'s own digest non-conformant. (4) On unparseable JSON the port falls back to the regex where jq-present bash returned empty — a **deliberate divergence in the SAFE direction**: the port may scrape a command and DENY where that bash allowed, which is over-deny on a deny boundary, and it matches jq-ABSENT bash exactly. Strict jq-present parity would be the fail-OPEN choice and is refused.
- **C2-D28. DELEGATED 2026-08-29 (operator's delegate, Fable 9eab15, Decision 21). THE SECURITY EXIT BAR FOR A BEHAVIOUR-PRESERVING PORT, and it is what makes this close terminable at all.** A port that preserves behaviour CANNOT be gated on "zero fail-opens": the bash it ports HAS fail-opens, and C2-D2 mandates reproducing them record for record. **The bar is ZERO FAIL-OPENS THE PORT INTRODUCED, with every ported or pre-existing fail-open MEASURED — bash against port, token for token or pair-differential — and QUEUED for the operator.** A faithful-port fail-open is therefore never a blocker of this close and never a delegate's to fix: fixing one is a deliberate security divergence from bash, which is the operator's on the never-solo list. Without this bar every review round would keep finding pre-existing bash defects the port faithfully carried, each looking like a blocker, and the close could never end. **The ported-versus-introduced measurement is ratified as METHOD, not just as this round's evidence:** run the frozen bash and the port over the same inputs and compare outputs before classifying anything a review reports. A reviewer's "this file is entirely new in the range" is a claim about the FILE and never about the BEHAVIOUR.
  - **What that bar sorted, on the day it was written.** The re-review reported seven. **One was ours** — a stack-overflow fail-open the F3 fix introduced, fixed below. **One was the half-swept remainder of a class already granted** — fixed below. **Five were faithful ports**, measured and queued: three lexer holes and two locale-family divergences.
  - **THE REGRESSION, and it is recorded because a round that introduces one should say so.** The F3 fix replaced an iterative field reader with a mutually recursive walk carrying no depth bound, and `core/src/bin/gate.ts` built the envelope as an ARGUMENT to `runGate`, outside `runWith`'s `try/catch`. A payload nested past ~3,050 levels threw uncaught: **exit 1 with empty stdout, where the blocking contract is exit 2** — so one payload disarmed all four PreToolUse rails at once, measured on the shipped bundle. Fixed in two parts, and the second is the one that generalises: the walk became an explicit ITERATIVE traversal preserving jq's parent-before-child order and the direct-hit-before-recursion property — no depth cap, no two-regime split, no threshold left to bisect, verified reading correctly at depth 1,000,000 — and **the entry point now catches its own construction**, emitting the exit-2 fail-closed transport on ANY throw, so the next throw nobody has met also fails closed. That second part is F1(b)'s shape one layer out. It also closes the `Object.values` ordering divergence by construction: the direct-hit invariant that makes it inert is now PINNED BY TEST rather than observed.
  - **The bracket remainder.** `readBracket` consumed a leading `]` as a finished member, so `[]-a]` read as a member set where grep reads a RANGE — ASCII, **locale-independent**, 97 of 470 spellings failing open, with the live bypass proved end to end through the real gate. Fixed by handing that position to the range reader. Differential, corpus stated: leading-`]` **97 → 0** fail-open and 181 → 0 over-deny across 44,180 pairs; a 752-spelling control 1 → 0; a 354,192-pair wide-bracket regression sweep 7 → 0.
  - **QUEUED FOR THE OPERATOR, and the first item is the one that matters most in this whole child.** Three lexer holes — `vercel $'--prod'`, `git $'commit' -m x`, `git $'push' origin main` — are FAITHFUL PORTS, proved by running `plugin/hooks/lexer.sh` and `core/src/shell/lexer.ts` over the same lines and comparing token streams: identical, token for token. **They are LIVE IN THE SHIPPED 0.25.0 HARNESS TODAY** — the production deny rail, the commit gate and the run-branch rail all bypassable — and **SILENT, logging zero events**, which makes them strictly worse than C2-D17's two pinned holes. The recommendation put to the operator, and it is theirs alone to take: **treat all five as ONE CLASS and decide whether the rewrite closes them as a DELIBERATE, DOCUMENTED security divergence from bash**, because faithfully porting a live production bypass is a contradiction the port cannot resolve on its own, and the natural home is C3's lexer work where the divergence can be stated as intent rather than smuggled as a port. Also queued: the equivalence-class expansion (`[[=a=]]`, which agrees with production bash under a UTF-8 login locale and diverges only under C) and the non-ASCII word-character divergence — **whose surface is corrected here from the 12 under-denies first recorded to 86 pattern families**, since routing word-edge atoms through the shared quantifier path UN-MASKED the pre-existing defect rather than introducing it. Both join the `LC_ALL` decision, which is one question with three parts rather than three questions.
- **C2-D1 amended.** The verdict union carries SIX variants, not four: `Deny{message}`, `Allow`, `Context{additionalContext}`, `Push{reason}`, plus `GateError{subject}` for the stderr-and-exit-2 transport `block_with_gate_error` implements, plus `NoVerdict` for `statebin`, `teardown` and `handoff`, which produce none. Folding exit 2 into `Deny` would change both the stream and the exit code, which G5 forbids.
- **C2-D2 amended.** The lexer's tests are the commit gate's sixteen heredoc tables, which pin its shapes, PLUS direct PORT TESTS — because the lexer has no direct tests today and its only current basis is a gate that folds the `unread` verdict into allow, so that signal would otherwise be exercised only through a gate that ignores it.
- **C2-D4 amended.** Fixtures are READ from the suite's assertions and committed, never generated by running the bash at test time, which would be tautological and would put a Git Bash dependency on the Windows leg. S1 and S3 must enumerate and RECORD the case counts for `block-prod-deploy.sh` and `auto-continue.sh`, the two the exploration could not enumerate; a count recorded, never pinned to a floor. A bounded set of SPAWN fixtures crosses the real process boundary, one per transport shape per host serialiser, so the serialisation layer is not the one layer the parity net never touches.

The rendered exec shape is `{"type":"command","command":"node","args":["${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/dist/gate.js","<gate>"]}` — `args`, never an argv-valued `command`, which the pinned client rejects with `expected string, received array`.

S1's Verify gains a structural test asserting that `routes.ts`'s rows equal `tools/hook-gates.txt`'s parsed rows while both exist, deleted by the slice that deletes the `.txt` under G14, so the two copies cannot drift during the window.

Depends-on: S1, S2, S3.

### C3 — `rewrite-installer`: one `oso` CLI for install, verify, repair and purge on every host

**Intent.** Replace `bootstrap/install*.sh`, `verify*.sh`, `purge*.sh`, `repair*.sh`, `bootstrap/lib/*` and `install.ps1`'s body with `core/src/install/*` behind `node bootstrap/oso.js <install|verify|repair|purge> --host <claude|codex|opencode> [--yes]`, tested in-process against a fixture HOME, and delete `tests/hooks-test.sh`. (`[--ci]` struck 2026-08-30 per the C3-D2 amendment below — measured, it never existed in the tree.)

**Decisions.**
- C3-D1: host adapters own their config surface: Claude — `settings.json` hooks/permissions region, `CLAUDE.md` global source, the marketplace registration, MCP entries (engram, context7, fallow), Impeccable mount; Codex — the managed TOML region of `config.toml` (`smol-toml` parse → edit → stringify, preserving the unmanaged part byte for byte, which is what `toml-regions.awk` guarantees today and the tests must keep asserting), agents, skills, hooks copy, trust hashes; OpenCode — `opencode.json` managed keys (merge, never overwrite), plugin copy, skills, agents. The backup, transaction and rollback semantics of today's installers are kept as one shared module.
  - **C3-D1 amended 2026-08-30 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable 9eab15, model Claude Fable 5, C3-S1).** The phrase "settings.json hooks/permissions region" above is struck — no such region exists. Measured against a fixture HOME (not read off the source), Claude's ownership over `~/.claude` is six distinct mechanisms, never one region:
    1. `env.OSO_STATE_BIN` — key-wise **set** (`bootstrap/install.sh` `store_client_env`, called from `publish_state_bin_path`).
    2. `env.CLAUDE_CODE_GIT_BASH_PATH` — key-wise **set**, the same `store_client_env`, called from `publish_git_bash_path`.
    3. `outputStyle` — **set**, guarded upstream to act only when the existing value is absent, `Gentleman` or `Oso` (`bootstrap/install.sh` `ensure_output_style`; the write itself is `rewrite_settings_json(settings, '.outputStyle = "Oso"')`).
    4. `.hooks[]` — **remove-only**: a jq filter drops array entries whose `.hooks[].command` matches a legacy pattern and prunes any event key left with an empty array; it never adds or edits an entry (`bootstrap/install.sh` `remove_legacy_settings_entries`).
    5. `CLAUDE.md`'s `<!-- oso-code:start -->`/`<!-- oso-code:end -->` marker region — **region-rebuild**: everything outside the markers is preserved in its original relative order, the region itself is dropped and a freshly rendered one is appended at the file's end — not at its original position (`bootstrap/install.sh` `merge_global_claude_md`).
    6. MCP wiring (engram, context7, fallow) — **not a `settings.json` key at all**: mediated through the `claude` CLI (`claude plugin install`, `claude mcp add`), which writes `~/.claude.json`'s `.mcpServers` key, a file `install.sh` never touches directly; `claude mcp add` measured **insert-if-missing** — it refuses to overwrite an existing entry, matching `wire_fallow`'s own "already wired" vs. "mismatched, fail" branches (`bootstrap/install.sh` `wire_fallow`).

    **What was measured, and how:** rows 1–5 by sourcing `bootstrap/install.sh` under a `mktemp -d` HOME and calling `store_client_env`, `ensure_output_style`, `remove_legacy_settings_entries` and `merge_global_claude_md` directly, reading the written `settings.json`/`CLAUDE.md` before and after each call; row 6 by running `claude mcp add --scope user <key> -- true` under a fixture HOME and confirming both which file changed (`~/.claude.json`, `~/.claude/settings.json` left untouched) and that a second `claude mcp add` with a different command for the same key left the first entry unmodified. The Codex and OpenCode rows of the table below are **S3's and S4's to measure**, not read off their bash the way this amendment refuses to.

    The Codex mechanism above also contradicts itself, corrected here: `smol-toml` parse → edit → stringify **cannot** preserve the unmanaged part of `config.toml` byte for byte — a round trip through any TOML library drops comments and reformats whitespace, which is exactly what `toml-regions.awk` exists to avoid. The managed region is split and rebuilt at the **text** level, a port of `toml-regions.awk`'s marker-scanning state machine (`bootstrap/lib/toml-regions.awk`); `smol-toml` **parses only** — it validates the file, reads keys, and detects oso-owned keys living outside the managed region — and it never re-emits the operator's file.

    OpenCode's "merge, never overwrite" is imprecise the same way; measured from `bootstrap/install-opencode.sh` lines 560-655 at `2bc77ad`: owned `permission` keys are **overwritten** (`permission.update(OWNED_PERMISSION_VALUES)`); owned `mcp` servers are **insert-if-missing** (`servers.setdefault(name, declaration)`); every other key, and every non-owned `permission`/`mcp` entry, is **preserved** verbatim (`preserved` accumulation at five sites, `:615`, `:625`, `:632`, `:637`, `:645`).

    **Both citations on the line above were corrected 2026-08-31 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Decision 33, c3-s4-amend-1), and this gloss covers three of six policies rather than all of them.** The span was `:596-651`, which opened at `owned_object`'s `def` — after the constant block the owned sets live in — and closed at `handle.write("\n")`, before the preserved-key ledger is written at all; the corpus is the whole heredoc, `<<'PY'` at `:559` through `PY` at `:656`. The accumulation list was `:615,625,645`, three of the five sites: it dropped `:632` (`permission.skill.*`) and `:637` (`permission.task.*`), so an operator's non-owned skill mode and task pattern read as unpreserved from this record while the script preserves both. **The table below now carries all six policies and states why there is no seventh.**

    **The ownership table this decision now requires** — one row per (host, file, key-or-region, write policy from the closed set {set, remove-only, region-rebuild, insert-if-missing, overwrite, preserve, create-if-absent}), cited by path and verbatim symbol name.

    **`create-if-absent` joins the closed set 2026-08-31 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Decision 33, c3-s4-amend-1), as the set's seventh shape and OpenCode's fourth.** It creates an EMPTY container when the key is absent, REFUSES when the key is present carrying the wrong type, and never writes a value of its own into it — which is none of the six already here: `insert-if-missing` inserts an OWNED value, `preserve` writes nothing at all, and `overwrite` replaces whatever it finds. OpenCode's `plugin` key is its only member. **Decision 24c restated here, because the table is where it binds:** each row's policy is proven by a fixture-HOME case BEFORE the port reproduces it, so a row is a measurement the port is held to rather than a description read off the port after the fact.

    | host | file | key or region | write policy | win32 behaviour | cited by |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | Claude | `settings.json` | `env.OSO_STATE_BIN` | set | same absolute path, but the file it names is a `#!/usr/bin/env node` script no win32 reader starts directly — the verifier's round-trip probe spawns it as `process.execPath <script>` (`runOsoStateProbe`); measured FAILED on `windows-latest` at `1ccb71d` on a correct install, fixed in C3-S2c | `bootstrap/install.sh` `store_client_env` — **measured, C3-S1** |
    | Claude | `settings.json` | `env.CLAUDE_CODE_GIT_BASH_PATH` | set | the only platform-guarded row — written on win32 alone (`publishGitBashPath` returns undefined off it), so off-win32 the key is never read, written or removed by install or repair | `bootstrap/install.sh` `store_client_env` — **measured, C3-S1** |
    | Claude | `settings.json` | `outputStyle` | set | no divergence — a JSON value written through `writeJsonFile`, which reads and emits the same bytes on either platform | `bootstrap/install.sh` `ensure_output_style` — **measured, C3-S1** |
    | Claude | `settings.json` | `.hooks[]` (legacy-command entries) | remove-only | no divergence — the filter matches on the command string alone, never on a path separator | `bootstrap/install.sh` `remove_legacy_settings_entries` — **measured, C3-S1** |
    | Claude | `CLAUDE.md` | `MARKER_START`/`MARKER_END` region | region-rebuild | the rebuild NORMALISES line endings: `withoutMarkerRegion` folds `\r\n` and bare `\r` to `\n`, so an operator's CRLF file comes back LF outside the markers too — pinned by `claude.test.ts`'s "CRLF-normalises on strip", which runs on both legs | `bootstrap/install.sh` `merge_global_claude_md` — **measured, C3-S1** |

`renderCodexManagedConfig` composes with `path.posix.join` throughout and is byte-identical for the same two path strings on any platform, so the region rebuild adds no divergence of its own — the host paths it is HANDED do. **Re-measured for this record, and now enumerated by `core/test/install/codex.test.ts` over the whole nine-file import closure of the two renderers rather than over `core/src/install/codex.ts` alone: THREE sites compose host paths with native `path.join` and reach `config.toml` bytes, five expressions across two files**, so the earlier "confined to `codexPathsFor`" reads wider than it measures. `codexPathsFor` (`core/src/install/codex.ts`, by name) yields the `codexHome` and `runtimeRoot` that `renderCodexManagedConfig` then tails with `/`-separated segments: on a native win32 host the managed region carries the `config.toml` bytes `OSO_STATE_BIN = "C:\\Users\\operator\\.local\\share\\oso-code\\runtime/bin/oso-state"` (backslash-escaped by `tomlQuote`, and mixed-separator because the posix tail joins onto a native root) where bash's Git Bash run renders `/c/Users/operator/.local/share/oso-code/runtime/bin/oso-state`. `normalizeEngramPointers` (`core/src/install/codex.ts`, by name) composes the two engram pointer VALUES the same way, and the REPAIR rail writes them verbatim as the `model_instructions_file` and `experimental_compact_prompt_file` rows — measured on the linux rails over a fixture HOME: `installCodex` exit 0, then `repairCodex` exit 0 reporting `engram pointers: OK — moved above the managed region`, with both rows rewritten from the native join. The third site is `firstExecutableOnPath` (`core/src/install/verify-claude.ts`, by name), reached through `resolveFallowMcpCommand`'s PATH branch (its `firstOnPath(FALLOW_FALLBACK_COMMAND)` arm in `core/src/install/codex-config.ts`), injected at the `resolveFallowMcpCommand` call inside `resolveFallowCommandFor` (`core/src/install/codex.ts`) on the install rail (`writeCodexInstall`) and the repair rail (`repairCodex`), and written as the `[mcp_servers.fallow]` `command` row by `renderCodexManagedConfig` in `core/src/install/codex-config.ts` — measured over a win32-shaped PATH entry as the `config.toml` bytes `command = "C:\\Users\\operator\\AppData\\Roaming\\npm\\fallow-mcp"`, which `smol-toml` decodes back to the native path `C:\Users\operator\AppData\Roaming\npm\fallow-mcp` verbatim. **Judged a DIVERGENCE and not a defect**: the branch returns its value unnormalised where the sibling `npmPrefixOf` branch (in the same function) normalises, and `bootstrap/lib/codex-managed-config.sh` carries the same asymmetry for the same reason — the normalised value is then `/`-joined, where without the normalisation `C:/Users/operator/AppData/Roaming/npm/fallow-mcp.cmd` would render as the mixed `C:\Users\operator\AppData\Roaming\npm/fallow-mcp.cmd`, while the PATH branch is terminal and joins nothing onto its value. What diverges on win32 is the VALUE, the way the other two sites' does: Git Bash's `command -v` answers `/c/Users/...` where Node's native join answers `C:\Users\...`, and only the second is a path a native Codex spawns. A fourth native-join site, `wireGitCommitHook` (`core/src/install/codex.ts`, by name), diverges the same way but reaches `.git/config`'s `core.hooksPath`, never `config.toml`. **NOT MEASURED on a real win32 host**, named rather than assumed silently: no PR-gate test may spawn the Codex binary or touch the real HOME (G4), the bash oracle is guarded off win32 by `skipUnlessBashRunsTheInstallerPipeline`, and nightly's Windows leg still runs the bash installer | `bootstrap/lib/toml-regions.awk` → `core/src/install/toml-regions.ts` `runTomlRegion`, all 8 actions differentialled byte for byte over 57 committed `config.toml` shapes (570 cases, `core/test/fixtures/toml/region-differential.json`); the rebuild assembly measured against `write_config_region`'s own pipeline by `codex-config-oracle.ts` — **measured, C3-S3** |

Driven over a fixture HOME whose seed carries `permission.question = "deny"`: the bash renders `allow`, and so does the port | `bootstrap/install-opencode.sh` line 642 at `2bc77ad` `permission.update(OWNED_PERMISSION_VALUES)` — **confirmed against `mergeOpenCodeConfig`, C3-S4** |

So the Codex row's measured divergence is inherited literally, and nothing else across the six rows composes a host path. `context7` (a literal URL) and `engram` (a literal argv) compose none, and an operator's own `engram` declaration survives insert-if-missing unchanged | `bootstrap/install-opencode.sh` lines 646-647 at `2bc77ad` `servers.setdefault(name, declaration)` — **confirmed against `mergeOpenCodeConfig`, C3-S4** |

No value of oso's reaches it, so there is nothing for a platform to shape | `bootstrap/install-opencode.sh` lines 618-622 at `2bc77ad` — `config["plugin"] = []` when absent, `raise SystemExit` on a non-array, and the key is EXCLUDED from `preserved` at `:615` because it is neither preserved nor written — **confirmed against `mergeOpenCodeConfig`, C3-S4** |
