  - **C3-S4b record — the staging, host-spawn and artifact half, measured 2026-08-31 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, c3-s4b-apply-1).** Every figure below was produced by the drivers this slice runs rather than transcribed, and the five it contradicts are named as contradictions rather than quietly corrected.

    **Working verbs, and the throwing list is now EMPTY.** `install`, `verify` and `purge --host opencode` land COMPLETE beside S4's `repair`: install takes `--yes --no-impeccable --no-git-hook`, verify assembles all seventeen rows, purge takes `--yes`, `--dry-run`, `--keep-gentle-ai` and `--restore <dir>` with the seven ordered exclusions S4 tabled. **`VerbNotImplementedError` is DELETED rather than retargeted**, which is the consequence of ruling (3) reaching its own end: with every host × verb reaching its own command there is no verb left for it to name, and a class whose last caller is gone is dead code. A case asserts the CLI source carries no not-implemented string at all, so the four verb cases beside it cannot pass vacuously.

    **The install parity result, which is the strongest one this child has measured.** One seeded operator config and AGENTS.md copied into two fixture HOMEs, installed once by the bash and once by `installOpenCode`, compared as WHOLE TREES: **every file byte-identical**, with exactly two files needing the HOME spelled once — the backup manifest and the owner registry, both of which embed the HOME they were written under — and the backup directory's own timestamp folded. **94 files compared**, the whole fixture HOME rather than the config home alone. The operator's three preserved keys come back in the same order through both, both leave one install backup declaring the OpenCode format and label, and both leave a gate tree the published hashes cover completely. **Shown catching a difference rather than assumed**: the same comparison over the port tree with one entry dropped is not equal.

    **Purge and restore take Decision 30's condition 2 in full, and the restore round trip is what closes it.** One populated HOME — config, state, cache, binary and both gentle-ai homes, plus three project-level `opencode.json` files outside every target — built twice and purged through both. The purged HOMEs are identical file for file; both take the whole seeded tree with them; the three project configs are intact in both; and **each implementation then restores its own backup to the tree it started from, byte for byte, and the two restored trees are equal to each other**. `--keep-gentle-ai` leaves both gentle-ai homes standing through both; `--dry-run` removes nothing through both; an already-absent HOME is reported settled rather than purged through both. Nine refusals compared by exit through both: three customized-XDG guards and six project-config guards, each with the seeded tree asserted unchanged afterwards.

    **`verify` runs the loop TWICE and the artifact rows are read row for row.** Seven artifact rows — skills, agents, commands, plugin, engram, registry, published gate bytes — are read by `bootstrap/verify-opencode.sh`'s own status functions, sourced and called directly, and by the port, over one fixture HOME the bash installer staged; the shim-on-PATH and shim-absent runs are S4's and stand unchanged. **Twelve deliberately damaged copies of that HOME** drive every other verdict each row can reach through both implementations: `divergent`, `missing-shared-bodies`, `shared-differs`, `count:`, `entry-divergent`, `unbundled-sources:1`, `missing`, `missing: <target>` and `bad:` — including the leading space the bash's own `printf 'divergent:%s' "$divergent"` emits, which the port reproduces rather than tidies. The two rows that drive an installer of their own — `an install outside the named home is refused` and `isolated fixture install` — are driven through both as well.

    **The assembly, and the ROW the enumeration would otherwise have hidden.** `verifyOpenCode` emits all seventeen rows in the table's order, read back out of its own report by a case rather than asserted beside it, with every fixture row passing and the version row skipping on a PATH carrying no host binary. The three `repository` rows spawn `npx tsc --noEmit`, `node --test` and `bash -n` exactly where the bash does, and are the only rows whose verdict that case leaves free — because their subject is the repository rather than the fixture.

    **The shim's enumerated call list, measured again and narrowed to the drive that answers it.** S4's list is right and this half adds what an install drive alone proves: driving `bootstrap/install-opencode.sh` under a shim that logs every argv it is asked answers **exactly one call, `--version`**, at `bootstrap/install-opencode.sh` line 91 at `2bc77ad` reached from `check_baseline`; the port's own drive over the same shim answers **ZERO**, because it takes the version as an injected probe and spawns nothing. **A shim that refuses that one question takes the bash install red** — exit 1, `host baseline not met` — which is what makes the list the gate rather than a decoration. The case reads each entry's spawn site, defining file and caller out of the shell sources by content.

    **How the port stages a fixture install without manufacturing a shim, recorded as a divergence rather than left to be discovered.** `setup_local_fixture` writes a `#!/bin/sh` shim so the bash install's version gate passes; the port calls `installOpenCode` in process with the pinned version supplied as the probe's answer. Same question, same answer, no spawn — and no `#!` file, which is the construct C3-S4 fix round 1 measured as unstartable on win32.

    **Corpus, stated GROSS and NET, with the C4 exclusion cited by its verbatim phrase.** The Intent routes *"and the OpenCode wave-smoke parts of `verify-opencode.sh`"* to C4: `create_wave_fixture` through `run_wave_smoke`, eleven functions and 189 lines. `bootstrap/verify-opencode.sh` is 654 lines, so this half's verify denominator is **654 gross and 465 net**, and both are reported labelled. The consequence the exclusion carries and no row can: the port's verify has **no wave-smoke section and therefore no `exit 3`**, and `OSO_VERIFY_SKIP_SMOKE` is read by nothing in it, because the block that gate guards lives in C4. `OSO_VERIFY_SKIP_SMOKE` survives with its exact name and `=1` semantics in `bootstrap/verify-codex.sh`, so C3-D2's switch pair is intact.

    **Per-kind ratios, labelled with their denominators.** Install/purge/restore: **880** port lines (`opencode-install.ts` 529, `opencode-purge.ts` 256, `opencode-trust.ts` 63, `cli.ts` +21, `backup.ts` +2, `report.ts` +4, `store.ts` +5) ÷ **1,601** whole-file bash lines (`install-opencode.sh` 860 + `purge-opencode.sh` 617 + `restore-opencode.sh` 124) = **0.55**, or ÷ **1,477** net of the unported restore = **0.60**. Verify: **340** port lines (`verify-opencode.ts` 156 → 496) ÷ **654** gross = **0.52**, ÷ **465** net = **0.73**. **Whole-host, S4 and S4b together, which is what C3's record needs:** install/repair/purge **1,496** ÷ **1,692** (`repair-opencode.sh` 215 + `install-opencode.sh` 860 + `purge-opencode.sh` 617) = **0.88**; verify **540** ÷ 654 gross = **0.83**, ÷ 465 net = **1.16**. **One line count went DOWN and it is reported as a subtraction rather than dropped:** `verify-codex.ts` 478 → 473, because the byte-comparison predicate this half needed was already written there privately and moved to `store.ts` instead of being copied a third time. `opencode-trust.ts` is counted once, under install, and is read by the verify half too.

    **The record drift this slice was routed to close, re-measured.** The per-kind-ratios paragraph above cites `core/src/install/verify-opencode.ts` at 155 lines. Measured at this slice's start (`git show e43e69b:core/src/install/verify-opencode.ts | wc -l`) it is **156**, so the verify numerator that paragraph reports is 199 where it measures **200** and its whole-host figure moves with it. The ratio it states, 0.30 gross and 0.43 net, is unchanged at two decimal places.

    **The three OpenCode-specific sourced libraries, measured by function span rather than taken.** They total **253** lines and the boundary pass is right that no other host's port covers them. Split by which half reaches them: **137 belong to this host's install/verify surface** — `opencode-install-backups.sh` 17, `opencode-trust-bytes.sh` 70, and 50 of `opencode-verification.sh`'s 166 (`opencode_version_of`, `opencode_fixture_config_home`, `run_in_opencode_fixture`, `write_opencode_installer_shims`, and the file header) — and **116 travel with C4's bars** (`first_free_model_in`, `run_within_bound`, `opencode_binary_candidate`, `resolve_pinned_opencode_binary`, `install_opencode_fixture`, `remove_opencode_fixture`, `run_in_installed_fixture`). 137 + 116 = 253, which is the balance that makes the split a measurement. **This contradicts the ~154 this slice was armed with, by 17**, and the difference is `write_opencode_installer_shims`: it exists to satisfy a gate the port does not consume, so the port replaces it with nothing rather than porting it. Of this half's 137, S4 had already ported 27; this half ports the other 110, at `opencode-trust.ts` 63 ÷ `opencode-trust-bytes.sh` 70 = 0.90 for the one library it ports whole.

    **The `pipefail` + early-exiting-reader class, ENUMERATED across this half's corpus rather than closed at the one site it was routed.** The class is a reader that exits early at the READ end of a pipeline under `pipefail`, never a command name. Seven sites live in the five files this half owns. **Two are closed here.** `bootstrap/install-opencode.sh` line 184 at `2bc77ad` is the routed one and the only one with a live consequence: `printf '%s\n' "$published" | grep -Fxq` returns 141 the moment `printf` outruns the pipe buffer, so a PUBLISHED gate reads as unpublished and the install FAILS. **Reproduced red before the fix** — a driver with a 200,000-line `published` list reports `PIPED: published gate read as UNPUBLISHED (pipeline status 141)` while the here-string form reads it as published — and closed by replacing the pipeline with a here-string, one line for one line so no citation into that file moves. `bootstrap/lib/opencode-verification.sh` line 6 at `8fb780e` is the site the earlier enumeration missed and is NOT a `grep -q`-with-a-file-argument site: `grep -E … "$1" | head -1` puts grep at the write end of a pipe an early-exiting `head` closes, and it is closed with `grep -m1`, also one line for one line. **Five are enumerated and deliberately left**, each graded: `bootstrap/install-opencode.sh` line 20 at `2bc77ad` and `:712`, `bootstrap/verify-opencode.sh` line 12 at `2bc77ad` and `:293` are `sed -n … | head -1` where the writer emits one line and stops, and `bootstrap/purge-opencode.sh` line 280 at `2bc77ad` is `find … -print -quit | grep -q .` where `find` exits before the reader closes — all five latent at any input the tree can produce, all five in files C3-S6 deletes, and each fix costs a second line, which would shift the line numbers a dozen ledger citations into `install-opencode.sh` depend on. **That trade is the reason they are left, and it is stated rather than silent.**

    **The two measured divergences C3-S4 queued are REPRODUCED, not resolved, and their names are untouched.** `core/test/install/opencode-verify-parity.test.ts:255` and `core/test/install/opencode-verify-parity.test.ts:269` still open `UNRULED, AWAITING THE OPERATOR` and still pin the measurement rather than either verdict; this half's artifact rows read a different subject and neither case changes. The falsy non-object `mcp` and the unreadable config remain fail-closed in the port and remain the operator's to rule.

    **Four divergences this half adds, each measured and none silent.** (1) **The purge backup FORMAT.** `purge-opencode.sh` writes a `tar` archive per label plus a sha256 manifest; the port writes the shared install-backup shape — a `cp -a` copy under `items/` plus the `manifest` — keeping only the `oso-code-opencode-purge-v1` marker. That is `purgeCodex`'s precedent applied, taken because Node ships no tar writer and a hand-rolled one is unmeasured code in the integrity path; the consequence is that **neither implementation's `--restore` reads the other's backup**, which is why the parity claim is the restore ROUND TRIP through each implementation's own backup rather than a shared archive. The port's restore refuses a directory whose format marker is not its own, by name. (2) **The backup root's MODE, and the port was wrong.** Every bash backup root is `umask 077` plus `chmod 700`; `beginTransaction` chmod'd nothing, and a port install left the snapshot at **755** where the bash left **700** — measured on this machine, on the same drive that proved the trees identical. Closed in the shared `beginTransaction` rather than in the OpenCode caller, because Claude and Codex inherit the same miss from the same line. (3) **The Impeccable MOUNT is not ported**, following `installCodex`'s precedent exactly: the `--no-impeccable` arm writes the opt-out marker the bash writes and the verifier reads, and the mount arm reports where the mount belongs. The mount is a `git clone` of a pinned marketplace, which no PR-gate test may drive; it is named here rather than left as a silent narrowing, and it is the same gap `installCodex` already carries. (4) **The `npm prefix -g` probe inside the fallow resolver runs on the win32 arm only**, and the port reaches it through `resolveFallowMcpCommand`, which `claude.ts` already exports complete with that probe — so nothing is narrowed and no third private copy of the prefix probe is created.

    **`bootstrap/restore-opencode.sh` is NOT ported, and this contradicts the payload this slice was armed with.** Measured against the frozen text rather than the summary: C3's Intent names `bootstrap/install*.sh`, `verify*.sh`, `purge*.sh`, `repair*.sh`, `bootstrap/lib/*` and `install.ps1`'s body as the replaced set, and **`restore*.sh` is absent from it**; the CLI signature it freezes is `<install|verify|repair|purge>`, four verbs with no fifth; and `restore-codex.sh`, the sibling in exactly this position, was left unported by C3-S3 through three verifier rounds with nobody naming it. A `restore` verb is therefore a CLI-surface change the Intent does not authorise and an applier may not take. **The consequence, stated rather than left to be found:** the port's `prune_install_backups` gate reads `.install-restore-verified-opencode`, and the only thing that ever writes that marker is `bootstrap/restore-opencode.sh`, so retention on a port-only machine stays the standing no-op ADR-0124 designed until that script — or its replacement — runs. **This is a question for the operator, not a decision this slice took**: either `restore*.sh` stays bash and the ≤ 400-line `bootstrap/` exit accommodates its 124 lines plus `restore-codex.sh`'s 124, or a fifth verb is ruled and both hosts' restores land in one slice.

    **The bar this half closed at, and the suite total measured AFTER this prose landed, per G4's standing text.** `npm run typecheck` 0, `npm run build` 0, `npm run check` 0, `bash tests/plugin-lint.sh` clean on twelve rules, `bash -n` clean over every tracked shell source, and `npm test` exit **0** at **2,861** registered cases — 2,858 pass, 0 fail, 3 skipped, `pass + fail + skipped = tests`, 210 suites disjoint, and all three skips `OSO_NIGHTLY`-gated rather than win32's. The total moved from S4's 2,784 by **77**, and the three-term rule accounts for it exactly: **69** new cases measured with the prose absent — **59** of them read from the three parity files' own runs (14 install, 18 purge, 27 artifacts) and **10** from `cli.test.ts`'s edit and from the structural gates that emit one case per scanned source — plus the **8** full-path citations this block carries, plus **0** written inside a test NAME. **The prediction of 10 citations was wrong by 2 and the run corrected it**, because `` `:712` `` and `` `:293` `` are written as bare continuations of the citation before them, which the gate counts as bare and emits no case for — a distinction worth carrying: a second spelling of the same file's line is only a case when it repeats the path. `bash tests/hooks-test.sh` exits **0** at `passed: 1743, failed: 0, skipped: 6`, read off the producing process rather than a wrapper — **which contradicts the `passed: 1742, failed: 1` this slice was armed with, and the contradiction is recorded rather than resolved**: the case that moved is the one the ambient `oso-state` round-trip red claims, and one green run is one observation, not the five the standing rule requires before that condition is re-characterised. It is still named as ambient here. **Budgets re-measured under S4's stated convention, tracked `.ts` sources only, by `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard`:** `core/src/install` **5,804** of 9,000, `core/test/install` **6,220** of 8,000, `core/src` **11,269** of 14,500, `core/test` **14,378** of 17,500. **Three ambient conditions are dismissed BY NAME rather than chased:** the `oso-state` round-trip red, the journal usage-error red, and `tests/hooks-test.sh`'s external network dependency. **`core.hooksPath` is unchanged** at `~/.local/share/oso-code/runtime/git-hooks` and `git config --local --list` still reports its 19 entries, asserted by `repository-git-config-guard` after every drive in the three new files.

  - **C3-S4b fix round 1 — the privacy posture the port narrowed, and the net that could not see it, measured 2026-08-31 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, c3-s4b-fix-1).** Four figures this round was armed with are contradicted by measurement and each contradiction is named where it sits rather than quietly corrected.

    **The net was compared over the wrong dimension, and that is the deliverable rather than the chmod.** `core/test/support/opencode-fixture.ts`'s tree reader listed FILES ONLY and carried BYTES ONLY, so an installer whose privacy posture is expressed in modes was being compared on the one axis that posture never touches: a directory was invisible entirely, and a file whose bytes matched compared equal at any mode. It now returns a discriminated `directory | file` entry carrying `kind` and an octal-spelled `mode`, and the walk yields directories beside files. **Shown RED on the pre-fix tree before its green was believed**: the install parity comparison went red on **95 mode divergences over 125 entries per leg** — 31 directories and 94 files, 0 kind divergences, 0 content divergences and no entry present on one leg alone, so the corpus was identical in every dimension the old net could read and wrong in the one it could not. 20 directories read `0755` against the bash's `0700` and 75 files read `0644` against its `0600`, leaving 11 of the 31 directories and 19 of the 94 files already in agreement — **both terms of this split were wrong as this record first carried them (21 and 74), re-measured and corrected by fix round 2 below, which also derives where the 21 came from**.

    **The mechanism, and the half a umask cannot reach.** `bootstrap/install-opencode.sh` line 246 at `2bc77ad` sets `umask 077` inside `begin_transaction()`, and a shell function runs in the same process, so that umask governs the whole remaining install — including the payload copies, which the bash makes with `cp -R` rather than `cp -a`, creating each file at the source mode masked by the umask. The port's `cpSync` is `cp -a` semantics: **it PRESERVES the source mode and a umask never reaches it**, measured directly — under `process.umask(0o077)` a `cpSync` of a `0644` file lands `0644` while the directories it creates land `0700`. So the class needed both halves, and a umask alone would have closed the directories and left all 74 files open. `core/src/install/opencode-install.ts` now runs the whole install inside the owner-only umask and masks the staged tree in `replaceTree`, which is the single seam all seven payload trees already passed through. **Re-measured after the fix: 95 → 0 over the same 125-entry corpus**, so the green is not an emptied comparison.

    **The primitive was already written and is reused rather than copied.** `core/src/state/store.ts` already held a scoped `withOwnerOnlyUmask`, private; it is exported rather than duplicated, so the three call sites share one implementation and the process umask is always restored.

    **Which hosts the fix reaches, and the one it deliberately does not.** OpenCode install and purge, and Codex install and purge — **two hosts, not the three this round was armed with**. The scope test is each host's own bash oracle: `bootstrap/install-codex.sh` line 369 at `2bc77ad` and `bootstrap/purge-codex.sh` line 120 at `2bc77ad` both carry `umask 077`, so Codex inherits the same miss and is closed with it; **`bootstrap/install.sh`, which is what installs Claude, carries NO `umask 077` anywhere and no `chmod` but a single `chmod 755` on the engram binary**, so the port's Node defaults there are parity-CORRECT and narrowing them would be an unmeasured behavior change against that host's own oracle rather than a hole closed. Claude is therefore named as outside the class rather than skipped. The purge umask is scoped to the backup-taking path alone, never the restore, because `bootstrap/purge-opencode.sh` reaches `create_backup_root` only on the purge leg and its restore runs at the ambient umask — wrapping the whole verb would have diverged in the opposite direction.

    **The backup-root claim this child's own record carries is overstated, and this is the fourth contradiction.** That record states every bash backup root is `umask 077` plus `chmod 700`. Two of the three are: `bootstrap/install.sh` creates `$BACKUP_DIR` with a bare `mkdir -p` under no umask and never chmods it. The shared `chmod 700` this child added therefore leaves the port **stricter than the bash for Claude**, at `0700` where that script leaves `0755`. It is kept — a snapshot of the operator's config is exactly the thing that should be owner-only, and the direction is closed rather than open — but it is a narrowing beyond parity rather than the parity fix the record calls it.

    **What the kind dimension found that no mode could: two claims in `core/test/install/opencode-purge-parity.test.ts` that were true of files alone.** With directories visible, `both took the whole seeded HOME with them` failed — the purge leaves the parent directories its targets hung from, and leaves the scaffolding holding the backup it deliberately keeps — and `restores to the tree it started from` failed by exactly that scaffolding. **Both implementations agreed exactly**, so neither was a divergence; both were the byte-only net's blindness stated as fact in a test name. The claims are rewritten to what is true: every seeded FILE is taken, every named target is gone, and what survives is directories — guarded against vacuity by asserting the survivor list is non-empty. The kept backup's scaffolding is excluded **only where the comparison is against the pre-purge tree, never where the two implementations are compared**, so the mode gate that this round's fix closed stays at full strength.

    **The `pipefail` corpus is NINE across FOUR files, not seven across five.** Re-enumerated over the pre-slice tree by scanning every pipeline whose read end can exit early: `bootstrap/install-opencode.sh` `:20`, `:94`, `:184`, `:712`; `bootstrap/verify-opencode.sh` `:12`, `:293`, `:336`; `bootstrap/purge-opencode.sh` `:280`; `bootstrap/lib/opencode-verification.sh` `:6`. **Two are closed and SEVEN are left, where the record says five.** The two the earlier enumeration missed both carry the shape and neither carries a consequence: `bootstrap/install-opencode.sh` line 94 at `2bc77ad` ends `| head -1 || true`, where the `|| true` absorbs the status outright, and `bootstrap/verify-opencode.sh` line 336 at `2bc77ad` is `head -n N "$global" | cmp -s - "$seed"`, where `cmp -s` exits at the first difference and both non-zero statuses collapse to the same `rewritten` branch. **The record stated seven as the corpus when seven is the count that REMAINS after two were closed** — the subset with a reachable consequence was never the corpus, and the two framings were folded into one number.

    **`:184`'s "live consequence" grading is withdrawn; the fix stands.** Its input is `published_gate_scripts`, which emits only the `plugin/hooks/*.sh` rows of `bootstrap/hook-hashes.txt`. Driven directly, that is **12 entries and 254 bytes** against a 65,536-byte pipe buffer — a factor of **258**, and the whole hashes file carries 33 rows, so the list would need roughly three thousand times its content to reach the buffer. The `141` was reproduced only under a synthetic 200,000-line list **that function cannot produce**. Replacing the pipeline with a here-string was right and cost one line for one line; what is corrected is the grading that separated it from the others as the only site with a live consequence, when on every input the tree can produce it is **latent exactly like the other seven**.

    **A net that could drop a row it was written to catch, at `core/test/install/opencode-verify-artifacts-parity.test.ts`.** Its row reader filtered its own findings down to the names the table already declares before comparing them to that same table, so a row the port emitted and the table never declared was silently dropped rather than caught — the child's own recurring shape, a check that cannot notice what it was not already expecting. The filter is removed and **shown RED on a planted undeclared row against the old reader** before the new one's green was believed, with the suite's other 27 cases green either way, which is what proves the filter protected nothing and only blinded.

    **The symlink-escape guard's fallback direction is closed.** `core/src/install/opencode-purge.ts`'s physical-path helper returned the LITERAL path when `realpathSync` threw, inside the guard that decides whether a purge target escapes HOME — so a resolution that failed on a permission error, an `ELOOP` or a race read as a path below HOME and the guard OPENED. The bash closes it: its `physical="$(cd ... && pwd -P)"` leaves the value empty on failure and the empty value matches no `"$HOME_PHYSICAL"/*` case, so the run fails. The helper now returns `undefined` and both call sites refuse by name, which is the bash's direction rather than the opposite one.

    **One constant returned to its use.** `core/src/install/backup.ts` named `0o700` at the top of the file for a single `chmod` of the backup root. Judged against the rubric's over-extraction arm it is a one-use, self-evident value whose name was longer than the mode it hid, and it now reads as `chmodSync(backupRoot, 0o700)` beside the bash's own `chmod 700`. The mask the new code needs is NOT the same judgment and keeps its name: `0o7700` clears group and other while preserving the owner and the setuid, setgid and sticky bits, which is behavior-carrying and unreadable as a bare literal.

    **The bar this round closed at, and the suite total measured AFTER this prose landed, per G4's standing text.** `npm run typecheck` 0, `npm run build` 0, `npm run check` 0, `bash tests/plugin-lint.sh` clean on twelve rules, `bash -n` clean over all **43** tracked shell sources, and `npm test` exit **0** at **2,865** registered cases — 2,862 pass, 0 fail, 3 skipped, `pass + fail + skipped = tests`, 210 suites disjoint, all three `OSO_NIGHTLY`-gated rather than win32's, and the exit read off the producing process rather than a wrapper. **The total moved from S4b's 2,861 by exactly 4, and the three-term rule accounts for it with nothing left over:** **1** new case — the undeclared-row gate below — plus the **3** full `path:line` citations this block carries, plus **0** written inside a test NAME. Every other path this block spells is a bare path or a bare `:line` continuation, which the citation gate counts as bare and emits no case for. **`bash tests/hooks-test.sh` is recorded as ONE OBSERVATION AMONG A FLAPPING SET rather than a settled value, which corrects the record above.** That record carries exit 0 at `passed: 1743, failed: 0`; this round ran it TWICE and both runs report exit **1** at `passed: 1742, failed: 1, skipped: 6`, the single failure being the named ambient `oso-state` round-trip red — **four red observations now stand against that one green**, two from the verifier and two from this round. The condition is established as ambient rather than assumed: the failing case drives `bootstrap/verify.sh`, which this slice never touched, and `git diff -- plugin/` is empty so the probed binary is byte-unchanged. **It is NOT re-characterised** — five runs is the bar and this is four — it is recorded as flapping, and the record should carry the same care about a green as about a red. **Budgets re-measured under this child's stated convention, tracked AND untracked `.ts` sources by `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard`, AFTER this prose landed:** `core/src/install` **5,827** of 9,000, `core/test/install` **6,251** of 8,000, `core/src` **11,292** of 14,500, `core/test` **14,427** of 17,500 — the four moved by +23, +31, +23 and +49 against the figures this round was armed with, and the only non-`.ts` sources under either tree remain the JSON gate fixtures and the one `.mjs` sandbox harness the convention already excludes. **Three ambient conditions dismissed BY NAME rather than chased:** the `oso-state` round-trip red, the journal usage-error red, and `tests/hooks-test.sh`'s external network dependency. **`core.hooksPath` is unchanged** and `git config --local --list` is byte-identical to its pre-round snapshot at 19 entries, diffed rather than eyeballed. **The two unruled pins at `core/test/install/opencode-verify-parity.test.ts` are untouched**, that file byte-unchanged across this round.
  - **C3-S4b fix round 2 — the oracle the repair verb actually ports, and a split that was wrong in both terms, measured 2026-08-31 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, c3-s4b-fix-2).** Three items closed, one class deliberately left OPEN on its own measurement rather than narrowed on the reading this round was handed.

    **The mode split was wrong in BOTH terms, and reproducing the wrong one is what identifies it.** Re-driven over the same seed with the port's umask half reverted and both legs compared by name, kind and mode: the corpus is **31 directories and 94 files, 125 entries per leg**, 0 kind divergences, 0 content divergences, no entry present on one leg alone — all of which the record above already had right. The split of the 95 is **20 directories** (`0755` against the bash's `0700`) and **75 files** (`0644` against its `0600`), leaving 11 directories and 19 files already in agreement. **The 21 is not invented:** with `core/src/install/backup.ts:86`'s `chmodSync(backupRoot, 0o700)` ALSO reverted — the whole pre-fix port rather than the pre-umask one — the same drive reports **96** over the same 125 entries, **21 directories and 75 files**. The twenty-first directory is the backup ROOT itself, which divergence (2) closes separately, so the 21 is a reading taken before (2) landed and carried past it. The 74 is wrong under either reading, and 21 + 74 closing on 95 is the arithmetic accident that let it travel.

    **`bootstrap/repair-engram-codex.sh` is NOT `oso repair --host codex`'s oracle, so the umask class is NOT closed for that verb.** `repairCodex` (`core/src/install/codex.ts:263`) does four things: take an install-backup transaction over `config` and `global`, normalise the Engram pointers, rebuild the managed `config.toml` region, rebuild the global `AGENTS.md` region. **Three of the four appear nowhere in that script**, whose own `main()` downloads a pinned Engram release, replaces the binary, runs `engram setup codex`, `doctor` and `stats` and then spawns `bootstrap/verify-codex.sh` — none of which `repairCodex` does — and which rebuilds NEITHER managed region, normalising the pointers strictly OUTSIDE them. ### C4 — `rewrite-certification`: host certification as a nightly lane in TypeScript

**Intent.** Port `tests/opencode-contract-bar.sh` and `tests/opencode-behavior-bar.sh` (and the Codex smoke parts of `verify-codex.sh`, and the OpenCode wave-smoke parts of `verify-opencode.sh`) to `core/test/certify/*.test.ts`, run only under `OSO_CERTIFY=1` with the pinned binaries, reporting per gate what was measured; `docs/parity-*.md` shrink to the divergence tables those runs fill in.

  - **Intent amended 2026-08-31 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Decision 33, c3-s4-amend-1), recorded before C3-S4 arms.** The Intent above gains **the OpenCode wave-smoke parts of `verify-opencode.sh`** beside the Codex smoke parts. Measured for this entry at `381350a`: `create_wave_fixture` through `run_wave_smoke` inclusive, **eleven functions and 189 lines** — `create_wave_fixture`, `cleanup_smoke_on_exit`, `run_in_smoke_home`, `choose_smoke_model`, `run_wave_child`, `smoke_verdict_of`, `run_wave_children`, `worktree_permission_auto_rejected`, `wave_smoke_outcome`, `record_smoke_not_run`, `run_wave_smoke`. The block spawns `opencode models` against the live model catalog and `opencode run --dir "$worktree" -m "$SMOKE_MODEL" --format json` across two branches made with `git worktree add`, which is `OSO_CERTIFY`'s charter and **G4's prohibition** in one block: it cannot live in a PR-gate test, and C4 is the lane holding the pinned binaries.
    **Anchored by NAME and never by line, which is a rule here rather than a preference.** The Codex ownership row above was found stale by four lines in a way that still RESOLVED, because the citation net checks file existence and numeric bounds and never content — a line-anchored span rots into a confident wrong answer where a function name either resolves or fails loudly. The 189 is a MEASURE, and measures age; the eleven names are the citation, and it does not.
    **Two more stale citations in that same row, measured at `381350a` while confirming the rule and recorded rather than rewritten, because the Codex row is C3-S3's record and not this entry's to edit.** `codexPathsFor` is cited `core/src/install/codex.ts:84-92` and sits at `:88` — the four lines above, still inside its own span. `normalizeEngramPointers` is cited `core/src/install/codex.ts:347-348` and sits at `:355`, which its span does NOT contain. `firstExecutableOnPath` is cited `core/src/install/verify-claude.ts:480` and sits at `:477`. So the net is failing wider than the one case that prompted the rule, and the fix when somebody owns that row is a name anchor, never a fresh number that will rot the same way. **Decision 34 gave them a destination rather than a margin note: they join the Decision 9 residual stream for the sweep and C5**, which is where another slice's record gets corrected by somebody who owns it. **Corrected in C5's opening record commit, 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 46), and the count in this entry was itself WRONG: the class sweep G12 requires found TEN line anchors on that row, not two more beside Decision 34's three.** Measured at `1c85baa`: **five land in the wrong function** — the `normalizeEngramPointers` and `firstExecutableOnPath` spans this entry already names, plus one claiming `wireGitCommitHook` that lands on `writeGlobalGuidance`, one claiming the `resolveFallowMcpCommand` injection that lands inside `normalizedEngramPointerConfig`, and one claiming the repair rail that lands on `writeCodexInstall`'s closing brace, a line before `repairCodex` opens; **two are degraded but inside the right body** — the `codexPathsFor` span, correct only because it still contains its target, and one claiming the install rail that lands on a `fatalOutcome` inside `writeCodexInstall`; **and three are correct**, all three in `core/src/install/codex-config.ts`. **Every one of the ten passed `citations-resolve.test.ts`**, which reads a cited file's existence and its line's numeric bounds and never its content, against two files long enough to swallow any of them — which is the whole case for the name-anchor rule and the third time in one week it has been made rather than argued. **The row was converted WHOLE, the three correct anchors included**, because they are correct the way `codexPathsFor` was correct until it was not, and a reader of a half-swept row cannot tell which anchors were checked; the sweep's declared surface is that row and no other line anchor in this document.
    **C3-S4b's corpus EXCLUDES this block BY NAME with its measure stated, and C3-S4b reports its coverage GROSS AND NET of it** — Decision 31 part 3's rule applied verbatim. `bootstrap/verify-opencode.sh` is 654 lines and the excluded block is 189 of them, so C3-S4b's net denominator is 465; both figures are reported labelled, never one of them silently.
**Decisions.** C4-D1: a certification test that cannot drive its gate reports `not-run` as a result, never `pass` — the `exit 3` tolerance of today's CI is replaced by a report line the nightly summary shows. C4-D2: the pinned host versions live in one file, `core/src/install/pins.ts`, read by installers, certification and the parity docs check.
  - **C4-D2 amended 2026-08-30 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable 9eab15, model Claude Fable 5, C3-S2b, Decision 26).** `pins.ts` is created in **C3-S2b**, not here — the engram release provisioning it pins needed a home for `SUPPORTED_ENGRAM_VERSION` before C4 exists, and C4-D2's own text ("live in one file... read by installers, certification and the parity docs check") already describes the file this slice creates rather than a new one. C4 adds pins to it; C4 does not open it.

**C4-D3. DELEGATED 2026-08-29 (operator's delegate, Fable 9eab15, Decision 14), recorded here before C4 is armed:** the certification lane reports, per host, the absolute cold start, the bare-node control and the ratio between them, and from that data the PR gate's cold-start FRACTION (C2-D26, today 1.5 from Linux alone) is re-derived by the SAME method used to set it — the worst observed ratio across hosts times a stated headroom — never as a per-platform constant and never tuned by hand toward a runner's observed number. **First real per-host pair, measured 2026-08-29 on run 33260013786 and recorded here rather than left in a log:** ubuntu-latest 83.9 ms bundle against a 49.7 ms control, overhead 34.2 ms against a 74.6 ms budget; windows-latest 160.6 ms against 82.7 ms, overhead 78.0 ms against 124.0 ms, **ratio 0.94**. So the worst observed ratio is no longer the 0.82 Linux alone showed and 1.5 sits **1.6×** above it rather than 1.83×. Had the tightening toward 1.0 been taken, the Windows leg would have gone red at 78.0 against 82.7 — the number that looked conservative on one platform was barely adequate on the other, which is the whole reason this re-derivation waits for per-host data.
  - **C4-D3 IS UNIMPLEMENTED IN C4 AND IS CARRIED FORWARD TO C6, amended here 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable d2c142, Decision 43), because a chapter does not close over its own ledger in silence.** (a) **Why not in C4:** the decision needs a whole instrument — per-host timing inside the certification lane plus a re-derivation of a PR-gate constant — which is a slice rather than a fix, on a chapter that grew from three slices to thirteen under a budget the operator set to the minimum. Bolting it on at the close would have built a fourteenth slice to satisfy a ledger line rather than a measured need. (b) **What already flows:** the PR gate publishes the bundle mean and the bare-node control per leg on every run, so the DATA stream exists; what is missing is the lane's per-host report and the re-derivation. (c) **The trigger:** C6's certification runs on the three hosts produce the pairs, and the FRACTION is re-derived there by the frozen method — the worst observed ratio times a stated headroom — BEFORE the release tags. Until then **1.5 stands**, already recorded above as sitting 1.6× above the worst observed ratio rather than tuned toward a runner's number.

**Proposed slices.** S1 (opus) contract + behaviour bars as tests; S2 nightly wiring + parity doc tables; S3 CONTRACT delete the two bash bars. Exit: nightly green with every gate row `measured` on the pinned OpenCode and Codex.
  - **Exit line amended 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable d2c142, Decision 43).** "every gate row `measured` on the pinned OpenCode **and Codex**" is unmeetable by design and was recorded as such in `docs/parity-codex.md` rather than amended, which is the drift this amendment closes. The Codex authenticated smoke copies the operator's real credential and spawns an agent with the sandbox disabled, so it is DEFAULT-CLOSED behind an explicit opt-in and reports not-run without it; no machine in reach carries Codex credentials. **The Exit reads: nightly green with every gate row of the pinned OpenCode `measured`, and every Codex row either `measured` or reporting a not-run that names why it could not be driven.** A lane that reports honestly what it could not drive meets this; a lane that claims a measurement it never took does not.

Sequential rather than parallel because S1a lays the lane's support — the `OSO_CERTIFY` guard shape, the not-run recorder, the pinned-binary locator — and the later slices import it; three appliers inventing that module at once is the moving part this roadmap does not buy, and the operator's constraint is quota rather than wall-clock. Every applier and verifier of this child launches SONNET, including the slice G13 names by id as opus: **that is the operator's own amendment to G13, taken for budget on 2026-08-31, not a delegated ruling.** The compensation is that payloads are transcription-shaped — each bar's payload carries its check table (row name, the bash function at `381350a`, the expected value, how the row is driven) so the applier transcribes rather than reads.

Running an installed entrypoint with node against a fixture HOME reads no host binary, so these are layer 1 and belong on the PR gate. **Priority (1) is what makes it the first slice rather than a residual: today `oso verify --host codex` on a CORRECT install reports three failures and exits 1, so the operator's Codex install can never verify green — a functional defect on a host, today.** S0 reuses the two Claude drivers rather than writing a second spawn shape, lifting them into one shared module if the import direction is wrong; it retires the literal `installed-probe-is-nightly-only` from the source AND from the committed bundle `bootstrap/oso.js`, which mirrors it and is what nightly actually runs, so S0 rebuilds; and it proves each row red-first over `core/test/support/codex-install-fixture.ts` — passing on a fixture install and failing on a deliberately broken input.

The figure is RE-READ from the leg after S0 as arithmetic on a structural ground, never hand-set, and a different published number is a finding rather than a typo to absorb.

No such assertion exists or should. The narrowing is carried into the residual stream below rather than left claimed as closed.

(1) The cross-host verify coupling is **eight edges**, not one: nine install modules import from `verify-claude.ts` for host-neutral utilities, including `pins.ts` — C4-D2's own neutral pin module — depending on a host's verify; the remedy is a host-neutral probes module taking the whole set, or the next host port reopens it. (9) The `hooks/*.sh` executable glob, above. (10) `probeEnvironment` duplicated between product and certify support, where the lane meant to prove the port carries its own copy of the port's probe environment.
  - **A naming debt this chapter created and records rather than carries silently.** This document named three C4 slices; the tree carries nine — S0a, S0b, S0c, S1a-1, S1a-2, S1b-1, S1b-2, S1c-1, S1c-2 — and the letters accreted for three unrelated reasons: a decided pre-split of the bars, an applier hitting the size guard mid-slice, and a defect found mid-chapter. **The cost was demonstrated rather than suspected: the operator, who commissioned the chapter, could not tell from the names which slices existed or which were done.** An identifier should say WHICH; the journal says WHY. A future chapter numbers its slices in one sequence and puts the genealogy in prose.

  - **The size bound's INSTRUMENT, ratified 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable d2c142, model Claude Fable 5, Decision 41 part 1), because the two C4-S0 appliers measured the same bound with different ones and only luck kept it from costing a round.** A slice's size is its **AUTHORED CHANGED LINES**, `git diff --numstat <slice base>..HEAD -- . ':!bootstrap/oso.js'` summed over insertions and deletions, with the generated set being exactly `bootstrap/oso.js` today and extended only by naming a file. Re-measured under it: S0a is 181 authored against 454 raw, S0b is 343 against 816, the bundle alone accounting for 87 and 141 lines nobody wrote. Raw `git diff | wc -l` is the wrong instrument in the other direction — it counts file headers, hunk markers and unchanged context, inflating S0b's figure by roughly two thirds. Every payload names the command rather than saying "450 diff lines" and letting each applier pick a counter, and every slice record carries both figures with the instrument named.
  - **The instrument gains its DELETION-SLICE branch, 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable d2c142, Decision 43), because C4-S3 met a case the rule had no answer for and answered it in a commit message where the next slice would not read it.** The instrument sums insertions AND deletions, under which a CONTRACT slice that removes four files is 1,516 against a 450 bound before it authors a line. **A deletion slice reports TWO figures by the same `--numstat`, never one:** the line count of the files it deletes, and the authored changes to everything else. **The bound governs the second alone.** C4-S3 measured 1,483 deleted against 33 authored and both reproduce exactly. Folding them would make the bound meaningless precisely where a CONTRACT slice needs it, since such a slice removes a great deal and authors very little.
  - **The instrument gains its REWRITE branch, 2026-09-02 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 58), sibling to the deletion branch above and argued from it rather than beside it.** The deletion branch says a slice that removes files reports TWO figures by the same `--numstat` — the lines it deletes and the authored changes to everything else — and the bound governs the second, "since such a slice removes a great deal and authors very little". **A rewrite-in-place under C5-D1 is that same asymmetry one file at a time:** the old prose leaves, the new prose is what a verifier reads, and a blended count charges ONE act twice. Measured rather than argued: `plugin/skills/_shared/bodies/plan.md` is 289 lines and C5-D1 caps its rewrite at 300, so the blended instrument reads ≈ 590 for ≈ 300 lines of authoring. **So a REWRITE slice reports lines DELETED from the files it rewrites and lines INSERTED, and the bound of 450 governs INSERTIONS.** The deletions are not unpoliced, they are policed by a different instrument: the capability inventory's prose rows, the `none`-count, and the verifier's sentence-level sweep — the same three that carried C5-S1b-3, where 290 lines left the tree and the sweep proved nothing left with them. **The arithmetic that makes this a design fact rather than a convenience, and it is what sets the seams:** C5-D1 caps every body at 300 lines, so any single body's authored figure is ≤ 300 BY CONSTRUCTION, and one body plus its rows fits 450 with room while two bodies do not. **Two alternatives rejected with their reasons.** A raised bound of 900 for prose slices: a bound raised once for the biggest file stops meaning anything, and C5-S0's 121-line overage stands on this record as exactly that argument. A section seam inside `plan.md`: it ships a hybrid file on the branch and splits one flow's voice across two verifiers' reads, for a number the instrument was mis-measuring in the first place — the fix belongs in the instrument, not in the prose.

  - **A BOUND CROSSED BY A QUARTER, and the two-clause lesson it earned, 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 49).** C5-S0 measured **571 authored lines against its 450 bound** — 565 across `core/test/support/prose-inventory.ts` and `core/test/structure/capability-inventory.test.ts` plus 6 workflow insertions, the numstat form returning nothing for the untracked half because HEAD had not moved and an applier may not `git add`. **Recorded as a DEVIATION and refused as an exception**, on the entry above's own reasoning: the bound stays 450 for every later C5 slice. The magnitude argument was put and answered — C4-S1c-2's precedent was written for 7 lines at 1.2% and this is 121 at 27% — and it cuts the other way: **a quarter is when a bound must NOT bend, or it stops meaning anything.** **Where the 121 went, traced rather than estimated:** the slice stood at 447 before its fix round, and the overage is entirely the multiset arithmetic with its independent phantom-origin and duplicate-origin checks and the five-axis independent-witness structure — both ORDERED BY A RULING AFTER THE BOUND WAS SET, neither unreported scope; the same ruling's own estimate that they "should net near zero" was wrong by 121. **The lesson, both halves in one sentence: a bound set before a fix round does not price the mechanisms that round is later ordered to add, so a ruling that adds machinery to an armed slice PRICES it — a line estimate per mechanism — and the round's payload carries a bound re-derived from that price BEFORE it runs, so the next overage is a mis-estimate to correct rather than an unpriced deviation to report.**
  - **A SECOND DEVIATION, sixteen lines, and an estimate wrong in KIND rather than degree, 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 55 Q2).** C5-S1b-2 measured **466 authored against 450** — tracked 462 less the 282 excused overlay deletions is 180, plus 286 untracked authored. Recorded as a DEVIATION at 3.6%; the bound stays 450. **The cause is a property of the instrument, not an excess of the slice: a relocation into NEW files earns NO RENAME CREDIT**, because `git diff` never lists untracked files, so moved prose is paid as full insertion on both sides. **This invalidates an estimate in Decision 52 that priced references at 60% of the overlay content, and the delegate records it as wrong in kind rather than in degree** — C5-S1b-1's apparent 60% discount was an artifact of that slice's own file shape and never a property of relocations. **CORRECTED METHOD for every remaining relocation:** authored is the full line count of the content moved into new files, plus the machinery; the only discount is a whole-file deletion, which the deletion branch already excuses. **Re-projected NOW rather than at the wall:** C5-S1b-3 is ≈ 290 moved whole + ≈ 10 renderer + ≈ 10 verify, so ≈ 310–330 with a margin of at least 120, and its rows are data. C5-S1c's collapse rewrites in place and was priced on both sides already.
