It joins the generated set **for the RENDERED hosts only — `codex.md` now, `opencode.md` at C5-S1b-3.** **`claude.md` is NOT named and stays counted**, because C5-S1b-1 AUTHORED it; the three files share a directory and a reader who skipped that sentence would exclude the authored one.
  - **THE C5 RESERVE IS TWO-THIRDS SPENT WITH SIX SLICES LEFT, measured and recorded 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 50 Q2), BEFORE anything goes red rather than at a close.** By G4's own instrument after C5-S0, C5-S0b and C5-S1a, `core/test` holds **17,494** lines of the 18,500 ceiling — **1,006 of headroom**, against a starting 16,727, so **767 of the 1,000 reserved for C5 is gone** and S1b, S1c, S2, S3, S4 and S5 have not run. **Owned rather than explained away: the 1,000 predates the operator's capability constraint, and C5-S0's instrument alone is 565 TypeScript lines — more than half the reserve — for two slices, S0 and S0b, that Decisions 45 and 47 added after the reserve was written.** S5 is the slice that threatens the ceiling: porting the eleven surviving structural checks out of `tests/plugin-lint.sh` projects between 480 lines at C3's measured 0.9 test-kind ratio and 1,030 at C4's measured 1.93, and the high end does not fit in 1,006. `core/src` is not in question at 11,889 of 14,500. **THE CEILING DOES NOT MOVE ON A PROJECTION — G4's own standing text forbids it, and the last time this roadmap acted on one it was short by 1,388.** Three clauses govern the rest of C5 instead. **(1) WATERMARK.** The slice whose measurement first crosses **18,000** reports the figure and STOPS; the ceiling is then re-derived by the frozen method with MEASURED inputs — the on-disk count at that moment, the latest measured per-kind ratio applied to the remaining plugin-lint surface measured by `wc -l` at that moment, and C6's stated allocation, which for `core/test` is none — rounded up to the next 500, by an amendment showing its arithmetic, in ONE decision request carrying those inputs so it rules in one pass. **(2) BEFORE ARMING.** Every remaining payload states its projected `core/test` cost with the method named, against the measured headroom; a projection that exceeds the headroom is a decision request BEFORE the round, never a red gate after it — Decision 49's prospective clause pointed at the ceiling. **(3) AFTER LANDING.** Every slice record states the `core/test` figure it leaves behind, so the number is on the record slice by slice rather than reconstructed at a close.
  - **The certify ports are PRE-SPLIT into six halves at named function-boundary seams, with a 600-line guard (Decision 41 part 2), superseding the S1a/S1b/S1c triple above.** The stop-and-report bound is **600 authored lines for slices whose diff is new files under `core/test/certify/`**, and stays 450 for slices editing existing code. Pre-splitting rather than relying on the guard alone, because at any ratio between C3's 0.9 test figure and its 1.57 verify figure both bars project past 450, so a guard-only run has the applier draft an entire bar, measure, revert by hand and report blocked — which S0 proved works, at the price of a discarded draft, and that price doubles on an 800-line file. Fixed criteria per half also let a sonnet verifier judge a fixed list rather than a moving boundary, and halve every fix round's re-read. **The seams.** S1a-1: the lane support — the `OSO_CERTIFY` guard shape, the not-run recorder, the pinned-binary locator — plus the pin, fixture and catalog rows and the four live-server registration rows. S1a-2: the roster, the five per-agent permission loops, the skills registry, and the routed-agent capability assertion that reads both sides from the real binary, with the file-level floor raised to 58. S1b-1: the session driver plus the authorization and transcript rows, including the same-turn host-fact row, with the behaviour bar's gated-verb judge re-expressed as a `lineVerdict` judge callback over the lexed command's tokens and no `lib.sh` ported. S1b-2: the commit and deploy refusals, the plugin-registers-nothing trio and the full plan cycle entering through the plan command, with the floor raised to 11. S1c-1: the Codex authenticated smoke, its skip-request and login-status gates becoming not-run reasons. S1c-2: the OpenCode wave smoke — the eleven functions, the thirteen module-level variables they read and the consumer in `main` — importing the session-verdict reader rather than porting it. **Each half asserts ITS OWN row count as a `provedSomething` floor, the second half of each pair raises the file floor to the bash's pinned total, and the two halves' counts must SUM to that total or a row was lost.** Order stays sequential.
  - **C4-S2's record-only enumeration of the PR gate's Windows/Linux registration gap, measured 2026-09-01 and reproducing the observed 98 exactly.** The Windows leg of the PR gate has registered 98 fewer `node --test` cases than the Linux leg on ten consecutive commits, and the mechanism is a distinction confirmed live rather than assumed: a `describe(name, { skip: reason }, callback)` never invokes `callback` when `reason` is truthy, so no `test()` inside it ever registers, while a `test(name, { skip: reason }, fn)` still registers with a `# SKIP` annotation — a leaf-level skip changes nothing about the count, only a describe-level skip removes children from it. Ten of the sixteen call sites that import a guard from `core/test/support/win32-skip-guards.ts` gate a single `test()` leaf and so contribute nothing; the four that gate a `describe()` are `core/test/install/backup.test.ts`'s `backupSizeKib` describe (`skipUnlessDiskBlocksAreAllocated`, 3 leaves — its 3 `test()` calls), `core/test/install/verify-claude.test.ts`'s `engramBinaryRuns` describe (`skipUnlessKernelRunsScriptFixtures`, 4 leaves), `core/test/port/private-run-directory-mode.test.ts`'s private-state-directory describe (`skipUnlessMkdirHonoursOwnerOnlyMode`, 2 leaves) and `core/test/gates/teardown-rotation-failure.test.ts`'s aged-rotation describe (`skipUnlessChmodDeniesDirectoryWrites`, 1 leaf), summing to **10**. The remaining **88** come from `skipUnlessSpawnable` (`core/test/support/state-sandbox.ts`), a different guard from a different module, included because its `STATE_SUBJECTS` loop's `plugin/bin/oso-state` iteration gates the exact same class of divergence the four guards above name by a different mechanism: that subject is an extensionless `#!/usr/bin/env node` script win32 cannot spawn directly, matching what `skipUnlessGitRunsShebangHooks` and `skipUnlessPathResolvesExtensionlessNames` already state about win32 having no shebang handling — never included to reach a number, excluded first on a stricter reading that summed to 10, and folded in only once its own mechanism was independently verified. Six files carry that describe over the shebang subject: `core/test/parity.test.ts` (58 leaves — one `test()` per fixture `readdirSync` finds under `core/test/fixtures/state/`, counted directly: 58 `*.json` files, none other), `core/test/port/argv.test.ts` (21 leaves — one per entry of its `REFUSED_ARGV` array, counted by hand against the array literal), `core/test/port/clear.test.ts` (5 leaves), `core/test/port/unreadable.test.ts` (2 leaves), `core/test/port/lock.test.ts` (1 leaf) and `core/test/port/journal.test.ts` (1 leaf) — each of the last four counted directly against its own `test()` calls inside the shebang-subject iteration. **10 + 58 + 21 + 5 + 2 + 1 + 1 = 98**, matching the observed shortfall exactly; no site was adjusted after the sum was known, and this paragraph is the whole enumeration rather than a summary of one kept elsewhere.

### C5 — `rewrite-prose`: skills, agents and records in the shape the models read best

**Intent.** Reshape the instruction layer per G10 without changing what any flow decides or asks: one SKILL.md per skill, host references one level deep, agents from one source, the eleven contradictions and dead references closed, the standing-rule text cut to what a gate does not already enforce, README and blueprint rewritten as maps.

**Decisions.**
- C5-D1: `plugin/skills/<skill>/SKILL.md` carries the flow (today's `_shared/bodies/<skill>.md`, rewritten: ≤ 300 lines, no sentence over 60 words, no exception-of-an-exception — a rule is stated once with its one escape hatch); `plugin/skills/<skill>/references/<host>.md` carries only what that host spells differently; `_shared/rubric.md`, `reporting.md`, `front-surface.md`, `didactic.md` stay shared and are cut to their operative content. Codex and OpenCode wrappers are rendered by `npm run build` from a six-field stub.
  - **C5-D1 amended 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 52), on the sentence it left out: WHICH HOST READS WHICH PATH, decided by the installer's payload shape and not by the prose.** Measured at `47183ca` rather than assumed: Claude receives `plugin/` whole, as a marketplace plugin; Codex receives `codex/skills/<name>/` plus a copy of `_shared`; OpenCode receives `opencode/skills/oso-<name>/` plus a copy of `_shared`, each wrapper directory copied RECURSIVELY (`core/src/install/opencode-install.ts`, `cpSync` with `recursive: true`), so a `references/` subdirectory beside a wrapper does ship. **Therefore `plugin/skills/<skill>/references/<host>.md` — the path this decision names — reaches CLAUDE ALONE.** The rule the decision now carries: **host-specific prose lives beside that host's own wrapper** — `plugin/skills/<skill>/references/claude.md` AUTHORED, `codex/skills/<name>/references/codex.md` and `opencode/skills/oso-<name>/references/opencode.md` RENDERED from sources under `core/src/prose/skills/<id>/` — and **host-neutral prose lives under `_shared/`, the one tree every host receives.** The asymmetry is stated once here rather than left to be found: Claude's binding is authored and the other two are generated, which is what puts them under `npm run check` so they cannot rot by hand. **The rejected alternative is recorded because it would have passed the exit line while delivering nothing:** a per-host tree under `_shared/references/<host>/<skill>.md` is `_shared/platform/<host>/<skill>.md` with a different word — same shape, same hand maintenance, same invisibility to `npm run check` — and C5's "zero platform files" would have been satisfied by a rename.
  - **The same fact decides THE FLOW, written here so S2 does not rediscover it (Decision 52).** G10 has one `SKILL.md` per skill per host wrapper carrying the flow, and this decision moves the flow into `plugin/skills/<skill>/SKILL.md` — which Codex and OpenCode never receive. So the flow reaches them the only way the payload allows: **RENDERED into their own `SKILL.md`** — host front matter from the stub, the host preface from `core/src/prose/skills/<id>/<host>.md`, then the flow body VERBATIM from `plugin/skills/<skill>/SKILL.md`, the one authored source. **The slice that rewrites a skill's flow — S2 for plan and roadmap, S3 for quick and debug, S4 for the judges — renders it into all three wrappers in the SAME commit and deletes that skill's `_shared/bodies/<skill>.md` then**, under G14's CONTRACT shape with one completeness grep per body; `_shared/bodies/` is gone when the last flow moves. G10's ≤ 300 lines is measured on the RENDERED `SKILL.md`, so a flow leaves room for its host preface. **And the stub is FOUR fields, not six:** `routes.ts`'s `SkillStub` carries `id`, `description` per host, `argumentHint` per host and `disableModelInvocation`, so this decision's "six-field stub" reads as "the stub `routes.ts` defines". **One correction to a premise this amendment was argued from:** C5-S1a's skill sources are the wrapper PREFACES — the host precondition and the two READ pointers — not the bindings, so the binding sources are NEW files beside them and the renderer gains one output kind rather than reusing an existing one.
  - **The shared layer's host binding, 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 53) — a RESTATEMENT of the rule above rather than an exception to it.** The per-skill rule leaves three files per host without a home, because they bind no wrapper: measured at `96dc1bc`, Claude carries `reporting` 15 lines, `front-surface` 14 and `delegation-wait` 13; Codex carries `front-surface`, `reporting` and `subagents`; OpenCode carries `front-surface` and `reporting`. **And `delegation-wait.md` is not a leaf — FIVE files read it** (`platform/claude/`'s `plan.md`, `debug.md`, `quick.md`, `front-surface.md` and `reporting.md`), each saying "READ `delegation-wait.md` beside this file NOW. It is the single Claude Code binding for how a launch is waited on": one rule, stated once, read by five. Folding it into per-skill bindings would restate it in five files, in the slice built to stop exactly that. **So the rule generalises rather than gaining a carve-out: a host binding lives BESIDE THE THING IT BINDS** — beside a skill's wrapper for a skill, beside `_shared/` for the shared layer. The shared layer's binding is **`plugin/skills/_shared/references/<host>.md`, ONE file per host, present only where the host differs** — `claude.md` authored, `codex.md` and `opencode.md` rendered, so the two generated ones sit under `npm run check` like every other rendered artefact. **What keeps this out of the shape Decision 52 rejected, said plainly because the two look alike from a distance:** that shape was a per-host tree of per-SKILL files, hand-maintained and invisible to the build; this is ONE file per host for the whole shared layer, and two of the three are generated. **One sentence for the reader who finds it:** `_shared/` ships every host a `references/` directory whose files are host-specific, which is harmless because a host's wrappers point only at that host's own file — and it is stated here so nobody reads it as `platform/` returning under another name.
- C5-D2: the closed defects: `plan.md:19` uses `oso-state deny-pattern add`; `slice B14`, `trap 5`, the ADR-0097 cap, "your Codex role's payload" in host-neutral bodies, the AUTO-marker "alone vs same write" contradiction, `/oso-code:plan` vs `oso-code:plan` spellings, `reporting.md:28` missing OpenCode, the default-branch discovery contradiction between debt-sweep and security-pass, `didactic.md` scope vs debug, and the 3-question vs 1-question round for quick — each fixed in one place and covered by a structural test where a test can read it (spelling and reference checks), by the verifier's read otherwise (`Verify-exception` stated).
  - **One of C5-D2's eleven closes EARLY, at C5-S1b-3 rather than at S4, 2026-09-02 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 56 Q2).** The defect listed above as "`reporting.md:28` missing OpenCode" is closed by C5-S1b-3's fix round, and the reason is that the round had no correct two-host spelling available to it. S1b's deletions left `plugin/skills/_shared/reporting.md:28` naming `platform/claude/reporting.md` and `platform/codex/reporting.md`, two files that no longer exist, so G14's completeness bar makes that sentence S1b-3's to repair — and a repair naming two hosts while the third host's binding already exists would be a sentence written false on purpose. **The binding exists on all three, verified by heading rather than assumed:** `plugin/skills/_shared/references/claude.md` carries "The native card is not the report", `codex.md` carries "No card exists here", `opencode.md` carries "Native agent files, no card". **No declared-drop row is earned, and the reason is Decision 54's own row unit:** a corrected path is not a retired rule, and the unit is a rule rather than a sentence. S4 still cuts `reporting.md` to its operative content and reads the sentence then.
- C5-D3: the unattended-run material (AUTO, park, ceiling, wait) becomes `_shared/unattended.md`, read by plan and roadmap; the parallel wave loop becomes `references/parallel.md`, read only when PARALLEL was chosen.
  - **C5-D3 amended 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 52).** `references/parallel.md` is HOST-NEUTRAL, and under the per-skill `references/` the amendment above establishes it would reach Claude alone. It becomes **`_shared/parallel.md`**, beside `_shared/unattended.md`, read only when PARALLEL was chosen — the same placement rule as the four shared files, for the same reason: `_shared/` is the one tree every host receives.
- C5-D4: the orchestrator preamble bound (G4, ≤ 6,000 words for `/plan` on Claude) is asserted by a structural test that sums the words of the files a wrapper binds.
  - **C5-D4's bound MEASURED for the first time, its closure ruled, and the bound HELD rather than moved, 2026-09-02 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 59).** C5-S2a's cut prompted the first actual measurement of this bar, and it produced a figure that looked fatal: **the closure without `plugin/skills/_shared/bodies/plan.md` is 6,757 words against a bound of 6,000** — over before the flow body contributes a word. **THE CLOSURE IS READ-ALWAYS ONLY, which Decision 44 Q3 (ii) already said and this applies rather than invents:** read-on-condition files are excluded from the sum and LISTED with their counts and their condition, the verifier reading each conditional as a real branch and never as a hiding place; and **a path handed to a subagent that reads it in its own context is THAT SUBAGENT'S preamble, not the orchestrator's.** Measured against the bindings rather than assumed: `plugin/skills/_shared/front-surface.md` is conditional at `plugin/skills/_shared/bodies/plan.md:74`, `:132`, `:200` and `:284`, every one of the form "when the change touches a front surface"; `plugin/skills/_shared/didactic.md` is the register's definition, read when that register is chosen (`plugin/skills/_shared/bodies/plan.md:55`); `plugin/skills/_shared/rubric.md` is a subagent-handed path at `:199`, `:204`, `:223`, `:237` and `:277` and is read by the orchestrator itself ONCE, for four named rules, at `plugin/skills/_shared/bodies/plan.md:77`; `plugin/skills/_shared/reporting.md` is genuinely read-always. **The rubric is the one judgement call and it is S2c's to make by design rather than the delegate's by fiat:** if the rewritten wrapper still has the orchestrator read the rubric itself the rubric counts, and if those four rules are inlined so the rubric becomes a subagent-handed path only, it does not — either way the payload states which and the test measures what the wrapper says. **G4's "Today" column is RESTATED on the same instrument so the target and the measure share one closure:** today's read-always set is **18,717 words without the rubric and 19,984 with it**, so the table's "~20,000" was, in effect, this closure with the rubric in it. **THE BOUND IS NOT RE-DERIVED ON A PROJECTION AND NO CUT IS ORDERED FOR THE NUMBER'S SAKE.** S2c cuts honestly toward 6,000 on the ruled closure and MEASURES; the round IS the measurement rather than a waste of one; and if the honest cut lands over, the path Decision 44 Q3 already built runs — BLOCKED with the per-file breakdown, one decision request, and a ruling between an amendment whose arithmetic is the measured honest closure by G4's method and a second cut round. **THE ORCHESTRATOR'S OWN PROJECTION WAS WRONG IN ITS PREMISE AND THAT IS RECORDED RATHER THAN QUIETLY DROPPED:** its arithmetic left the body 2,272 words, which is true only because it held the other read-always members at their UNCUT sizes — `plugin/skills/plan/references/claude.md` at 1,233 and `plugin/skills/_shared/references/claude.md` at 1,397 were relocated WHOLE by C5-S1b-1, are S2c's to cut to what the host spells differently, and had never been cut at all. **A target the operator set is replaced by a measurement, never by a delegate or an orchestrator reading a projection over files nobody has cut.** **S2c's SCOPE WIDENS so the closure is cut as ONE act:** `bodies/plan.md` rewritten, AND `plugin/skills/plan/references/claude.md` cut, AND `plugin/skills/_shared/references/claude.md` cut — that last unassigned until now, plan being its heaviest reader, with the capability inventory and the prose rows guarding the other skills' bindings. The preamble test lands with S2c, red on the pre-S2c tree at the restated today figure.
- C5-D5 (DELEGATED 2026-09-01 — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 45), recorded before C5-S0 arms, on the operator's own constraint that this chapter must not lose capability and must PROVE that it did not: the rewrite is instrumented by a CAPABILITY INVENTORY extracted from every `*.md` under `plugin/skills/` and `plugin/agents/` at `1c85baa` — one glob, no judgment about which files matter — committed as JSON under `core/test/fixtures/prose/` with its anchor commit and its extraction rules recorded verbatim, so a verifier REGENERATES it and diffs rather than trusting it; a fixture that cannot be regenerated is a claim. Four axes, graded by what each proves. **(A) `oso-state` command spellings**, which must survive or be declared AND must resolve to a verb the state CLI's own switch carries, read from that source rather than from a copy — this is what turns C5-D2's `deny-pattern add` defect red until its row names the correct spelling as its home, `deny-pattern` existing nowhere in `core/src` today. **(B) invocations** — the agent role names and every `oso-code:<skill>` — survive or declared. **(C) verdict tokens** in the backticked `Name: token` form, surviving VERBATIM or declared, which is §6 item 1 made checkable and is not the verdict-grammar test's job, that one covering the implementation and never the prose vocabulary. **(D) section headings, which are NOT a survival axis** — the rewrite reshapes by design and forcing old headings to survive buys paperwork — but a MAP: every pre-rewrite (file, heading) pair carries exactly one destination row, kept, merged into, or dropped with a reason and a home, and every destination must resolve mechanically. **Every axis asserts a FLOOR equal to the fixture's count, and the fixture's own counts are asserted against tree-derived minimums shown failing on an empty walk**, so no broken extraction can pass by inventorying nothing — the `provedSomething` shape, applied to the instrument that guards against loss. A declared drop lands in one table beside the fixture — axis, item, source file, reason, and a home that is a gate named by an id existing in `core/src`, a test file that exists, or a prose file that now carries it — and an unresolving home is red. Each slice adds only its own rows and lists them in its report, a verifier reads only those rows, and the C5 sweep re-reads the whole table and the whole map once. **What this does NOT prove is MEANING**: a surviving name whose sense changed is the verifier's read under a stated `Verify-exception`, C5-D2's own mechanism, bounded to the rows that slice touched. **The instrument and C5-D4's preamble bar pull opposite ways BY DESIGN** — a section dropped to reach 6,000 words must be declared with a home that resolves — and that tension is where this chapter should expect BLOCKED reports rather than quiet greens. **The instrument lives through C5 and C6**, C6's certification being the last consumer of the proof; whether it survives the tag, freezing `1c85baa`'s names against every later rename, is a post-rewrite decision for the operator and is recorded as a residual rather than decided in C5.
  - **C5-D5 amended 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decisions 48 and its addendum), on three findings its own first implementation produced.** **(i) Axis D resolves by MULTISET, not by set.** The rule above said KEPT is the same heading text present in any file, and that is a set-membership test over a corpus that is a multiset: at the anchor there are **200 heading occurrences across 96 distinct texts, and 27 of those texts recur in two or more files** — `Shared-file paths` eighteen times, `Reporting binding` twelve, `The state command` nine. Seventeen of the eighteen `Shared-file paths` sections could have been deleted and all eighteen reported KEPT. So: for each distinct heading text, KEPT absorbs `min(anchor count, current count)` and the shortfall demands that many rows, each keyed to one of that heading's own anchor origins, with phantom-origin and duplicate-origin rows failing INDEPENDENTLY of whether the count already balances. Moving a heading to a file that never carried it still demands NO row, which is the case that proves the fix did not quietly become the same-file rule this decision rejected. **(ii) The source set is SIX prefixes, not two** — `plugin/skills/**/*.md`, `plugin/agents/*.md`, `codex/skills/**/SKILL.md`, `codex/agents/*.toml`, `opencode/skills/**/SKILL.md`, `opencode/agents/*.md` — because the original glob left outside the instrument the fourteen agent files and eighteen wrappers that Codex and OpenCode actually ship and read, which is a first-priority blind spot in a decision written to protect capability. 54 / 3 / 9 / 7 / 9 / 7 = **89 files** at the anchor, per-prefix counts asserted rather than only the total. `core/src/agents/` is deliberately OUT: no host reads it, and keeping it out is what lets C5-S1a render those files byte-identically without moving a single count. **(iii) The fixture's independence is a REVIEW-TIME guarantee and is established by nothing else.** The fixture carries, per axis, a number AND the exact command that produced it; the test asserts the extractor's counts equal those recorded numbers and spawns no POSIX tool of its own, because the suite runs on windows-latest where a shell pipeline is a host-dependent instrument. **So every payload that touches this fixture re-executes every recorded command and compares its last line to the recorded number** — the preconditions being bash (Git Bash on Windows), the repository root, and an anchor reachable in that checkout's history, a shallow clone's `fatal: Not a valid object name` and exit 128 being an unreachable anchor and never a broken witness. **The clause is written here rather than carried in payloads because a criterion that lives only in a payload is remembered prose, which is the §9.1 shape, and the next chapter's reader reads this document.** **What made the clause necessary, on its first use:** one recorded command chained two loops with `;` and a single trailing `| wc -l`, so the pipe bound to the second loop alone and the command printed `2` where `8` was recorded. The count was right and the extractor was right; what had never been established was the independence the witness exists to provide, since the recorded number and the extractor's were two numbers the same author typed. It is G4's fourth standing clause on its own instrument.
  - **C5-D5 gains a FOURTH AXIS, `prose`, 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 54), because the first slice that could lose something proved the table could not hold what it lost.** The three axes are `oso-state` spellings, invocations and verdict tokens; ordinary body prose is none of them, so when C5-S1b-1 retired a clause its neutral body already stated word for word, there was nowhere to write it down. **The row unit is a RULE — an instruction, condition or fact the model would act on — and NEVER a sentence.** Rewording that keeps the rule needs no row, which is the clause that stops G10's cut to sixty-word sentences from generating a row per paragraph across 30,750 words. A row is required whenever a rule no longer appears in the file that stated it, and carries `axis`, `item`, `sourceFile`, `retired` (the retired clause verbatim, as the record), `reason` and `home`. **`item` is the rule AS IT SURVIVES in its home, and where the home is a prose file the test asserts that file CONTAINS it verbatim** — which turns "it was redundant" from a claim a reader accepts into an assertion something checks. Homes are a prose file that contains the item, a gate whose id exists under `core/src/gates` for an absolute a gate now enforces, a test file that exists for a rule a test pins, or **`none`**. **`none` is a GENUINE DROP**, allowed only with a reason saying why the thing is not a capability, and **COUNTED SEPARATELY: each slice reports its `none` count and C5's close reports the total, and THAT NUMBER IS THE CHAPTER'S TRUE CAPABILITY-LOSS FIGURE** — the one the operator's constraint asks for. C5-S1b-2 built the axis, backfilled C5-S1b-1's retirement as its first row, and reported a `none` count of **zero**. **A property worth stating so nobody misreads the table:** the rule is `rows.length >= required`, a floor and not an equality, so a heading whose text coincides with a new file's can carry more rows than its shortfall demands — three rows there are not three losses.
  - **A figure of Decision 54's corrected, with its mechanism, 2026-09-01.** That ruling put C5-S1b-1's references at 314 lines. The eight per-skill files plus the shared one sum to **274** — 85 + 44 + 38 + 39 + 9 + 9 + 5 + 5 + 40 — and 274 plus `tracked-files.test.ts`'s 50 is the 324 untracked the slice reported. The 314 came from a `wc` invocation that listed `_shared/references/claude.md` explicitly AND matched it again through a `*/references/claude.md` glob, counting its 40 twice. **The observation the figure supported is unaffected and independently verified: C5-S1b-1 relocated the overlays WHOLE, every reference matching its overlay's line count to the line.**
  - **C5-D5's exclusion corrected 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 50 Q1).** The amendment above excludes `core/src/agents/` from the inventory's source set. The sources live at `core/src/prose/`, so that is the prefix excluded, and the reason is unchanged and still exactly right: **no host reads it.** Keeping the render sources outside the six prefixes is what lets a slice move bytes between source and output without the inventory demanding a declared drop for work that changed nothing a host can see — which is precisely what C5-S1a's oracle proved and what C5-S1c will rely on in the other direction.

**Proposed slices.** S1 shared references + agents from one source (rendered) + wrappers rendered; S2 plan + roadmap + unattended + parallel; S3 quick + debug; S4 judges (debt-sweep, quality-pass, security-pass, doubt-pass, triage) + rubric/reporting/front-surface cuts; S5 README, blueprint, CHANGELOG, the twelve structural checks ported to `core/test/structure/` and `tests/plugin-lint.sh` deleted. Parallel S2/S3/S4 after S1.
- **Slices re-cut 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 45), superseding the line above.** The order is **S0 capability inventory → S1 → S2 / S3 / S4 → S5**, S0 sequential and first. S0 is a slice of its own rather than a part of S1 because S1 is already the relocation slice — 32 platform files deleted and every `references/<host>.md` written — and an applier that builds the instrument judging its own relocation in the same round is the shape C4 learned to refuse; and it lands before S1 rather than before S2 because the overlays S1 cuts carry capability too, **18,580 words of host bindings across 32 files** (claude 11 files / 4,716 words, codex 11 / 5,977, opencode 10 / 7,887), and a host losing a binding is the first-priority loss. S5 ports ELEVEN structural checks, not twelve, per G10's amendment above.
- **Slices re-cut again 2026-09-01 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable ce7154, model Claude Fable 5.1, Decision 47), superseding the bullet above.** The order is **S0 capability inventory → S0b the two G6 verbs → S1a render pipeline → S1b prose relocation → S2 / S3 / S4 → S5**. EIGHT slices. **S0b** is the slice G6's unmet contract earns, beside G6 above. **S1a is EXPAND under G14 and deletes nothing:** it creates `core/scripts/build-prose.mjs` with a `--check` leg wired into BOTH `npm run build` and `npm run check` — there is no prose renderer today, `core/scripts` holding only the three bundle builds, so G2's discipline that a committed artefact must equal a fresh build is EXTENDED to rendered prose here or the wrappers rot exactly as a stale bundle would; it authors the seven agent roles once under `core/src/agents/*.md` with a per-role HOST LIST in the source, because the roster is not uniform — three roles reach Claude and all seven reach Codex and OpenCode, the four extra being debt-sweep, doubt-pass, security-reviewer and triage, which are SKILLS on Claude and AGENTS on the other two; and it renders the Codex and OpenCode skill wrappers from C5-D1's six-field stub. **The oracle is what makes S1a first:** after `npm run build`, `git diff --exit-code` over `plugin/agents`, `codex/agents`, `opencode/agents`, `codex/skills` and `opencode/skills` must be empty — a renderer is correct if and only if its output equals bytes already in the tree and already certified, and that proof has to land before the renderer is trusted with prose that has no oracle. Any deviation must be whitespace or field order alone, enumerated in the slice report; a content deviation is a FAIL. S1a touches no file under `plugin/skills/`. **S1b is the relocation** — the 32 overlays deleted, every `references/<host>.md` written, the wrappers re-rendered from the new stub content, plugin-lint taken to eleven, `docs/parity-opencode.md`'s section removed, and axis D's map rows added for every overlay heading it retires. **If S1b projects past 450 authored lines it PRE-SPLITS BY HOST at a named seam, never by skill**, because a host's references set is what that host's wrappers bind and every commit must leave every host whole. **Instrument amendment, extended by name as Decision 41 part 1 requires:** from S1a's landing, `build-prose.mjs`'s outputs — `plugin/agents/*.md`, `codex/agents/*.toml`, `opencode/agents/*.md`, `codex/skills/*/SKILL.md`, `opencode/skills/oso-*/SKILL.md` — join the size instrument's GENERATED SET beside `bootstrap/oso.js`; authored lines are the sources and the renderer, and whole-file deletions stay under Decision 43's deletion branch. The reserve range recorded beneath G4 gains **+ S0b ~150** and **+ S1a ~250**.

The seam is instead: **S1c-1 `oso-applier` plus the four judge roles (≈ 312), S1c-2 `oso-verifier` (≈ 229), S1c-3 `oso-integrator` (≈ 226)**, the judges going with the applier because 312 is the lowest pairing total against 333 and 330, which MAXIMISES the worst margin across the three. **The worst margin is 138 lines, which absorbs this chapter's largest estimate error — 121, at C5-S0 — with room; a balanced two-slice cut whose larger half lands at 434 of 450 does not, and a seam whose safety depends on an unmeasured collapsed-size estimate being right to within 4% is not a seam.** Projection method, stated so a later reader can check it rather than trust it: a collapsed body ≈ 50 lines per universal role, a host delta ≈ 15, a judge body ≈ 8 with deltas ≈ 2. **S1c costs `core/test` about nothing** — the sources are `*.md` under `core/src/prose/` and G4's instrument counts tracked `*.ts` alone — so only a test a payload names lands on the ceiling. **The queue is TEN slices: S0 → S0b → S1a → S1b → S1c-1 → S1c-2 → S1c-3 → S2 / S3 / S4 → S5.**
- **A NAME THAT DIFFERS BY HOST IS A HOST FACT, NOT A PARAPHRASE — the lesson C5-S1c-1 paid a fix round for, 2026-09-02.** The collapse's whole method is to tell a shared body from a host delta, and its first application got the distinction wrong in the one place it is subtle: a skill's own NAME. Codex installs `debt-sweep`, OpenCode installs `oso-debt-sweep`, each declared in its own `SKILL.md` front matter — and the round read that difference as two spellings of one thing and put **Codex's into the shared body**, so all four judge roles told OpenCode to expect a skill that does not exist there. **Severe rather than cosmetic, and the reason is the sentence's own next clause:** it continues "if either field is absent, report blocked instead of locating or inferring it", so a model on OpenCode handed a real path ending in the segment `oso-debt-sweep` and told to expect one ending in `debt-sweep` can judge the field absent and refuse — on every OpenCode invocation of those four roles. **The pre-collapse files were CORRECT**, so this was a regression the collapse introduced rather than debt it inherited. **What caught it is worth keeping:** the round's own report defended the wording as "still literally true for both" on a suffix reading, and the verifier was asked to judge it as an instruction a model must ACT on rather than as a string-matching puzzle. Those two readings disagree, and only one of them is what a host does. **The rule this leaves:** a shared body carries no host-varying literal at all — not a skill name, a wrapper directory, a path segment, a tool name or an agent id — and the check is a sweep of every body rather than of the sites a finding named. **And the trap inside the trap, recorded because a mechanical fix gets it wrong:** the ROLE is `oso-security-reviewer` while the OpenCode SKILL is `oso-security-pass`, so prefixing the role name with `oso-` produces a path that does not exist. S1c-2 and S1c-3 inherit this rule rather than rediscovering it. S1c-2 inherited it and found a SECOND SHAPE of the same class, which is why the rule is stated as "no host-varying literal" rather than "no name that differs": `oso-verifier`'s Claude body used `/plan` and `/debug` as literal shorthand for the flows, and on Codex `/plan` is that host's OWN native Plan-Mode toggle — an unrelated concept wearing the same string. **That is a COLLISION rather than a variance**, and it is worse, because a variance merely fails to resolve while a collision resolves to the wrong thing. Codex's and OpenCode's bodies had already sidestepped it independently with "a plan slice" and "the debug flow", and the collapse kept that phrasing instead of promoting Claude's literal. So the sweep every later slice owes its own bodies is for BOTH shapes: a literal that differs between hosts, and a literal that means something else on one of them.

### C6 — `rewrite-release`: 0.26.0 prepared, installed here, certified

**Intent.** Bump the version, write the CHANGELOG entry, regenerate hashes and bundles, install the tree on the operator's machine with the new installer for the three hosts, run the certification, and stop at the tag: the release itself is on the never-solo list.

**Slices.** S1 version bump + CHANGELOG + `npm run build` + `npm run check`; S2 local install of all three hosts via the new CLI, `oso verify` green on each; S3 `OSO_CERTIFY=1` run green. Exit: the roadmap parks with the tag and the marketplace publish as the operator's two pendings.

## 4. Autonomy policy for this roadmap

The roadmap mode's three tiers, its irreversibility bar and its never-solo list apply unchanged. Specific to this roadmap:

- Deleting a bash file, a test, a lint rule or a workflow is REVERSIBLE (git) and is decided here, so it never reaches the bar; the CONTRACT slices carry those deletions under G14.
- Choosing a library version, a bundle flag, a test file name or a fixture layout is the flow's own recommendation tier; no such choice reaches the operator.
- Any finding that a documented host contract differs from what the pinned binary does is a RECONCILIATION: record what was measured, emit what works, and queue the divergence for the presence phase with the evidence. It never blocks a child.
- A verifier that fails a slice on PROSE it was not asked to judge is outside its remit (G12): the orchestrator routes the finding to the sweep and does not re-run the applier for it.
- The two pendings of C6 (tag, publish) are the operator's.
- **The set-aside cascade, stated rather than hidden.** The children are sequential dependencies (C1 needs C0's gate, C2 needs C1's state library, and so on). The roadmap's own rule arms the next child after a set-aside; here that next child's first bar goes BLOCKED, which is one of the three moments the chain stops at and hands itself back. So a set-aside in C0–C5 ends the run at that point, and the answer is completeness in this document, never a question mid-chain. The children's decision rounds must find every core lens answered here: Contracts (G5, G6, G7), Architecture (G1, G2, G10), Errors (G5's error paths, G6's `StateInvariantError`), Verification (G4, G8, G9, each child's bar), Reuse (G1's Node APIs, G3's single runtime dependency); derived — rollback/release (G14, C6), observability (G7, C4-D1), record integrity (G11), security (trust hashes in G2 and C3-D1).

## 5. Host compatibility after the rewrite

| | Claude Code | Codex CLI | OpenCode |
|---|---|---|---|
| Delivery | marketplace plugin, root `plugin/` | `~/.codex/` managed region + hooks dir + `~/.codex/skills` | `~/.config/opencode/plugin/oso-code.js`, `opencode.json` keys, `skill/` |
| Gate transport | `node` exec-form hooks, bundle under `plugin/dist` | `node` shell-form hooks, bundle copied by the installer, hashed | in-process import of the core |
| State | `node plugin/dist/oso-state.js` | same bundle | library call |
| Windows | node.exe; no Git Bash for the harness (the client's own Bash tool still needs it) | as documented by the host; installer via `node` | verified by nightly from C3 |
| Skills | SKILL.md + `references/claude.md` | rendered wrapper + `references/codex.md` | rendered wrapper + `references/opencode.md` |
| Agents | rendered `plugin/agents/*.md` | rendered `codex/agents/*.toml` | rendered `opencode/agents/*.md` |
| Certification | nightly `claude plugin validate`, real install | nightly real install + hooks smoke on the pinned binary | nightly contract + behaviour bars on the pinned binary |

## 6. What stays exactly as it is (so nobody "improves" it)

1. The flows' phases, gates, verdict vocabularies and report shapes.
2. The state keys, their values, the file path and its `key=value` format; `oso-state`'s verbs and texts.
3. What each gate denies and the message it prints (the parity fixtures are the proof).
4. `events.jsonl` and the run journal formats.
5. The engram topic keys and the index standard.
6. The three hosts and the gate-per-host table (only the transport column changes).
7. `docs/decisions/` as history.

## 7. The parity harness (the safety net of C1–C3)

A fixture is a JSON file: `{ "name", "env": {…}, "home": { "<relative path>": "<content>" }, "cwd": "<fixture repo or none>", "argv": […], "stdin": "…", "expect": { "exit": n, "stdout": "…", "stderr": "…", "home_after": { … }, "events": [ … ] } }`. `core/test/parity.test.ts` materialises `home` into a temp directory, runs the subject with `HOME` and `XDG_*` pinned there, and compares. While bash and TS coexist the subject is BOTH and both must pass; after the CONTRACT slice the subject is the bundle alone and the fixtures stay as regression tests. Fixtures are written from the suite's assertions by reading them (C1-D2); a fixture whose expectation was recorded from a run is rejected in review as tautological, per the verifier's own quality gate.

## 8. Risks and their treatment

| Risk | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Claude `if` silently skipping a DENY gate | it matches raw `argv.join(' ')` by literal prefix with no basename normalisation and no wrapper stripping — measured on 2.1.250 via the client's own `Skipping hook due to if condition` log line; a passing `claude plugin validate` proves nothing about a field's semantics, only the typed-error probe proves it is read. G5's standing rule (amended 2026-08-28) forbids `if` on every deny-or-block gate |
| Codex hook exec form or `if` unsupported | Codex stays on shell form with a plain `node` command (G5); measured in C2-S4's smoke on the pinned Codex |
| The pinned Claude client honours a different Stop field | C2-D7: measured, both fields emitted if needed, recorded as delegated |
| TypeScript 7 compiler rejects something | G3 fallback to latest 5.x, recorded |
| Node version drift on operator machines | `engines.node >= 22` in every package.json; the wrappers refuse older nodes with the install command |
| A parity fixture encodes a bash bug as expected behaviour | the fixture's expectation is read from the suite's assertion text, and a verifier flags any fixture whose expected value cannot be traced to an assertion |
| Windows exec form path quoting | `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` inside `args`, never a shell string; nightly runs the real hooks.json on windows-latest |
| Scope creep into flow semantics | §6 list; the debt-sweep's conformance axis judges against this document's decisions |
| The live run `opencode-plan-execution` (branch `oso-run/opencode-runtime-parity`, slice 3 of 11) | precondition in §9 |

## 9. Preconditions before C0 is armed

The run `opencode-plan-execution` is PARKED (`auto=parked`, state `mode=plan active_slice=none verify_green=false`) with slice 3 pending in the tree across ten paths, unverified on prose only: the code criteria passed, the verifier failed the same prose-claim class twice, and the fix-round cap of two (G12) says stop. It is not finished and not resumed. What lands from it, and how:

1. **Commit slice 3's tree as it stands, from the operator's own terminal** (the git pre-commit hook allows a commit with no session id; the harness's commit rail is per session): `git add -A && git commit -m "chore(opencode): park slice 3 — /oso-plan routes to build, prose claims unverified"`. Nothing is edited first; the commit message is the record that the prose is unverified.
2. **Merge PR #2 to `main` as it is** — `bea25f6` (the OpenCode host), `1f916cd` (the pin, measured), `2adebe0` (the host fact, measured) and the parked commit. The rewrite needs all four on `main`: C2-D6 keeps the OpenCode plugin's host-specific parts, C4 ports the permanent behaviour test and reads the pin, C5 rewrites every prose file the parked slice touched. CI is red on that PR for the same machine-dependent reasons C0 removes from the gate; the merge is the operator's.
3. **Drop the parked state**: `oso-state --session d67e0dc2-2c93-4775-abb8-9cd7106824cc clear` (the command the SessionStart notice offers), so C0 arms on a clean state. The run journal and the engram topics stay as the record.
4. `git switch main && git pull && git status --porcelain` is empty; `opencode --version` is whatever it is — C0-S2 makes the suite stop caring.
5. The operator runs `/oso-code:roadmap ts-core-rewrite` and points §1 at this file; the queue, the global ledger and each child's decisions are read from here; G15 and §11 are the inheritance.
6. Model policy G13 is stated in the arming instruction.

### 9.1 Ambient conditions of the run — the installed 0.25.0 rails govern it, with their known defects

The chain runs under the INSTALLED plugin (0.25.0, bash), not under the tree it rewrites, until C6. The orchestrator respects these at every child and every slice:

- `oso-state journal --path` does not exist in 0.25.0 (it appends `--path` as text). The journal is `~/.local/state/oso-code/runs/<repo digest>/<child slug>.log`; append with `oso-state journal <text>`, resolve the path by hand.
- Defect 1 of the installed stop net: `auto_wait` is never returned to `none` by the close commands. At EVERY slice close and child close the orchestrator writes `auto_wait=none` in the same `oso-state set` as the close, and removes the sidecar `~/.local/state/oso-code/runs/<repo digest>/<child slug>.waiting`. On a resume, a sidecar naming a dead session is removed first.
- The 0.25.0 sidecar compares only label and session: a second delegation under one slice label inherits the first one's clock. Re-arm `auto_wait=<label>` per delegation and clear it when its report is read.
- A delegation can die on an API timeout AFTER writing to disk (it happened twice on 2026-08-25). On any dead delegation, audit the tree by mtime against the launch window before relaunching; never assume a clean start or a finished round.
- The commit rail reads state at call time: the green write and the commit are two separate Bash calls.
- Until C0-S2 lands, the installed `opencode` binary self-updates and turns every pin check red; a red on a pin check is ambient, not a slice's, and is not chased by editing the pin. `bootstrap/verify-codex.sh` is ambient red for the same class.
- Model policy G13. Every launch names the model in its announcing sentence.

### 9.2 The arming instruction (what the operator pastes)

`/oso-code:roadmap ts-core-rewrite` — with this text: "Everything is decided in `docs/rewrite/ts-core-roadmap.md`. §1 reads the queue, the global ledger G1–G16 and each child's decisions from that file and asks nothing the file answers; §2 takes the policy in §4 of the file; present the one approval document. Every child is unattended (AUTO). Respect §9.1 of the file at every slice. Landing per G16. Models per G13."

## 10. Glossary of the numbers a verifier reads

- "Green" for `npm test`: exit 0, ≥ 1 test executed, zero skipped tests without an `OSO_NIGHTLY`/`OSO_CERTIFY` guard.
  - **Amended 2026-08-31 (DELEGATED — operator's delegate, Fable d2c142, model Claude Fable 5, C4/pre-S1, Decision 40 part 4), recorded before C4-S1a arms.** Green for `npm test` on the PR gate: exit 0, ≥ 1 test executed, zero skipped tests without an `OSO_NIGHTLY`/`OSO_CERTIFY` guard. Inside the certify lane (`core/test/certify/`, `OSO_CERTIFY=1`) a test may skip at RUNTIME only through the lane's not-run recorder, whose reason string names what could not be driven; a bare `t.skip`/`skip:` anywhere under `core/test/certify` outside the recorder is red in both lanes, enforced by a structure test. A not-run is never counted as a pass; the nightly summary lists every not-run line per host and warns when a host drove zero rows. **Why the runtime skip needed an amendment at all:** a not-run cause the certify lane discovers — no free model in the host catalog, a headless session that will not start — is decided AFTER collection, so no guard could have predicted it, and the sentence above forbade exactly that shape. The two rejected spellings are recorded with their reasons: a PASSING case asserting the gate was undrivable makes an undriven gate indistinguishable from a driven one in the pass count, which is this roadmap's signature defect; and a third exit code on `VerifyReport` changes installer production code for a test-lane concern, where C4-D1's own words replace the exit code with a report line. `VerifyReport` keeps its own `skip()` for verb rows — two surfaces, one meaning: never pass, never fail, always a reason.
- "Cold start overhead": 20 interleaved pairs of `node plugin/dist/gate.js commit` and a bare-node control — an empty ESM module beside a copy of `plugin/dist/package.json` — each with a 300-byte envelope on stdin; the bar is `bundle mean − control mean ≤ max(40 ms, 1.5 × control mean)`, measured by `core/test/gates/cold-start.test.ts`, on both PR runners and locally. The absolute means are reported in the test title and asserted nowhere. Amended 2026-08-29 (Decision 14): the row previously read "Cold start ≤ 100 ms" and named `core/test/perf/coldstart.test.ts`, a path that has never existed.
- "Preamble ≤ 6,000 words": `wc -w` over the wrapper plus every file it binds for the `plan` skill on Claude, computed by `core/test/structure/preamble.test.ts`.
- "Lines": `wc -l` over tracked files under the named directory.
